# Decision log

Single register for open decisions and resolved architecture decisions. Agents must read this before implementing anything that depends on an open item, and must not invent a resolution. Record a resolution only when a named owner has confirmed it, with the date and the evidence source.

## Open decisions

> Prioritised register of everything outstanding, with owners, evidence and a definition of done for each: `docs/2026-09-04-outstanding-work.md`. It covers the open decisions below alongside the engineering and tooling items that are tasks rather than decisions, so neither set gets read in isolation.

| OD-018 | Confirm the business calendar source for the 10-working-day remediation threshold (holiday set, working week, timezone). | Advice Quality / Operations | PP-13 SLA ageing; blocks the remediation clock | Resolved | **2026-08-30: settled by project owner direction.** Working week is **Monday to Friday**. The clock runs in **UK local time (Europe/London)**, so the day boundary moves with BST — Dataverse returns UTC, and a timestamp late on a BST evening belongs to the next UK day. The day a case enters remediation is **day 1**, not day 0, so the count is inclusive of the start day. A **rejected sign-off (BR-008) resets the clock**, but the previous timer is preserved as part of the audit trail, so a case that goes round twice shows both periods rather than one merged age. **Bank holidays: England's set is nominated, but applied manually rather than encoded** — the direction was to implement working days alone. `app/src/lib/workingDays.ts` therefore counts every Monday to Friday, and the consequence is deliberate: a remediation spanning Christmas or Easter reads a day or more older than it truly is, so the ten-day threshold can flag a case that has not really breached, and a breach near a bank holiday wants a human look. Adding a maintained holiday list is the fix when someone owns one. **Implemented:** the calculation, the ten-working-day threshold and the working-day ageing bands on the remediation report, replacing the provisional calendar-day display. **Implemented 2026-09-03:** the reset-and-preserve behaviour, as `al_remediationaction.al_clockstartedon` stamped by `SignoffProgressPlugin` on a rejected sign-off, with `createdon` keeping the original start so both periods survive (AD-079). The ten-working-day threshold measures the current period; the Code App reads it through `remediationClock()` and the portal remediation list shows the previous period beside the current one. Prior note: Elapsed calendar days are explicitly not acceptable. Until confirmed, remediation age is displayed in calendar days and labelled as provisional. |

It is bound alongside `AL Portal - Adviser Remediation` on the Contact-scoped `Remediation Action - assigned to me` permission (`…06b`, AD-069) and on the Remediation page rule (`…0b4`, AD-068), so the two roles now share remediation routing rather than the routing distinguishing between them — a finer split, if wanted, is a later permission-model change, not a schema gap. 2026-08-30: Two separate portal web roles. Project owner direction. `powerpages/outcome-testing---outcometesting/webrole.yml` currently defines `AL Portal - Adviser Remediation` and no Planner role, so a Planner web role must be added and the remediation routing split between the two. Minting its id goes through the AD-059 band rules and `Check-ComponentIds.ps1`, because a clashing id silently destroys another component. Prior notes: Raised 2026-08-29 from the Power Pages requirements pack section 3.1. |

| OD-022 | Confirm the Power Pages case-access mechanic for `al_OutcomeCase`: N:N Contact relationship, or per-persona Contact lookups. | Delivery (empirical test in DEV) | Phase 2 security gate; blocks all portal case reads | Resolved | **2026-08-31: neither mechanic. All authenticated users see all cases; they can action only what is assigned to them.** Project owner direction. This extends AD-056's answer from the two reviewer roles to every authenticated portal user, so no case-level Contact mechanic is needed at all: read is Global scope (756150000), and the ability to act is carried by the review assignment, not by the case. **The site already implements this** — `Outcome Case - read all` is Global read bound to the Authenticated Users role, and write reaches responses only through the Contact-anchored `Review Instance - assigned to me`, whose child `Response - on a review assigned to me` carries create and write. Verified against DEV 2026-08-31; no permission change is required. **Consequence: the N:N `al_contact_al_outcomecase` created in DEV on 2026-08-30 to test the alternative is now unnecessary and should be deleted** so an unused intersect table does not outlive the question it was built to answer. The edit boundary AD-056 describes is unchanged and still structural: write is reachable only through the Contact-anchored parent chain. Prior notes: **2026-08-30, empirical step 1 done:** the N:N `al_contact_al_outcomecase` (contact <-> al_outcomecase) has been created in DEV and Dataverse reports it as an N:N on the table, so the relationship half of the test is settled — an N:N between contact and a custom table is creatable. What is still unanswered is whether Power Pages will bind it: this environment runs the Enhanced data model, so a table permission is a `powerpagecomponent` of type 18 carrying JSON, and the only Contact-scoped example in the site (`Review Instance - assigned to me`) names the N:1 `contact_al_reviewinstance` via `contactrelationship`. Creating a probe permission naming the N:N was not run. Note any such probe needs a control naming a relationship that does not exist: if the write path accepts both, acceptance proves nothing and only the running portal settles it. The decision's own stated method — check the Contact-access relationship picker in the maker portal — is now unblocked, because the relationship it needs exists. A case must be readable by the assigned checker, the adviser and the T&C Manager. A lookup holds one contact, and Power Pages child permissions traverse parent-to-child only, so the case cannot be inherited from the review. Custom access (FetchXML) is excluded while preview. Resolve by creating the N:N relationship in DEV and checking whether it appears in the Contact-access relationship picker. See AD-047. **Partially resolved 2026-08-29 (AD-056):** for the two reviewer roles the answer is neither mechanic — they get Global read on `al_OutcomeCase` and write scoped to their own reviews, so no case-level Contact mechanic is needed for them. Still open for the Adviser and T&C Manager roles, where case readability cannot be Global. **2026-09-08: the unused intersect is deleted.** `al_contact_al_outcomecase` held 0 rows and is gone from DEV and from `src/`, through `deleterelationship`, which refuses unless the relationship is custom, unmanaged and its intersect empty. This closes the consequence the 2026-08-31 resolution recorded and left open. |
| OD-023 | Confirm the production support owner and escalation route for the portal. | Platform owner | Go-live checklist | Closed | **2026-08-31: owning teams named as AQS and Tax.** That closes gap (a). Note what it does and does not cover: AQS and Tax are the review disciplines, so they are the people who *notice* a portal problem and who own it as a business service, but they do not hold Power Pages or Dataverse admin — the service account does (answered 2026-08-30). So the queue is owned by AQS and Tax, and anything needing a configuration or platform change still has to reach whoever operates that account. Whether that hand-off is a second line worth naming, or simply the same service desk queue routed onward, is still unstated. **Remaining gap: hours of cover and response targets**, split between portal-down and a single user blocked. Escalation is human-triggered by direction, so nothing fires on a timer, which makes the hours the only thing that tells a user when to expect a response. **2026-08-30, partially answered by project owner direction — still Open, two gaps.** Settled: production support is owned by **a team**, not an individual; **all escalations are human-triggered**, so nothing auto-escalates on a timer and the route is a decision someone makes; intake is the **service desk queue**; and in PROD the **service account holds Power Platform/Dataverse admin**, with the expectation that a signing-key rotation re-registers all required plug-in types (see OD-025 — that is 17 types today, because the AssemblyQualifiedName on every plug-in type embeds the public key token). Still needed to close this: **(a) the name of the owning team** — 'a team' is the shape of the answer, not the answer, and a go-live checklist needs something to route to; and **(b) hours of cover and response targets**, split between portal-down and a single user blocked, since 'human-triggered' escalation only works if someone is on the other end at a known time. Prior note: Raised 2026-08-29 from the Power Pages requirements pack section 26. **2026-09-03, further answered by project owner direction — still Open, narrowed to two specifics.** Settled: **cover is normal working hours only**, roughly 9am–5pm Monday to Friday, so a portal fault outside those hours waits until the next working morning; and **admin-level escalation goes to one named person** who holds the service account, rather than to a service-desk queue routed onward. Two things are needed before this can close, and one is a caution rather than a question. **(a) The person's name.** Gap (a) has changed shape but not closed — 'one named person' is still the shape of the answer, and a go-live checklist needs someone to route to. **(b) Response targets.** Hours say when someone is *there*; they do not say how quickly a user hears back, and the split still matters — portal-down against a single user blocked. Escalation is human-triggered, so nothing fires on a timer and the target is a promise a person keeps, not a mechanism. **Caution, recorded because it reverses an earlier direction:** 2026-08-30 settled that production support is owned by *a team, not an individual*, precisely to avoid a single point of failure; naming one person reintroduces it. With working-hours-only cover there is no second line, so that person's leave, illness or departure leaves the portal with no admin route at all. If that is accepted, name a deputy alongside them; if it is not, this reverts to the team answer. **2026-09-08: OUT OF SCOPE by project owner direction.** Hours of cover, response targets and the hand-off owner for changes needing Power Pages or Dataverse admin will not be stated. The consequence is accepted rather than resolved: nothing fires on a timer, so nothing tells a user when to expect a response. Recorded so it is not re-raised as a gap. |
| OD-024 | Confirm who is recorded as accountable for a File Quality or Advice Quality fail, and where that judgement is captured. | Advice Quality Manager | Eight of the twenty AD-039 Trail Light export columns (11-14, 17-20) | Resolved | 2026-08-30: accountability is recorded as **four flags on `al_Outcome`** — `al_fqadviseraccountable`, `al_fqparaplanneraccountable`, `al_aqadviseraccountable`, `al_aqparaplanneraccountable`. The export takes name and code from the case where a flag is set and leaves the pair blank where it is not, which expresses AD-039's "and/or" exactly without duplicating name or code data. The flags are set after the Outcome exists, through `al_SetFailAccountability`, and `al_GenerateExport` refuses a non-pass Outcome that records no accountability so nothing incomplete reaches Trail Light. Copying the case's adviser and paraplanner into all four pairs on any fail was rejected as asserting an attribution nobody made; deriving from `al_FailReason` categories was rejected because no approved document maps a category to a person. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-outcome-creation-design.md`. |
| OD-025 | Approve rotation of the plug-in signing key, its removal from git history, and injection at build time. | Platform owner / IT security | Closing code audit finding C3 | Partially resolved | **2026-08-31: deferred by project owner direction, with the resolution path named — an Azure Key Vault will be created to hold the signing key, so it is injected at build time rather than committed.** That settles the third part of this decision and leaves the other two unscheduled: rotating the key (which changes the public key token and so requires re-registering all 17 plug-in types in every environment, using the service account per OD-023) and removing the key from git history (a force-push over commits already published to `github.com/maphalemohlala/OutcomeTesting`). Standing where it was: strong-naming is not a .NET trust boundary and exploiting this needs Dataverse deployment privilege, so this is defence-in-depth. Note the Key Vault alone does not close the finding while the committed key still reproduces the current token — it is the enabler for rotation, not a substitute for it. Raised 2026-08-29. `OutcomeTesting.Plugins.snk` is a full private key blob committed to the repository, and it reproduces the `PublicKeyToken=86b764d5a2430b1f` the registration tool expects. Rotating it changes that token, so every registered plug-in type must be re-registered and `src/PluginAssemblies/` re-exported; removing it from history rewrites published commits. Both are outward-facing and irreversible, so neither is done without approval. Strong-naming is not a .NET trust boundary and exploiting this needs Dataverse deployment privilege, so this is defence-in-depth, not an open door. **2026-09-08: the key is untracked and out of `main`'s history, and the finding is NOT closed.** `git filter-repo` stripped it from all 175 commits; `main` was force-pushed; the 37 commit citations that broke were repointed from the tool's own commit-map. It remains on disk because `SignAssembly` names it, so a fresh clone cannot build until it is supplied out of band. **Two remote branches still carry it** — `feat/role-assignment-conflict-rule` and `feature/outcome-creation-dev-deployment`, both fully merged, both needing a force-push of their rewritten versions. And the key was public on GitHub, so it must be treated as compromised: history removal does not un-publish it. **Only rotation closes this.** |
| OD-026 | Re-export the solution plug-in assembly so managed promotion carries every plug-in type. | Delivery (needs an authenticated DEV connection) | TEST/PROD promotion; DEV runtime is unaffected except for `SetFailAccountabilityPlugin`, which this branch adds and which has no DEV registration at all | Resolved | Raised 2026-08-29 from code audit finding C1, and **narrowed 2026-08-30 after checking the environment directly**. The audit's runtime half no longer applies: DEV holds all 16 plug-in types and all 14 Custom APIs, `al_UpdateUser` and `al_SetUserActive` among them, so renaming a user and deactivating a leaver do work. What remains is solution source: `src/PluginAssemblies/` still declares 11 types against the 16 the assembly now builds, so a managed promotion would carry an assembly missing five. Fixing it means exporting the solution from DEV and committing what Dataverse emits (AD-013), not hand-authoring the `PluginTypeId` values. **Widened 2026-08-30, same day, later on this branch**: the branch adds `SetFailAccountabilityPlugin` (`al_SetFailAccountability`, OD-024), so the assembly now builds 17 plug-in types against the 11 `src/PluginAssemblies/` still declares — a gap of six, not five. Unlike the other five, this one is not a solution-source-only gap: `SetFailAccountabilityPlugin` has not been pushed, its plug-in type has not been created, and its Custom API has not been registered anywhere, including DEV by hand. The deployment order recorded in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-30-outcome-creation.md` (Verification in DEV) covers registering it; until that runs, the export refusal it exists to satisfy cannot be cleared through the intended command in any environment. **Solution-source half closed 2026-08-30** by the DEV deployment: `pac plugin push` landed the 17-type assembly, `Register-CustomApiFromContract.ps1` created the plug-in type and the Custom API, and the AD-013 export-and-replace round trip brought all 17 types back into `src/PluginAssemblies/` and added `src/customapis/al_SetFailAccountability/`. `al_SetFailAccountability` was then verified end to end in DEV. What stays open is narrower and is now the *opposite* concern: `src/PluginAssemblies/` should probably not hold a DLL at all, because while it does, every full-`src/` pack is one import away from reverting the live assembly (AD-062). **2026-09-01: the hazard stopped being hypothetical.** Merging `a56d05b` put two different committed builds of the same assembly against each other — 17 plug-in types on this branch, 16 on `main`, overlapping in 13 — so taking either side of the conflict would have dropped live registrations. `53c71fa` resolved it by rebuilding the DLL from merged source and hand-assembling the manifest as the union of both exports (20 types, 20 distinct ids; every shared type carried an identical id, confirming a common origin org). That is a defensible merge resolution but it is **not** an AD-013 artefact: no export produced it. The merged 20-type assembly was pushed to DEV on 2026-09-01 and landed cleanly. The round trip stays owed and is now explicitly ordered *after* registering the three RBAC Custom APIs, since exporting first would strip them from `src/customapis/`. See `docs/deployment/2026-09-01-dev-reconciliation.md`. **RESOLVED 2026-09-02.** Both prerequisites ran in order: the three RBAC commands were registered from their contracts by `plugins/OutcomeTesting.Registration` (`registerall`), added to the `OutcomeTesting` solution by a Custom-API-only import, and the AD-013 export-and-replace round trip then ran. The export carries 18 Custom APIs and a plug-in assembly manifest declaring 20 plug-in types, matching what the assembly builds, so the manifest is now an export artefact rather than a hand merge and a managed promotion carries every type. Verified from the export itself, and `pac solution pack --folder src` succeeds with only the expected `CanvasApps` warning (AD-012). What this does **not** close is AD-062: `src/PluginAssemblies/` still holds a committed DLL, so anything packing the whole of `src` must strip it first, and that remains true after this round trip because the round trip is what puts the DLL back. **2026-09-06: the round trip ran again, and `src/` is the source of truth for the first time since 2026-09-03.** `al_Notification` had no definition in `src/Entities/` at all — created through the metadata API — so a managed promotion would have shipped plug-ins referencing a table the package does not create. The export now brings in `al_Notification`, `al_DrainNotifications` (22 Custom APIs), four SDK steps including the PP-15 drain (14 total), and a manifest declaring **31 plug-in types matching the 31 the assembly builds**. `pac solution pack --folder src` succeeds with only the expected `CanvasApps` warning. It also corrected a drift nothing reads at run time and nothing reported: 30 custom API parameter and response files carried qualified `<name>` values (`al_AssignCase.Reason`) where DEV holds the bare name. **AD-062 is still not closed** — the round trip is what puts the committed DLL back, so anything packing `src/` for an import must strip `PluginAssemblies/` first. See `docs/deployment/2026-09-06-ad013-round-trip.md`. |
| OD-027 | Decide how a Tax non-pass is preserved through a Tax-then-AQS route, where it is currently lost to the AQS outcome alone. | Product owner | A Tax-then-AQS case can close permanently with an unremediated Tax fail | Resolved | 2026-08-30: product owner decision — **"Tax fail goes to remediation, only passed Tax check can go to AQS."** This reverses the behaviour this plan had chosen and tested deliberately (see the analysis this row previously carried): a Tax-then-AQS route no longer queues for AQS allocation regardless of the Tax result. `OutcomeRules.NextCaseStatusForTax` now tests `TaxResultRequiresRemediation` first, so a Tax non-pass enters `Awaiting Remediation` whatever the route (BR-006); only a passed Tax check still returns `Queued` for AQS allocation (BR-003, AD-040). The reversal is enforced twice, not left as an emergent consequence of case status: `SubmitReviewPlugin.EnsureTaxPrecedesAqs` also refuses an AQS submit outright when the case's submitted Tax review recorded a non-pass on `Q-TAX-02`, so "only passed Tax check can go to AQS" holds as an explicit precondition on the AQS submit itself. `FinaliseReview`'s Tax branch now hops through `Submitted` for every destination except `Queued`, matching `OutcomeRules.HopsFor`, since a Tax fail with AQS still to come is now a finalising transition to `Awaiting Remediation` and that state is only reachable via `Submitted`. **2026-09-09 (OD-038):** completed rather than reversed. A Tax non-pass still enters remediation and still cannot reach AQS unremediated; once the T&C Manager approves that remediation the case returns to the queue and the AQS submit is admitted. `EnsureTaxPrecedesAqs` checks for an approved sign-off on the action the Tax review raised. |
| OD-028 | Correct the roles seed to match on the real alternate key, and reconcile any rows the current key already created. | Delivery | Roles seed idempotency (NFR-REL-01) | Resolved | Raised 2026-08-30 while verifying the route seed against DEV. `data/roles-seed/data_schema.xml` declares `primaryKey="true"` on `al_name`, but the only alternate key on `al_role` is `al_rolecodekey` on `al_rolecode` — confirmed in `src/Entities/al_Role/Entity.xml`. The importer therefore matches roles on display name rather than the business key, so renaming a role makes the next import create a second row instead of updating the first, and two roles sharing a display name collide. `data/route-seed/data_schema.xml` gets this right, which is what made the contrast visible. Pre-existing and outside the outcome-creation plan, so recorded rather than fixed in passing: the roles seed has already been imported against DEV, and changing the match key needs a check for rows it would now orphan. **2026-09-01: source fixed and the orphan check done.** `primaryKey="true"` moves from `al_name` to `al_rolecode`, matching the `al_rolecodekey` alternate key and the pattern `data/route-seed/data_schema.xml` already follows. The reconciliation half is answered: DEV holds 11 `al_role` rows — the 10 seeded roles plus a `Test Role` (`ROLE-TEST-ROLE`) created through the app's new Create role command — and every `al_name` and every `al_rolecode` is distinct, so no row is orphaned or duplicated by the key change and nothing needs reconciling. **Not yet re-imported against DEV**, because `pac data import` does not exist in the installed CLI (`2.11.2+g47bc199`, .NET 10.0.11) — see `docs/deployment/2026-09-01-dev-reconciliation.md`. The fix is therefore verified by inspection and by the DEV row check, not by an idempotency run. **RESOLVED 2026-09-03. The idempotency run has now happened.** The CLI gap is real and permanent — `pac data` does not exist in 2.11.2 (re-confirmed against the installed binary's verb list, not just the earlier note), and the Configuration Migration Tool is a WPF application whose `/I` and `ImportData` strings are control names, not switches. So a headless host was built: `plugins/OutcomeTesting.DataImport` drives `Microsoft.Xrm.Tooling.Dmt.ImportProcessor` — the same engine the GUI calls — via `ImportCrmDataHandler.ImportDataToCrm(folder, deleteBeforeAdd: false)`. Reusing the engine matters: the matching semantics being proved are the real ones, not a hand-rolled lookalike. `data/roles-seed` was imported against DEV **twice**, both runs reporting 0 failures. `al_role` held **11 rows before and 11 after** — the 10 seeded roles plus `ROLE-TEST-ROLE` — with every `al_rolecode` and every `al_name` distinct. The corrected `primaryKey="true"` on `al_rolecode` therefore matches on the real alternate key and updates in place, which is what NFR-REL-01 requires. Two traps worth carrying: the package targets **net48**, not net462, and `CrmServiceClient` binds ADAL at *runtime*, so omitting `Microsoft.IdentityModel.Clients.ActiveDirectory` builds clean and then fails on first connect. **Confirmed and closed by Delivery 2026-09-05.** This row carried its own evidence as complete since 2026-09-03 and stayed Open only because this log does not move an OD to resolved without a named owner and a date. Both are now recorded and nothing further was run: the source fix, the orphan check and the two clean import runs against DEV are the evidence, unchanged. |
| OD-029 | Build manual case allocation. (Role question resolved 2026-08-31: Team Lead maps to the existing Senior Checker role.) | Product owner (role question) / Delivery (build) | BR-003 allocation; unblocks the BR-004 Tax-then-AQS handoff, which currently has no supported path out of `Queued` | Resolved | **2026-09-02: built and deployed to DEV.** `al_CaseAssignment` gains `al_assigneduserid` (systemuser) and `al_assignedcontactid` (contact); the `al_AssignCase` command writes the assignment row, releases the prior active one, stamps the review instance in both identity systems, moves the case `Queued -> Assigned` through the AD-057 table and writes the audit event on the reserved value 120910751. The `/cases/:caseId/allocation` screen replaces its `NotBuiltYet` stub. See AD-072. Point (c) below is **still true and unclosed**: nothing stops a Tax lead allocating an AQS check, and the screen deliberately offers no team filter rather than implying an enforcement that does not exist. Point (a)'s consequence also stands — Senior Checker cannot be expressed in the `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` seed matrix, so granting it `command.assign` is a Dataverse row created through the Security configuration screen, not a code change. **2026-08-31: the role question is settled — Team Lead maps to the existing Senior Checker role, not a new one.** Project owner direction. No new `al_role` row, so the roles seed is untouched, which also keeps OD-028 out of the way. Three things about how that lands, all verified against DEV and the code on 2026-08-31: **(a) Senior Checker sits on the role-code path, not the app-role path.** The permission model matches a rule by *either* `al_approle` (a six-value optionset: Tax Checker, AQS Checker, Adviser, T and C Manager, Outcome Testing Manager, Administrator) *or* `al_rolecode`. Senior Checker exists only in the `al_role` table, so the grant is an `al_pagepermission` row with `al_rolecode = 'ROLE-SENIOR-CHECKER'`. `PermissionHelpers.MaxLevel` already supports this, so **no schema or optionset change is needed** — but the app's `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` seed matrix in `app/src/types/permissions.ts` is keyed on the six app roles, so Senior Checker cannot be expressed there and must be granted as a Dataverse row through the Security configuration screen. **(b) The `command.assign` resource key already exists** and is granted in the seed matrix to T&C Manager and Outcome Testing Manager, so adding Senior Checker is one row rather than a new key. **No plug-in enforces `command.assign` today** — it is a client-side gate only; the assign command built under this OD is what would enforce it server-side. **(c) Per-team scoping is not addressed by this choice.** The direction was that *each team* has a team lead, but Senior Checker is a flat role with no team affiliation, so nothing would stop a Tax senior checker allocating AQS cases. `al_CaseAssignment.al_assignedteam` is the hook if the assign command should scope by queue; whether it must is undecided. The grant is deliberately **not** wired up yet: adding a permission for a command that does not exist would be inert and would read as though allocation were available. It belongs with the build below. Raised 2026-08-31 from the direction that team leads and managers assign cases from the PowerApps app. Four things are missing, and AD-040 assumed all of them: **(1)** the assigned-user lookup on `al_CaseAssignment`, deferred by AD-029 and declared unblocked by AD-040 but never added; **(2)** a server-side assign/reassign command, which AD-040 requires be one (AD-003) and which does not exist among the 15 registered; **(3)** the permission gate for team leads and managers; **(4)** the allocation surface in the code app. The role question is genuinely open: the ten seeded `al_role` rows are Administrator, Outcome Testing Manager, T&C Manager, Tax Checker, AQS Checker, Senior Checker, Adviser, Paraplanner, Reviewer and Read Only User — **there is no Team Lead**, and whether it is a new role or an existing one (Senior Checker is the nearest) changes both the seed and the permission model. Note the manager half is already covered by existing roles, so only the team lead is new. Until this is built, allocation in DEV happens by editing `al_casestatus` directly, which bypasses BR-003's assignment history and BR-012's audit event. | | 2026-08-31 |
| OD-030 | Name the nine PP-15 notification events, and confirm the delivery mechanism now that Power Automate is no longer used anywhere else. | Product owner (events) / Delivery (mechanism) | Blocks the `al_Notification` outbox and all of PP-15 | Resolved | Raised 2026-09-02 on starting PP-15. Two gaps, both genuine. **(a) The nine events are not enumerated anywhere.** PP-15 says nine; AD-035 names five — allocation, submission, remediation assignment, sign-off outcome, rejection. The other four appear in no requirement, knowledge file or design doc, so a nine-value option set means inventing four business events. Everything else is ready: `al_Notification` is in the approved core model under Operations in `project-context.md`, and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-29-power-pages-portal-design.md` already fixes the shape — a row written in the same transaction as the state change and drained by a consumer, which is what makes retries safe and duplicate sends impossible. **(b) Delivery is no longer obviously Power Automate.** AD-035 chose the Office 365 Outlook connector, but AD-073 removed Power Automate from the portal and cancelled the submit flow, so the solution now carries no Power Automate dependency at all. Reintroducing one for email is worth deciding deliberately rather than by inheritance; the alternative is Dataverse server-side email, which needs an approved mailbox on the environment and has not been checked. Recorded rather than guessed because either choice commits the solution to a licence and an operational dependency. **2026-09-03, partially answered by project owner direction — still Open.** Direction given: **notifications are all via email.** That closes the *channel* question and rules out any second channel — no in-app notification centre, no Teams post, no SMS — so `al_Notification` drains to one transport and PP-15 recipients need only an email address. It does **not** close gap (b), because both candidate mechanisms are email: the AD-035 Power Automate Office 365 Outlook connector, and Dataverse server-side email. Choosing between them is still the decision that commits a licence and an operational dependency, and the Dataverse route still needs an approved mailbox confirmed on the environment. Gap (a), the four unnamed events, is untouched by this direction and remains the thing that blocks the option set. **2026-09-03, gap (b) closed by project owner direction: Dataverse server-side email, no Power Automate.** The solution therefore reintroduces no Power Automate dependency and carries none anywhere after AD-073 — the outbox drains through Dataverse's own email, not a connector, so PP-15 adds no per-user connector licence. **One prerequisite this commits the environment to:** server-side email needs an approved, tested mailbox on each environment, and that has not been checked on DEV, TEST or PROD. Confirm it before building the drain, because an unapproved mailbox fails at send time with the row already written — the outbox will look correct and nothing will arrive. Gap (a) alone now blocks PP-15. **2026-09-03, gap (a) answered by project owner direction: build the five AD-035 events only.** The other four are added when someone names them, because adding option values is additive and safe while inventing four business events is not. `al_Notification` is therefore **built** as an outbox carrying five events, with emitters for all five (AD-080); rows rest at `Pending`. **Two things still open, and PP-15 is not deliverable until both close.** (i) The drain is not built, because the server-side mailbox prerequisite above is still unchecked on DEV, TEST and PROD — nothing sends, which is why `Pending` is the resting state rather than a queue that looks healthy and is not. (ii) **Recipient routing for `Review submitted` is undecided.** For allocation, remediation assignment and both sign-off outcomes the record itself names the person, so the emitter reads it; for a submitted review nothing in any requirement says who hears about it. That event is queued with no recipient rather than sent to a guessed address, so the gap is visible in the outbox instead of buried in code. **2026-09-04, both remaining items closed; OD-030 is resolved.** (i) The drain is built: an asynchronous post-operation Create step on `al_notification` sends through Dataverse server-side email from the account the step runs as, and **registering the step is the per-environment gate** — an environment without an approved, tested mailbox does not get the step and its rows rest at `Pending`, which keeps this decision's own warning honoured rather than overridden. A send that fails now ends at `Failed` carrying `al_failurereason`, so the 'outbox looks correct and nothing arrives' failure this entry warned about is visible on the record instead of silent, and `al_DrainNotifications` retries it once the mailbox is approved. See AD-081. (ii) Recipient routing for `Review submitted` is settled by project owner direction: the para-planner named on the case, resolved to a Contact by name, with an ambiguous or missing match left unrouted rather than guessed. That is also what makes BR-009 deliverable. See AD-082. **What is still not closed is not this OD:** PP-15 names nine events and five are built (gap (a), answered by direction on 2026-09-03). The other four remain unnamed by anyone, and adding them stays additive. **2026-09-04, DEV mailbox checked for the first time — and it is not usable.** This entry said for two days that the server-side mailbox had never been checked on any environment. It has now been checked on DEV: `svc.automate.aq@ascotlloyd.co.uk` has `isemailaddressapprovedbyo365admin = No`, `outgoingemailstatus = Not Run` and `testmailboxaccesscompletedon = 1/1/1900` — configured for server-side synchronisation, never approved, never tested. The drain step was therefore **deliberately not registered** when everything else deployed (commit `9390177`), which is the AD-081 gate doing its job: registering it would have stamped every notification `Failed` and destroyed the honest `Pending` backlog. An O365 admin has to approve and test the mailbox before notifications can be switched on. See `docs/deployment/2026-09-04-pp15-drain-pp17-portal-deployment.md`. **2026-09-05: the mailbox is good and the path is proven end to end.** The 09-04 paragraph above is superseded, not deleted, because the sequence it records is the reason the gate exists: `svc.automate.aq@ascotlloyd.co.uk` now reads `isemailaddressapprovedbyo365admin = Yes` and `outgoingemailstatus = Success`, the drain step is registered, and an allocation raised in DEV travelled emitter -> outbox row -> asynchronous drain -> server-side email -> **delivered**, twice. Evidence: two `al_notification` rows at `Sent`, each with an Outgoing/`Sent` email activity and an Incoming/`Received` copy of the same message tracked back into the service mailbox. Reproduce with `provepp15`; read the standing evidence with `pp15evidence`. See `docs/deployment/2026-09-05-pp15-end-to-end-proof.md`. **2026-09-08: PP-15 is met at five events by project owner direction.** The remaining events named in the requirement text are descoped — they were never specified in any requirement, knowledge file or design document, and they are not going to be. The five AD-035 enumerates (allocation, submission, remediation assignment, sign-off outcome, rejection) are built, deployed and proved end to end. The difference between PP-15 as written and the delivery is a scope decision, not a gap. |
| OD-031 | Confirm whether a Tax-only case that closes belongs in the Trail Light export at all. | Advice Quality / Trail Light | AD-039 columns 10 and 15; blocks `al_GenerateExport` for any batch containing such a case | Resolved | **2026-09-03: resolved as candidate (c) — export Tax-only cases with columns 10 and 15 explicitly blank.** Project owner direction. Not (a) exclude, because a closed Tax-only case is still a checked case and dropping it silently from the file makes the export disagree with the case list; not (b) keep refusing, because Q-FQ-01 lives on the AQS review and no Tax-only case can ever answer it, so the refusal was permanent rather than a prompt to act. See AD-075 for the implementation and for the rule that was **not** relaxed. Original note: raised 2026-09-03 while closing two gaps in `GenerateExportPlugin`. The export selects every case at `al_casestatus = Closed`, but the AD-039 contract only carries File Quality and Advice Quality. A Tax-only case has neither: Q-FQ-01 is answered on the AQS review, so column 10 has no source, and `SubmitReviewPlugin.CreateOutcome` writes `al_Outcome` on the AQS submit, so column 15 has none either. Until now such a case exported a row of blanks, which in a fixed-position contract reads as an assessment that came back empty rather than one never made. The fix refuses the batch and names the case, which **fails closed and forces this decision** — the deliberate consequence is that one Tax-only closed case now blocks the whole export until someone answers this. Three candidate answers, none of which may be picked without approval because each changes what Trail Light receives: (a) exclude Tax-only cases from the selection, which is the reading AD-039 hints at ("the map contains no Tax column… a Tax export is not in this contract and must not be invented") but is still a scope rule nobody has stated; (b) keep refusing, and require every closed case to carry a file-quality answer; (c) export them with explicit blanks, which is the behaviour just removed. Note DEV cannot yet distinguish these empirically — it holds zero Closed cases and zero `al_outcome` rows (verified 2026-09-03), so no Tax-only closed case exists to test against. |
| OD-032 | Give `SetFailAccountability` its own `al_command` option value. It currently reuses 120910788, which the option set labels `SetRoleAssignmentActive`. | Delivery | Audit labelling and command-scoped idempotency | Resolved | Raised 2026-09-03 while adding `ResolveImportException` (120910791) to the same option set. Two consequences, both real. Every Audit Event `SetFailAccountability` writes is **labelled as another command**, so the AD-039 accountability trail reads as a role change. And `CommandHelpers.FindAuditByKey` scopes a replay lookup to `(idempotency key, command)` precisely so a key used by one command cannot replay against another — with two commands sharing a value, that protection is off between these two, and a key first used by one can return the other's result for work that never ran. The fix is additive: mint a new value, point `SetFailAccountabilityPlugin` at it, and leave existing rows alone since they are immutable (NFR-AUD-01). Not done here because it changes what already-written audit rows mean, which is a call for whoever owns the accountability trail. **RESOLVED 2026-09-05. Value minted, plug-in repointed, and the cut-over dated.** `al_command` now carries **120910792 = `SetFailAccountability`** (inserted and published against DEV by `addcommandvalue`, 24 values, read back from metadata rather than trusted from the insert), and `SetFailAccountabilityPlugin` writes it. The assembly was rebuilt and pushed; 301 plug-in tests pass. **How the history is read, which was the actual decision:** the cut-over is **2026-09-05**. Audit rows on 120910788 created **before** that date are `SetFailAccountability` writes if they carry `al_name = "SetFailAccountability"` and `al_targettable = "al_outcome"`, and are `SetRoleAssignmentActive` otherwise; on and after it, 120910788 means `SetRoleAssignmentActive` and nothing else. The five known rows (all created 2026-08-30) are covered by that rule exactly, so no data surgery was needed and none was done — NFR-AUD-01 makes those rows immutable and this fix never proposed to touch them. The replay-protection half closes with the same change: `CommandHelpers.FindAuditByKey` scopes on (idempotency key, command) again, restoring the safety net before a caller exists rather than after. **Not updated, deliberately:** `app/src/generated/models/Al_auditeventsModel.ts` and `app/.power/schemas/dataverse/auditevents.Schema.json` are autogenerated and already lag DEV by one value (they do not carry 120910791 either); they are refreshed by regenerating from Dataverse, not by hand, and nothing in the app calls this command. |
| OD-033 | Clear the authenticated-users flag from the `Administrators` web role in DEV, and reconcile the undeclared `Checker` web role. | Platform owner / Delivery | Every authenticated portal contact currently holds `Administrators` | Resolved | Raised 2026-09-04. Queried against `Env_AQ_Dev`: three web roles carry `mspp_authenticatedusersrole = Yes` — `Authenticated Users` (correct), `Checker` (created 2026-09-03, present in no source file), and **`Administrators`** (modified 2026-09-04 07:37). In Power Pages that flag auto-grants the role to every authenticated user, so every portal contact currently holds whatever `Administrators` is bound to. `webrole.yml` declares `adx_authenticatedusersrole: false` for `Administrators`, so DEV has drifted from source. Timeline suggests cause: the case detail page failed at 07:36:59 (see OD-035) and the flag was set one minute later, so this reads as an attempted workaround for that error rather than an intentional grant — and it did not fix it. **`pac pages upload` does not correct it**: uploads at 08:49 and 08:54 touched the web role rows (`mspp_modifiedon` moved) and left the flag `Yes`, so this needs a deliberate correction, not another upload. `Checker` is separate drift — it exists only in the environment, so an upload will never remove it; someone must decide whether it is wanted and either add it to `webrole.yml` or delete it. Not corrected in passing because both are security-relevant grants on a shared environment and the blast radius of `Administrators` has not been established — nobody has yet listed what is bound to it. **RESOLVED 2026-09-05. Both halves are now closed.** The `Administrators` flag was cleared earlier that day and verified reading `No`. **`Checker` was deleted from DEV** on project owner direction, taken after the evidence below was put to them: it granted nothing, it was declared nowhere, and declaring it in source would have meant keeping a role nobody had claimed. Deleted by `deletewebrole`, which refuses to delete a role anything is bound to — it scanned every site component's `content` for the role id (`0931e8d2-67a7-f111-aaac-e4fade069307`) and found no reference, then deleted and confirmed by re-query that no role of that name remains. That guard is the part worth keeping: an empty role and a role that grants something look identical from the role row, and only one of them is safe to remove. `Authenticated Users` correctly keeps the flag, which is what source declares. |
| OD-034 | Restore a working `pac pages upload` path, or adopt a supported alternative for portal deployment. | Delivery | Blocks every portal change that is not a web template or web file, including the OD-035 fix | Resolved | Raised 2026-09-04, escalating a tooling note carried since 2026-09-03. `pac pages upload` **cannot complete against this site**. Every run — delta and `--forceUploadAll`, both with `-mv Enhanced` — aborts at roughly 44% on one record: `Upload failed for file 'adx_entitypermission' (record a1000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000072): the target entity was not found`, then `The entity with a name = 'adx_entitypermission' ... was not found in the MetadataCache`. The site is on the **enhanced data model** (`mspp_*`); `adx_entitypermission` does not exist in the environment at all, so `pac` is routing this one record down the Standard path despite `-mv Enhanced`. The record is `Response - on a review assigned to me` (`table-permissions/Response-Of-Assigned-Review.tablepermission.yml`), parent-scoped under `...071`. It is **already correct in DEV** — verified by query, scope `Parent`, relationship `al_reviewinstance_response`, read/write/create/append/append-to as declared — so the upload is failing on a record that needs no change. This is not cosmetic: the abort happens *before* web pages are processed, so no web page change can be deployed at all. Removing the file to get past it was rejected as unsafe: `pac` deletes components absent from source (it attempted exactly that on `...0022` during the 08:49 run), so excluding the permission risks deleting a live permission. Note also that the runbooks' `%USERPROFILE%\.dotnet\tools\pac.exe` path is stale — the working binary ships with the VS Code Power Platform extension. **2026-09-05, root cause found and a fix proven on the export side.** The defect is narrow: `pac` routes **parent-scoped** table permissions (`adx_scope: 756150003` with an `adx_parententitypermission`) down the Standard-model path on an enhanced-data-model site and tries to write `adx_entitypermission`, which does not exist there. Exactly two permissions in source are parent-scoped — `Response - on a review assigned to me` (`…072`) and `Signoff - T&C attestation`; the run aborts on the alphabetically first, so the second is never reached and is presumably equally affected. Every other permission uses scope 756150000/756150001 and uploads fine. **Ruled out with evidence:** upgrading `pac` (2.11.2 *is* the latest published version, per `dotnet tool search`); scoping the upload (`pac pages upload` takes only `--path`, `--deploymentProfile`, `--forceUploadAll`, `--modelVersion`); and blaming manifest drift (the manifest does list 4 permissions absent from source, but all four are already gone from DEV, so the delete attempts are no-ops). **The fix is solution ALM.** Adding the site record alone is a trap — the export then carries an `Assets/powerpagesites.xml` of 842 bytes holding the site header and not one page, template, permission or role. Adding the site **and its 250 site components** (`addsitetosolution`) produces an export carrying all of them, and the parent-scoped permission travels intact with its scope, parent and role links. **Two things this does not yet prove:** the import side is untested (no second environment is authenticated here), and Power Pages solution awareness is documented by Microsoft as a **preview feature**, "not meant for production use" — adopting it as the PROD promotion path is a decision, not a tooling detail. See `docs/deployment/2026-09-05-portal-repairs-and-drain-enable.md`. **2026-09-05, fourth reproduction and one correction.** Run again after a clean pre-flight (every manifest entry absent from source was checked against the environment first: 39 orphans, all already deleted from DEV, so nothing was at risk). It failed identically on `…072`. **But the 2026-09-04 claim that the abort lands before web pages are processed is wrong.** This run reached 86.4 % (19 of 22 events) and did write the web pages — both `case-details` rows carry its timestamp — and the earlier ~44 % simply reflected more dirty records that day. So the upload is not a blanket block on web page deployment: it lands most components and silently drops the parent-scoped permission and anything ordered after it, which varies with what is dirty. That makes it an unpredictability problem rather than a total outage, and it is why every upload still needs verifying by query. | **Resolved 2026-09-07 by `powerpages/Deploy-Portal.ps1`** (`6e2e6ec`), which is now the only sanctioned upload path and is documented as such in `powerpages/README.md`. The script does not work around the fault, it removes what makes it reachable: the `adx_entitypermission` and `adx_entitypermission_webrole` sections are stripped from the manifest before `pac` runs, so there is nothing for it to route down the Standard-model path and — the more dangerous half — nothing for it to reconcile away. Table permissions are then written by `restoretablepermissions` as `powerpagecomponent` type 18 rows, which is the only thing on this site that can create one, and the result is verified by query because a successful-looking upload is not evidence here that a component landed. First run 2026-09-07: upload succeeded, 13 of 13 permissions written and verified, both gates clean beforehand (`Check-ComponentIds.ps1` over 237 identities). The manifest is left stripped deliberately — `pac` does not own table permissions on this site, so that section is not tracking, it is the mechanism that deleted 11 of 13 on 2026-09-06. Deployment record: `docs/deployment/2026-09-07-role-conflict-rule-deployment.md`.
| OD-035 | Repoint the two `case-details` web pages at page template `a1000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000002b` in DEV. | Delivery | `/case-details` is unusable; PP-05 unverifiable in DEV | Resolved | Raised 2026-09-04. Source is fixed and committed (`b04e9e3`); the environment is half-updated and the page is still broken. The page template `OT Case Detail Page` **was** created in DEV at the new id `...002b`, bound to web template `OT Case Detail` — confirmed by query. Both web pages `...032` (root) and `...042` (content) still carry a **null** `mspp_pagetemplateid`, because the upload aborts on OD-034 before reaching web pages. Until those two lookups are set the page renders the generic Power Pages error. Three ways to close it, none taken without direction: set the page template on the Case detail page in the Power Pages management app (two lookups, immediate); PATCH both rows over the Web API, which needs a Dataverse token this machine cannot currently mint (no Azure CLI, and none of `Az.Accounts`, `MSAL.PS` or `Microsoft.Xrm.Data.PowerShell` installed); or fix OD-034 and re-upload. See AD-084 for the cause. | **Resolved 2026-09-05** — see `docs/2026-09-04-outstanding-work.md` §1, which records the repoint and the corrected diagnosis (the pages were not carrying a null page template; both pointed at `…0022`, the colliding id). This row was left saying Open after the fact. Re-verified by query on 2026-09-07 during the OD-034 deploy: `a1000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000032` and `a1000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000042` both carry `mspp_pagetemplateid` `a1000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000002b`. The state is confirmed; the 2026-09-07 deploy is not what produced it.
| OD-037 | Confirm whether the now-unused `al_User` and `al_Role` tables are deleted from Dataverse, or left in place. | Product owner / Delivery | Nothing in flight; both tables are unread by the app | Partially resolved | Raised 2026-09-06. AD-085 decommissioned `al_User` and AD-087 decommissioned `al_Role`: the app reads neither, and `al_user` holds zero rows. Both tables still ship inside the managed solution, so **deleting them is a destructive ALM change** against environments that have already received them, and `data/users-seed`, AD-010 and OD-010 still reference `al_User` by name. Recommendation is to leave both in place until TEST and PROD have taken one managed deploy of the current solution, then remove them in a change that does nothing else. **2026-09-08: the reads are retired and the premise was wrong.** This row said both tables were unread by the app. They were not. `PermissionHelpers.IsRegisteredActive` queried `al_user` on **every permission check**, and because that table went to zero rows while the read treats a missing row as permitted, **deactivating a leaver withdrew nothing** — the sanctioned alternative to deletion under OD-010 failed open, silently. It now reads the contact's statecode, where `SetUserActivePlugin` had been writing since AD-085. A second live defect surfaced with it: `al_SetPagePermission` validated role codes against `al_role` alone, and no `al_role` row carries an `AL Portal - *` code, so configuring a permission for any role the app offers was refused; both call sites now accept a web role or a legacy code. Assembly deployed. **`al_user` is ready to delete** (0 rows, unread). **`al_role` is not yet**: 11 rows and still the fallback half of `RoleCodeExists`, though every `al_rolecode` in use across `al_pagepermission` and `al_userrolemapping` is a web role name, so dropping the fallback changes no behaviour. |
| OD-038 | Decide where a Tax-then-AQS case goes once a remediation raised by its Tax check is signed off: back to `Queued` for the AQS check, or on to `Awaiting Recheck` and `Closed` without one. | Product owner | BR-004 route completion; a `CaseLifecycle` edge `Awaiting Sign-off -> Queued` and its `CASE_STATUS_TRANSITIONS` mirror in `app/src/types/domain.ts` | Resolved | **2026-09-09: project owner direction — the case has to go through remediation before it can reach AQS.** Built the same day: `CaseLifecycle` and `CASE_STATUS_TRANSITIONS` gain `Awaiting Sign-off -> Queued`; `SignoffProgressPlugin.MoveCase` sends an approved sign-off to Queued when `SubmitReviewPlugin.AqsStillOwed` says the route still owes an AQS review, to Awaiting Recheck when the case carries an Outcome to regrade, and through recheck to Closed when it carries none (a remediated Tax-only case, AD-055, has no final outcome to set). `SubmitReviewPlugin.EnsureTaxPrecedesAqs` now admits the AQS submit after a Tax non-pass once the action that Tax check raised carries an approved sign-off (`RemediationApproved`), which is how OD-027's "only a passed Tax check can go to AQS" and this direction fit together: passed, or remediated and approved. The AQS checker picks the case up from the shared queue as for any handoff. Prior note: Raised 2026-09-09 from a logic review of the handoff. The 2026-09-09 direction holds a flagged Tax pass, and OD-027 a Tax non-pass, at `Awaiting Remediation` before AQS, and `OutcomeRules.NextCaseStatusForTax` recorded that the case "returns to the queue for its AQS check once the action is signed off, which is the transition CaseLifecycle already permits". It does not: AD-057 allows `Awaiting Sign-off` only to `Awaiting Recheck` or `Awaiting Remediation`, and `SignoffProgressPlugin` sends an approval to `Awaiting Recheck`. So today a Tax-then-AQS case whose Tax check raised remediation never reaches AQS: after sign-off it goes to recheck and closes with no AQS Outcome. For a Tax **non-pass** that may be OD-027's own wording ("only passed Tax check can go to AQS", and `SubmitReviewPlugin.EnsureTaxPrecedesAqs` would refuse the AQS submit anyway). For a **flagged Tax pass** Q-TAX-02 is Pass, the AQS submit would be permitted, and the direction said "before AQS", so the AQS check appears to be owed. Not built either way: adding the edge is an AD-057 decision, on both tiers, and `SignoffProgressPlugin.MoveCase` would then need to ask whether the route still owes AQS before choosing `Queued` over `Awaiting Recheck`. Also unstated: what moves a case from `Awaiting Recheck` to `Closed`. No command does, so the recheck step is a manual status edit through `al_UpdateCaseDetails` today. |
| OD-039 | Decide whether **subsection becomes a level of the data model**, or the AD-098 code mapping stands as the way the document's blocks are drawn. | Product owner / Delivery | `al_Section`, `al_Question`, `formBlocks` in `app/src/features/reviews/checklistForm.ts`, the matching `case` in `OT Review Detail`, and the AD-104 reference test | Open | Raised 2026-09-10 from the 8 September requirements pack §4.1 and §4.2, which ask for Section -> Subsection -> Question with explicit ordering **in the data model**. The model has two levels, not three: `al_Section` and `al_Question`, each with `al_displayorder`. Subsections exist only as presentation — AD-098 folds sections into the document's blocks by **section code**, in two hand-written mappings, one TypeScript and one Liquid. So §4.2's ordering requirement is already met for sections and questions and is **not modelled at all** for subsections, and §4.4's "add questions without changing application code" is only true within an existing block: adding a subsection, or moving a section between blocks, is a code change in two languages plus a redeploy of both front ends. AD-098 anticipated this exact question and said the mapping "can still be met later by a parent-section column that this mapping would then read instead". Doing it costs a schema change, a seed migration, both renderers rewritten to read the column, and `checklistDocument.test.ts` rewritten with them. Leaving it costs a code change per checklist restructure, permanently. **Not a defect either way** — V8 renders correctly today, verified against `docs/reference/checker-checklist.html`. This is a decision about what the next checklist version costs. |
| OD-040 | Decide whether a review's remediation stays **one action carrying a list of issues**, or becomes **one action per failed item**. | Product owner | `Remediation.NonPassItems`/`Describe`, `al_RemediationAction`, `CompleteRemediationPlugin`, `SubmitReviewPlugin.RemediationApproved`, the OD-038 approval gate, the PP-13 SLA clock, and all three remediation renderers | Resolved | Raised and resolved 2026-09-10, in that order, on the same day. **Raised** from the requirements pack §6.1, which asks that each relevant item display seven fields — the failed question, the recorded response, the failure reason, reviewer comments, the required remedial action, the original outcome type, and positive context. AD-097 had delivered that as **lines of text inside one action's `al_description`**, which cannot carry seven fields, cannot be filtered or counted, and cannot be completed one item at a time. AD-097 itself recorded the reason it had stayed one action and left the question open: completion, sign-off and the OD-038 gate all **count actions**, and the PP-13 clock runs per action, so splitting changes what "done" means for a case. **Resolved as one action per failed item**, built the same day in `d8ee4c4` with the migration verb in `efb4636`. The deciding reason is the agreed form itself: it carries a remedial action, an owner, a target date and a sign-off **against every numbered row**, and those columns are single-valued on `al_remediationaction`, so one action could render the rows but never let the adviser answer them separately. See AD-107 for the build and for the three consequences that were fixed rather than left to surprise. |
| OD-041 | Decide **where the supervisor records the final regraded outcome**: in the portal alongside the sign-off, or in the Code App as today. | Product owner | `al_RegradeCase`, `app/src/app/router.tsx` `/cases/:caseId/recheck`, `OT Remediation`, the contact trigger-column write path (AD-099), portal licensing | Resolved | **2026-09-10: the portal, beside the sign-off — built the same day.** Project owner direction. The page writes `contact.al_regraderequest` (JSON: outcomeId, finalOutcome, reason, expectedRowVersion) through the Self-scoped contact permission, and the synchronous post-operation `RegradeRequestPlugin` checks the contact's web roles server-side for `AL Portal - T&C Supervisor`, clears the column, and calls `RegradeCasePlugin.Regrade` — the same method `al_RegradeCase` now calls, extracted so the two front ends cannot drift on the mandatory reason, the preserved initial outcome (BR-007), the refusal to regrade something never graded, the audit event or the close of a case at Awaiting Recheck. The control offers the four BR-005 grades in BR-005 order and renders wherever the case carries a graded outcome; a remediated Tax-only case (AD-055) has none and gets no control. The Code App at `/cases/:caseId/recheck` is unchanged and still works — this adds a front end, it does not move one. See AD-111. Prior note: Raised 2026-09-10 from the requirements pack §7.2, which puts "record the final regraded outcome" in the same list of supervisor actions as approve, reject and add a decision note. Today those are **two applications**: approval is a portal act by the `AL Portal - T&C Supervisor` web role through the AD-099 trigger column, and the regrade is `al_RegradeCase`, reachable only from the Code App at `/cases/:caseId/recheck`, gated on `page.cases` plus `command.regrade` and sending `ExpectedRowVersion`. So one decision spans two front ends, two permission systems and two licensing positions, and a supervisor who only holds the portal cannot complete it. Building it into the portal is a known shape but not a free one: it would be the **fourth** browser-side create on the site, and the first three (answers AD-053, claims AD-076, sign-off AD-099) were each refused `90040106` whatever the permissions said and each ended as a trigger column plus a server-side plug-in. Note the alternative is legitimate — confirming that regrading is a back-office act closes this without code. |
| OD-042 | Decide the **landing page rule**, and whether any role loses the blanket `page.dashboard` grant. | Product owner / Delivery | `app/src/app/router.tsx` (`/` is ungated), `app/src/types/permissions.ts` seeding, `AppShell`, portal Home | Open | Raised 2026-09-10 from the requirements pack §10.3 and its acceptance criterion "users are routed only to pages they are authorised to access". `/` renders `DashboardPage` with **no `RequirePermission` wrapper**, and `permissions.ts` seeds **every** role with View on `page.dashboard`. Those two facts together are why nobody has met the fault: there is currently no role that lacks dashboard access to be misrouted. The requirement only becomes real once some role legitimately should not land there — which is the decision, and it is the business's, not Delivery's. Building the resolver is the small half (pick the first permitted nav entry, after permissions resolve and before first paint, in the app and again on portal Home); **withdrawing the blanket grant is the half that changes meaning**, because it changes what the 52 seeded web role rules assert. |
| OD-043 | Decide whether the dashboard and the portal outcome cards show **initial and final as two series**, or keep the single effective grade. | Product owner | `useCaseDashboard.ts`, `DashboardPage.tsx`, the PP-17 portal cards and the `?outcome=` list filter | Open | Raised 2026-09-10 from the requirements pack §3.4, which asks outcome reporting to distinguish the initial review outcome from the final outcome after remediation and sign-off. Both are stored and neither overwrites the other (`al_initialoutcome`, `al_finaloutcome`), so §8.1 is met at the data layer. The **presentation** collapses them: `useCaseDashboard.ts` reduces to `final ?? initial`, and AD-083 had the portal's five PP-17 cards do the same deliberately. Showing both means adding a "which grade" dimension to the card aggregates **and** to the drill-down filter, so a card and the list it opens keep agreeing — which is the part that breaks quietly if only one side is changed. Sequencing note: this lands in the same code as the §3.1 dashboard simplification, so deciding it before that work is committed is cheaper than after. |
| OD-044 | Decide whether **"Rejected for further action" becomes a distinct case status**, or stays a rejected case sitting at `Awaiting Remediation`. | Product owner | `CaseLifecycle` transition map, `CASE_STATUS_TRANSITIONS` in `app/src/types/domain.ts`, every case list filter, the dashboard buckets | Open | Raised 2026-09-10 from the requirements pack §6.5, which lists six states the workflow must distinguish and names "rejected for further action" as one of them. Five of the six exist. The sixth does not: a rejected sign-off returns the case to `AwaitingRemediation`, **the same option value** a first-time remediation carries, so nothing in the status can tell a first attempt from a returned one. The data to distinguish them already exists — AD-079 keeps `al_clockstartedon` for the current period and `createdon` for the original, precisely so both are visible — so this can be answered by rendering rather than by a new status. A new status value is the more literal reading and the more expensive one: it changes the transition map on both tiers, every list filter and every dashboard bucket. |
| OD-045 | Name the **shared defect-tracking mechanism** for UAT. | Project owner / Test lead | Nothing in the solution; blocks the pack's §15.4 and its final acceptance criterion | Open | Raised 2026-09-10 from the requirements pack §15.4 and §16 ("identified defects can be recorded and tracked during UAT"). No mechanism is named anywhere in the pack, this repository or the decision log, and nothing in the solution records defects. This is cheap to answer and blocks an acceptance criterion, which is a bad combination to leave until testing starts. It needs no build if an existing tracker is nominated. |
| OD-046 | Supply the **notification templates and the dedicated sender address**. | Business team | `NotificationOutbox` body composition; PP-15 | Open | Raised 2026-09-10 from the requirements pack §9.4, which names three outstanding business inputs: templates for remediation recipients, templates for reviewers and supervisors, and the dedicated sender address. The mechanism is built, switched on and proved end to end in DEV (AD-081, AD-082), and §9.2's direct action links are built — `NotificationOutbox.CaseLink` reads the site domain per environment. §9.3's dedicated sender is **already satisfied in DEV**: the drain runs as `svc.automate.aq@ascotlloyd.co.uk`, a service mailbox, not an individual's. What is missing is wording. Carry the shape risk with it: bodies are composed as C# strings in the assembly, so approved wording either gets hard-coded — a plug-in redeploy per wording change — or needs somewhere to live that is not code. Expect what the business supplies not to fit string composition, and treat "where templates live" as part of this decision rather than a later surprise. |
| OD-047 | Confirm whether **multiple Intelligent Office uploads in one day** are required, and any limit. | Operations / Advice Quality | `ImportCasesPlugin`, `al_ImportBatch`, the AD-010 work-email idempotency key | Open | Raised 2026-09-10, and raised by the requirements pack itself: §11.4 states the requirement "remains to be confirmed. No final limit was stated in the meeting notes." Recorded here so it is owned rather than left in prose. The import is idempotent on work email under AD-010 and NFR-REL-01, so repeat uploads are safe by construction today; what is unconfirmed is whether operations **need** several a day and whether anything should refuse the fourth. Related and worth answering together: §12.3's newer Intelligent Office export version, which is a parser, validation, route-derivation and fixture change, and is the natural moment to add `al_paraplanneremail` if the new extract carries one (see AD-082, where para-planner routing matches on full name because no address exists in the model). |
| OD-048 | Decide **what happens to a case whose route gains a discipline while a check is already open**: return it to the shared queue for the newly-required check, or leave it with the checker who holds it. | Product owner | `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.DeriveRoute`, `CaseQueueing`, `AssignCasePlugin.ReleasePriorAssignments`, the AD-057 `Assigned -> Queued` edge | Resolved | **2026-09-10: return it to the queue automatically — built the same day.** Project owner direction. `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.RequeueAfterRouteChange` runs after the save, and only in the narrow case: this edit actually changed the route, the caller did not set the status themselves, the case is at Assigned or Review In Progress, and the discipline now due has no open review instance. It releases the active assignment (preserved, never deleted — AD-037) and moves the case to `Queued` through the AD-057 edge that already exists for the Tax-to-AQS handoff, writing the reason to the case's history. A route change that did not change what comes next leaves the checker holding their work. The ordering decision is `ClaimCasePlugin.TryNextDiscipline`, extracted so `NextDiscipline` and this share one copy of it. 11 tests. Prior note: Raised 2026-09-10 from the IO-SEED-AQS-04 defect (AD-112). `DeriveRoute` re-derives the route on every edit touching the tax-check answer or the Tax team's disposition, and the review instances already opened are not reconciled with it. AD-112 fixes the half that is unambiguously a bug — a claim now asks the route which discipline is due, so the newly-required check can be created and a premature instance no longer blocks it. But a claim needs the case to be at `Queued`, and a case edited mid-flight sits at `Assigned`: nothing returns it, so it is still stranded, and the AQS submit it offers is refused by `EnsureTaxPrecedesAqs` until the Tax check is in. Requeueing is the obvious fix and AD-057 already permits the transition (it exists for the Tax-to-AQS handoff), but it pulls work off a named checker's desk because someone else edited the case, and releasing their active assignment with it — that is a workflow decision, not a defect, which is why it is here rather than built. The alternative is legitimate: leave the case where it is and require a manager to reallocate it deliberately, in which case the case detail should say so, which the AD-112 template change already does. Note the narrow version — requeue only when the discipline now due has no open instance — is the one that avoids disturbing a case whose route change did not actually change what is next. **IO-SEED-AQS-04 in DEV is stranded until this is settled or its status is edited by hand.** |
| OD-049 | Decide **where the sixteen columns the IO task extract writes are shown**. None is rendered anywhere today -- not `CaseEditPanel`, not `OT Case Detail`, not the case list. | Product owner / Delivery | AD-124; `al_checklistitems` in particular | Open | Raised 2026-09-12 from an audit of the AD-124 import. `al_checklistitems` is now the **only input to the BR-004 route**, and it is invisible: a checker cannot see why a case came to Tax rather than AQS, and nobody can check a route against the reasons the paraplanner selected. The same applies to `al_iooutcome`, which was kept precisely so the outcome IO holds could be reconciled against the grade issued here, and to `al_servicecaseref`, which is the only link back to the IO service case when two tasks share one. The paper conversion of the case-detail page on 2026-09-12 was deliberately scoped to presentation (project owner), so this was not addressed there. **Partly answered 2026-09-19, confirmed 2026-09-20 (AD-158): `al_checklistitems` is displayed** on the portal's review and case pages and the Code App's review and case pages, as a read-only list of the ticked items. The route can now be read against the reasons the paraplanner selected, which was the sharpest half of this question. **Still open for the other fifteen columns**, `al_iooutcome` and `al_servicecaseref` among them - neither is rendered anywhere, and the reconciliation `al_iooutcome` was kept for still cannot be done. |
| OD-050 | Decide **where Tax and AQS complete the nine remaining case-header fields the extract does not carry** (originally eleven; see the 2026-09-22 amendment). They were granted `page.cases` Edit on 2026-09-12, but that is the Code App; the portal case-detail page has no write path at all. | Product owner / Delivery | AD-124 D9/D10; AD-076, AD-113 | Open | Raised 2026-09-12 from an audit of the AD-124 permission change. The client's direction is that ~~adviser code~~, adviser status, ~~paraplanner code~~, products, case type, advice date, product/solution type, sample source, check date, vulnerable client and tax team disposition are "manually completed by Tax/AQS". The grant makes that possible in the **Code App**, on `CaseEditPanel`. But checkers work in the **portal** -- AD-076 and AD-113 put the queue, the claim and the review forms there -- and `OT Case Detail` is read-only: no fetch, no Web API call, no table permission for a case write. So either checkers are expected to use the Code App for this, or the portal needs an editable case-header block with its own write path, which `al_UpdateCaseDetails` does not currently serve (a Power Pages write arrives as the site's application user, AD-053). Note the grant also widened `al_SetFailAccountability`, which shares the `page.cases` Edit gate. **Amended 2026-09-22 (AD-207): adviser code and paraplanner code struck from the list.** Neither is a case field for Tax/AQS to complete any longer -- the code is now a property of the person, held on `contact.al_staffcode` and maintained on the People registry, and the export resolves it from there. The other nine are untouched and this OD stays Open for them. |
| OD-051 | Decide whether the **Tax allocation load** the checklist-derived route creates is acceptable. | Product owner | AD-124 D6; AD-113(a) | Open | Raised 2026-09-12 from an audit of the AD-124 routing. Any case carrying a Tax or Trust Documentation item becomes `ROUTE-TAX-AQS`, and AD-113 made the self-service queue **AQS only** -- a Tax check is allocated to a named checker by the Tax lead and is never picked up. Every row of the supplied workbook carries a Tax item, so on that data every imported case would sit at `Queued` awaiting manual Tax allocation and none would reach the AQS queue until its Tax review is submitted. That may be exactly the intended process (the client's rule is that Tax is always the starting point and decides whether AQS is owed), but the manual allocation volume it implies has not been sized. |
| OD-052 | Decide **what the checklist document's "IO reference" field should print** now that `al_casereference` holds a numeric IO TaskID rather than an IO-shaped reference. | Product owner | AD-124 D2; AD-104; `checklistForm.ts` | Open | Raised 2026-09-12 from an audit of the AD-124 import. The V8 document has a header field named "IO reference", and `checklistForm.ts` fills it from `al_casereference`. Under D2 that column now carries `TaskID` -- `253925362` -- while the value that actually looks like an IO reference, and which a person would quote, is `ServiceCaseSequentialRef` (`IOA07028411`), now held in `al_servicecaseref`. So the printed checklist and the case list both name a case by a number nobody outside IO recognises. The label itself cannot simply be renamed: the document's wording is pinned against the supplied reference file (AD-104), and `checklistForm.test.ts` asserts it. The options are to print `al_servicecaseref` under that label, to print both, or to accept the TaskID. Not urgent -- nothing is wrong in the data -- but it is the first thing a checker reads on a printed check. |
| OD-053 | Decide **how a Tax check outcome recorded in error is put right**. An AQS grade has a route - `al_RegradeCase`, a T&C Manager, a mandatory reason and an immutable Audit Event (AD-031). A Tax outcome has none: it is stamped on `al_outcomecase.al_taxoutcome` at submit and **no command can change it afterwards**. The options are a correction command mirroring the regrade, a lifecycle route that reopens the Tax check, or an explicit decision that a wrong Tax outcome is corrected only by re-running the case. | Product owner | `al_RegradeCase` (al_outcome only); `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.Editables` (thirteen fields, `al_taxoutcome` not among them); `SubmitReviewPlugin.StampTaxOutcome`; AD-031; AD-055 | Open | Raised 2026-09-13 from the project owner's question about why a Tax-only case shows no regrade. The regrade half of that is by design and needs no decision: `al_outcome` carries only the BR-005 four-value advice quality scale, the Tax scale is the three-value PassFailInsufficient of Q-TAX-02 (AD-055), and the two are different judgements - the Tax scale has a Fail that BR-005 has no equivalent for, BR-005 has a Potential harm a Tax check does not assess, and a row on al_Outcome would populate AD-075 export columns a Tax-only case must leave blank and stop the case closing. What is NOT by design is the consequence: verified 2026-09-13 that `al_RegradeCase` operates on `al_outcome` alone and that `al_taxoutcome` appears nowhere in `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin`'s editable-field map, so a Tax outcome entered in error today can only be changed by a direct database edit, which bypasses the audit trail BR-012 and NFR-AUD-01 exist to keep. AD-031 half-notices this - "a remediated Tax-only case carries no al_Outcome, so it has nothing to regrade and still closes only by a manual status edit" - but frames it as a closing problem; the correction problem sits alongside it and was unrecorded. Worth settling before TEST: it is the kind of gap a regulator asks about, and the answer decides whether a command needs building or a lifecycle edge adding. |

## Resolved architecture decisions
| ID | Decision | Rationale | Date |
|---|---|---|---|
| AD-001 | Dataverse is the system of record | Signed target solution supersedes earlier fixed-column Excel/Word designs | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-002 | The frontend is a single Power Apps Code App; Power Pages is superseded for operational users | Explicit direction recorded in the build package assumptions | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-003 | Privileged lifecycle transitions run as server-side commands, not unrestricted client updates | Security must not depend on UI enforcement | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-004 | Checklist content is data-driven and versioned; responses reference an immutable question version | Protects historic submissions and MI when questions change | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-005 | Publisher prefix is `al`; the unpacked solution root is `src` and the Code App lives in `app/` | Matches `AscotLloydOutcomeTesting.cdsproj` `SolutionRootPath` and keeps app build output out of the solution | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-006 | A completed check with a non-pass outcome enters remediation; only invalid or wrong-route cases are returned or cancelled | Resolves the conflict between the early stop-and-restart description and later process confirmation | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-007 | `app/power.config.json` may contain `environmentId` and `appId` as an approved exception to the no-hardcoded-identifiers rule | The Power Apps CLI generates and owns this file, and both values are required for `pa app run` and `pa app push`. See the exception note below. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-008 | Outcome status colours for BR-005 are `Pass #00A800` (brand Accessible Green), `Pass with issues #B87A3D`, `Insufficient evidence #8758E4` (brand Tertiary Purple), `Potential harm #B65A52` | The brand palette contains no warning or danger colour, but BR-005 requires four visually distinct outcomes. The amber and red are deliberately desaturated to sit alongside the brand teals. Measured contrast on white: 3.57, 4.59, 4.62 and 3.18, all above the 3:1 required for non-text indicators.
