Each outcome also carries a distinct shape and its text label, so colour is never the sole signal. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-009 | Outfit is the permanent heading typeface and Inter is the body typeface, both self-hosted through `@fontsource-variable` | Roundo is not being licensed, so Outfit is the approved heading face rather than a stand-in (OD-015, 2026-08-26). Self-hosting avoids an external CDN, which a code app host may block by CSP. Both are declared only in `--font-heading` and `--font-body`. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-010 | Work email is the canonical cross-system identifier between Intelligent Office, Outcome Testing and Trail Light | Resolves OD-003. Alternate keys on Outcome Case and Export Record are built on it, which is what makes imports and exports idempotent under NFR-REL-01. Employee ID may still be stored, but must not be used as a matching key. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-011 | Schema is deployed with `pac solution pack` + `pac solution import`, using the existing `pac` authentication. No service principal, access token or `Az` module is required. | Unmanaged import merges rather than replaces, so a schema-only package adds tables without disturbing other solution components. Verified: the published code app survived a schema import untouched. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-012 | `src/` carries schema and configuration only. The `CanvasApps` component is owned by `pa app push` and is deliberately excluded from solution source. | Packing a checked-in copy of the built bundle would risk overwriting a newer published app with a stale one. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-013 | Entity XML is authored by importing a first draft, then replacing the local file with the version Dataverse emits on export. | Hand-written entity XML is rejected for subtle reasons such as `OrgOwned` rather than `OrganizationOwned`, and required elements like `MaxLength` in a specific sequence position. Round-tripping yields the canonical shape and removes the guesswork for the remaining tables. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-014 | Every configuration table carries a single-column alternate key on its stable business code (`al_<table>code`), declared in solution source as an `<EntityKeys>` element on the entity. | Alternate keys are what make seed data, imports and exports idempotent under NFR-REL-01 without leaking GUIDs into external systems. The element shape is fixed by `CustomizationsSolution.xsd`: `EntityKey` takes `Name`, `LogicalName`, `IntroducedVersion`, `IsCustomizable`, `displaynames` and `EntityKeyAttributes/AttributeName`. Composite keys involving lookups are deferred until the relationship wave. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-015 | Versioned checklist content is modelled as two tables, Checklist Version and Question Version, each holding the payload that must not change after publication: version number, effective-from/effective-to dates, and for questions the exact wording, mandatory flag and display order. | Implements AD-004 and BR-013. Freezing display order and the mandatory flag on the version, rather than only on Question, is what stops a later reorder or requirement change from rewriting history in submitted reviews and MI. Response type is deliberately absent because the permitted response types are still blocked by OD-001. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-016 | The checklist configuration group is a strict single-parent hierarchy: Checklist 1:N Checklist Version 1:N Section 1:N Question 1:N Question Version. Each child carries one business-required lookup to its immediate parent (`al_ChecklistId`, `al_ChecklistVersionId`, `al_SectionId`, `al_QuestionId`). | Anchoring Section to Checklist Version rather than to Checklist is what makes a published version structurally immutable: re-issuing a checklist cannot reorder or remove sections in a version already in use. Question Version then covers in-flight corrections to a single question under FR-030/FR-031 retire-and-succeed, without reissuing the whole checklist. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-017 | All four configuration relationships are referential with `CascadeDelete=Restrict`; every other cascade is `NoCascade`. | Restrict is what enforces referential integrity for configuration data: a checklist, version, section or question that is already referenced by history cannot be deleted, which is the schema-level half of BR-013. `NoCascade` on assign/share/unshare is also the only valid setting, because these tables are organisation-owned. Retirement is modelled by effective-to dates, not deletion. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-018 | One-to-many relationships are authored in `src/Other/Relationships.xml`, with the lookup column declared by the relationship's `<field>` element rather than hand-written into the child `Entity.xml`. | The `EntityRelationship` type in `CustomizationsSolution.xsd` carries a `field` child specifically for this. A lookup cannot exist without its relationship, so declaring it once in the relationship avoids the duplicate-definition failure mode and keeps the pair in step. The export round trip under AD-013 then fills in the generated attribute block. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-019 | Every displayed answerable question is mandatory before its section can be submitted. Observation, note and comment fields are optional. A section that is not displayed is not validated, and a conditional section that is displayed is validated in full. | Confirmed in the process mapping session 2 workshop: every checklist question must be answered before submission. Making the rule structural rather than per-question is what stops a partially answered section reaching MI and satisfies FR-016 submission locks. `al_QuestionVersion.al_IsMandatory` still carries the flag per question version, because AD-004 requires the flag to be frozen on the version rather than recomputed from a rule that may later change. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-020 | Section ownership is a role, held on `al_Section`, not inferred from the section name. Tax check is owned by the Tax team; AML/CRA, file quality and all advice quality sections are owned by the AQS checker; remediation is owned by the Adviser; remediation validation and sign-off for Insufficient evidence and Potential harm are owned by the T&C Manager; checklist configuration is owned by Manager/Admin. | Resolves OD-001. Tax was the only section labelled with a team in V8, which was a documentation gap rather than a design intent. An explicit owner role is what lets FR-011 section ownership and the AD-003 server-side commands enforce who may write to a section, instead of relying on the UI. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-021 | CRP applicability is derived by the system from the case product/solution type. The checker is given no manual applicability control. Where CRP does not apply, its questions are recorded as N/A rather than omitted. | Resolves OD-006. The requirement is policy-driven, so leaving it to checker judgement would produce inconsistent MI and let the section be skipped. Recording N/A rather than omitting keeps the response set complete for every case, which is what makes the BR-010 MI comparable across cases. The ad hoc investment withdrawal case is unresolved and tracked as OD-016. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-022 | Fixed response scales and the two bespoke answer lists are modelled as Dataverse option sets, not as an answer-option table. Tax check reason is multi-select; Primary root cause is single-select. | The scales (`PassFailInsufficient`, `YesNoNA`, `YesNoInsufficient`, `YesNo`, `PassFail`, `Grade`) repeat across many questions, and tax reasons and root causes are policy lists rather than admin-maintained checklist content. BR-013 requires administrators to maintain *questions* without corrupting history, which option sets do not obstruct. Consequence to accept: adding a root cause or tax reason is a solution change, not a data edit. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-023 | A Response stores its answer in the typed column that matches the question version's response type: `al_AnswerText`, `al_AnswerDate`, `al_AnswerChoice` or `al_AnswerChoices`. `al_AnswerChoice` is one option set holding the union of every single-select value in V8, including the BR-005 grade and the Primary root cause. | The six V8 scales draw from a shared vocabulary of eight values, so a union option set stores them all without a column per scale and without an answer-option table (AD-022). The permitted subset for any answer is already fixed by `al_QuestionVersion.al_ResponseType`, so the constraint lives in the question version rather than being duplicated in the schema, and it is enforced server-side under AD-003. It is also what lets AD-021 record a non-applicable CRP question as N/A on a `PassFailInsufficient` question rather than omitting the response. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-024 | Review Instance and Response are user-owned; Response is parental to Review Instance, cascading assign, share, unshare, reparent and delete. Checklist configuration tables remain organisation-owned. | Reviews are allocated to queues and named individuals under BR-003, which organisation ownership cannot express. Making Response parental means BR-012 access granted on a review instance reaches its answers without a second security rule, and reassignment moves the whole answer set in one operation. Configuration is reference data with no owner, so it stays organisation-owned. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-025 | Fail Reason attaches to Response through a many-to-many relationship (`al_failreason_response`), not a lookup. | FR-013 permits several fail reasons on one response, and a file quality fail routinely cites more than one. A lookup would force either one reason per response or a duplicate response row, and either would distort the BR-010 fail reason MI. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-026 | Every business code column is declared with `MaxLength` 100. `Length` in the entity XML is output only: Dataverse ignores changes to it and re-emits its own value. | The Wave A and Wave B code columns were hand-written with `Length` equal to `MaxLength`, and Dataverse read `Length` as bytes, so each column was physically created at half its advertised size. `al_ChecklistCode` advertised 20 characters but held 10, which passed metadata validation and then failed in SQL with "String or binary data would be truncated" when the V8 seed wrote `CHK-CHECKER`. Raising `Length` alone had no effect; only raising `MaxLength` alters the physical column. All seven code columns were widened in one import, and widening is non-destructive. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-027 | Checklist content is seeded from a Configuration Migration package in `data/v8-seed`, loaded with `pac data import --data data\v8-seed`. | Re-running the import updates the existing rows rather than duplicating them, which is what NFR-REL-01 requires of configuration loading; four consecutive runs left exactly 117 records. The package lives outside `src/` because it is data, not a solution component, and must not be packed into the managed solution promoted to TEST and PROD. Lookup fields need `lookupentityname` as well as `value`, because the importer defers some lookups to a second pass that resolves by primary name. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-028 | Wave F adds three user-owned case-management tables: `al_OutcomeCase` (case header, BR-001 import key `al_casereference` as the alternate key, and the canonical lifecycle as the `al_casestatus` picklist), `al_CaseAssignment` (allocation history) and `al_RemediationAction` (BR-006 actions). Only the settled core is built; parts blocked by an open decision are omitted and cite the OD. | User ownership expresses both team-queue and named-individual allocation (BR-003) without committing to the OD-002 allocation model. The status picklist carries only the canonical `Imported`..`Closed` lifecycle; the Tax wrong-route / no-check-required statuses stay out pending OD-008. Remediation carries `Open`/`In progress`/`Completed` only; validation-outcome and regrade states stay out pending OD-007. Assignment stores the team as text and preserves superseded rows so no history is lost. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-029 | Wave F relationships: `al_reviewroute_outcomecase` (route lookup, Restrict delete), the self-referential `al_outcomecase_previouscase` (previous/replacement case, RemoveLink delete), `al_outcomecase_caseassignment` and `al_outcomecase_remediationaction` (Restrict delete to preserve history), and `al_reviewinstance_remediationaction` (the triggering instance, Restrict delete). The assigned-user, assigned-adviser and validated-by user lookups are deferred. | Restrict delete is the schema half of preserving assignment and remediation history (BR-003, BR-007, BR-012). The self-reference must not cascade a delete to the case it replaced, so it removes the link instead. `NoCascade` is invalid for a delete behaviour; `RemoveLink` is the correct "keep the other record" setting. The named-user lookups are entangled with the OD-002 allocation model and the OD-007 validation roles, so they are held back; current allocation is already carried by the record owner. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-030 | The Case worklist reads live Outcome Case data through a Power Apps code-app data source generated by `pa app add data-source --connector dataverse --table al_outcomecase`. The generated `Al_outcomecasesService` and typed model live under `app/src/generated`, and the data source is registered in `app/power.config.json` `databaseReferences`. | This is the supported code-app data-access path; the generated service wraps `getClient(dataSourcesInfo)` so row-level visibility is enforced by Dataverse security (BR-012), not by the query. Adding the data source required first creating a Dataverse connection (`pa connection create --connector dataverse`), which is a one-time interactive browser step because no connection existed in the environment. `databaseReferences` records only the entity set and logical name, so no secret or environment-specific connection identifier is committed; the AD-007 exception boundary is unchanged. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-031 | The privileged outcome-correction actions — reopen, override and regrade — are owned by the T&C Manager, mirroring AD-020's outcome accountability, with Outcome Testing Manager and Administrator as platform-level escalation; each requires a mandatory reason persisted to an immutable Audit Event and preserves the initial outcome. | Resolves OD-007. AD-020 already assigns regrade of Insufficient evidence and Potential harm to the T&C Manager, so the same role owns reopen and override of a submitted or closed outcome rather than a new authority being invented. The mandatory reason and the preservation of the initial outcome are not new rules: they follow BR-012 edit protection and audit history, NFR-AUD-01, AD-003 server-side commands and BR-007, and the `command-concurrency` skill already required them. Recording it unblocks the Sign-off, Recheck and Outcome schema wave and the `RegradeCase`/`SignOffRemediation` command guards. The resolution is isolated to the command caller-role map and a reason field, so Advice Quality/Compliance can override it with a one-line change. | 2026-08-26 **Amended 2026-09-09:** `al_RegradeCase` now closes a case that is at Awaiting Recheck when the final outcome is set, which is the lifecycle's own "Awaiting Recheck/Regrade -> Closed" step: the Recheck table is deferred and the final outcome on `al_Outcome` is the recheck. Nothing else moved a case off that state, and the export collects Closed cases only. A regrade of a Closed case leaves the status alone. Unresolved: a remediated Tax-only case carries no `al_Outcome` (AD-055), so it has nothing to regrade and still closes only by a manual status edit. **The correction half of that is OD-053, raised 2026-09-13:** the privileged corrections this decision grants reach `al_outcome` only, so a Tax outcome stamped on `al_outcomecase.al_taxoutcome` at submit can be put right by no command at all. |
| AD-032 | Wave H adds two user-owned tables that complete the outcome lifecycle now that OD-007 is resolved: `al_Outcome` (BR-007 initial and final outcomes; `al_regradereason`/`al_regradedon` capture the AD-031 mandatory-reason regrade) and `al_Signoff` (FR-023/BR-008 T&C validation, decision Approved or Rejected with mandatory rejection notes). Relationships are all Restrict delete: `al_outcomecase_outcome`, `al_reviewinstance_outcome`, `al_outcomecase_signoff`, `al_remediationaction_signoff`. | These tables were blocked only by OD-007; with the roles and the mandatory reason settled in AD-031, the regrade fields and the sign-off decision vocabulary are no longer deferred. User ownership matches `al_ReviewInstance`/`al_Response`, so BR-012 access flows to the outcome and sign-off records. Restrict delete is the schema half of preserving the initial and final outcome and the accountability trail (BR-007, BR-012). Lookups were hand-declared in the child entities and the relationships authored without a `<field>` element, per the verified pattern that avoids the silent lookup no-op. Option value block 1209107xx: Outcome initial 700–703, final 710–713; Sign-off decision 720–721. The separate Recheck table remains in the AD-028 model but is not built: FR-024 final outcome is already carried by `al_Outcome`, so a distinct Recheck entity is deferred until its trigger and decision vocabulary are specified rather than invented. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-033 | The intake wave adds two user-owned tables for FR-001 to FR-003: `al_ImportBatch` (one uploaded Intelligent Office extract — `al_source`, `al_importedon`, a `Received`/`Validating`/`Completed` `al_batchstatus`, and `al_totalrows`/`al_importedcount`/`al_exceptioncount`, keyed by `al_importbatchcode`) and `al_ImportException` (one row that failed validation — `al_rownumber`, `al_casereference` (BR-001), `al_reason` (BR-002), `al_rawdata`, an `Open`/`Resolved`/`Ignored` `al_exceptionstatus` and `al_resolvedon`, keyed by `al_importexceptioncode`). One relationship `al_importbatch_importexception` (Restrict delete). The `/imports` code-app screen reads both through generated data sources and is now built. | Intake was never OD-blocked; it only needed its own tables, so this wave carries no deferral. The exception carries the case reference as plain text rather than a lookup because a failed row has not yet become a case, and may be missing its key entirely; the batch code and exception code alternate keys make re-uploading the same extract idempotent under NFR-REL-01. Restrict delete keeps the exception triage history under its batch (BR-002, BR-012). The `al_importbatchid` lookup was hand-declared in the child entity and the relationship authored without a `<field>` element, per AD-013's verified round-trip pattern that avoids the silent lookup no-op. Option value block: batch status 1209107 30–32, exception status 40–42; next free block 1209107 50+. The upload and resolve write actions remain a later command/flow path; the screen is a read-only intake overview, the safe slice consistent with the other built screens. | 2026-08-26 |
| AD-034 | Automated SFTP delivery (OD-005) and a Power BI build (OD-012) are both out of MVP scope. Export is a manual, on-demand production of a Trail Light-compatible file; MI is served from live Dataverse data and that export, not a semantic model. | User direction 2026-08-27. Removing SFTP drops the DLP, connection and authentication approvals from the critical path, and removing Power BI drops the audience and RLS design; neither is required for the MVP outcome-testing flow. The `/reports` screen stays a `NotBuiltYet` placeholder describing the in-app MI, and the export path remains the manual Trail Light producer (OD-004 still owns its template). Re-scoping either is additive later. | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-035 | All event notifications are delivered by email through the Power Automate Office 365 Outlook connector. Teams is not a notification channel. | Resolves OD-009 (user direction 2026-08-27). A single channel keeps the flow templates uniform: every notification event (allocation, submission, remediation assignment, sign-off outcome, rejection) is an email built from a Dataverse-driven template, with the recipient resolved from the case/assignment owner or role. No Teams connector, DLP change or graph permission is introduced. Recipient addresses come from Dataverse user records, never hardcoded (AGENTS.md rule 7). | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-036 | Resolving OD-008, the case status model adds one bypass value, `No Check Required` (`al_casestatus` 120910592), for cases that must not receive a grading outcome. Tax wrong-route is modelled as a manager-controlled reassignment between Tax and AQS carrying a mandatory reason, not a status; the reassignment preserves prior `al_CaseAssignment` rows and the AQS/Tax `al_ReviewInstance`. Replacement cases reuse the AD-029 Previous/Replacement self-relationship and the AD-032 initial/final `al_Outcome`; a superseded case stays `Closed`. | Adam Strumidlo required an explicit bypass state so cases are not forced into a grade; a single terminal `No Check Required` value satisfies that without inventing a Tax-specific sub-lifecycle. Reassignment rather than a terminal wrong-route status matches the agreed manager-controlled transfer between Tax and AQS with audit retention (BR-003, BR-012); the schema half (assignment history + reason) already exists, so only the server-side reassignment command/flow (AD-003) remains to build, and it stays deferred here to keep this a schema-only slice. No replacement-case status is added because initial/final outcome preservation is already carried by `al_Outcome` (BR-007). | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-037 | Resolving OD-010, case data and audit data are retained indefinitely; hard deletion is not permitted. Data-subject erasure/rectification is satisfied by anonymising personal fields in place, preserving the immutable audit record and the initial/final outcome history. | Audit-trail integrity and traceability are stated core requirements, which forbids destructive deletion (BR-012, NFR-AUD-01). Anonymisation-in-place is the only erasure approach that reconciles a data-subject request with an immutable, indefinitely retained audit trail. The specific anonymisation field list and legal basis are an implementation detail for the erasure command and do not gate the schema or flows. | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-038 | Resolving OD-017, Checker Checklist V8 is the confirmed latest and official version. Its effective-from date is `2026-08-26`, the adoption date already seeded (AD-015), because no separate effective date accompanied the document. | The value is now a confirmed effective date rather than a placeholder, so no seed re-run is required. As a new build there are no earlier-dated cases, so effective-to stays open and question-version history begins at V8. | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-039 | Resolving OD-004, the Trail Light export is one row per case with 20 fixed columns in this order: 1 Adviser name (TEXT), 2 Adviser Code (NUMBER), 3 Paraplanner Name (TEXT), 4 Paraplanner Code (NUMBER), 5 Case type (TEXT), 6 Product/solution type (TEXT), 7 Check date (DATE), 8 Client name (TEXT), 9 Pre or post check (TEXT), 10 File Quality Grade (TEXT, Pass/Fail), 11 File Quality Fail Accountability Name — adviser (TEXT), 12 File Quality Fail Accountability Code — adviser (NUMBER), 13 File Quality Fail Accountability Name — paraplanner (TEXT), 14 File Quality Fail Accountability Code — paraplanner (NUMBER), 15 Advice Quality Grade (TEXT, BR-005 Pass/Pass with issues/Insufficient evidence/Potential harm), 16 blank separator, 17 Advice Quality Fail Accountability Name — adviser (TEXT), 18 Advice Quality Fail Accountability Code — adviser (NUMBER), 19 Advice Quality Fail Accountability Name — paraplanner (TEXT), 20 Advice Quality Fail Accountability Code — paraplanner (NUMBER). | Source of truth is `Trailight - Outcome Testing Map.xlsx` (header row, example row and per-column type row). Each fail section carries two accountability pairs so a File Quality or Advice Quality fail can be attributed to the adviser and/or the paraplanner by name and code. The map contains no Tax column, so the Trail Light export covers File Quality and Advice Quality only; a Tax export is not in this contract and must not be invented. `Export Record` maps one Outcome Case to this row; `Export Batch` controls one manual, on-demand production (AD-034). Column 16 is an intentional blank separator in the supplied template and is preserved so downstream column positions match. | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-040 | Resolving OD-002, allocation is a single shared queue per review team: a case sits in the queue and a manager manually assigns it to a named member. No skills routing, no auto-allocation, no load balancing; Tax and AQS use the same model. The deferred named assigned-user lookup on `al_CaseAssignment` (AD-029) is therefore unblocked and becomes the manual assignment target; `al_assignedteam` stays as the queue name. The manager-only assign and reassign are server-side commands (AD-003) that write a new `al_CaseAssignment` row and preserve prior rows (BR-003, BR-012). **Amended 2026-08-31:** the assigning actors are **team leads and managers**. Each review team (AQS and Tax) has a team lead, and allocation is done from the PowerApps code app, not the portal. | The shared-queue-plus-manual-assign model is the simplest that satisfies BR-003 and needs no skills reference table or allocation engine. A named `systemuser` lookup captures who the manager assigned; keeping the assignment as a new history row rather than an in-place edit preserves the allocation trail and lets the AD-036 Tax↔AQS reassignment reuse the same mechanism with a reason. AQS sharing the model means one command path serves both teams. The lookup and the assign/reassign commands are a build slice, not yet authored here. **The allocation capability does not exist yet.** Verified 2026-08-31: `al_CaseAssignment` carries `al_assignedteam`, `al_assignedon`, `al_releasedon`, `al_isactive` and `al_assignmentreason` but **no assigned-user lookup** — AD-029 deferred it and this decision declared it unblocked, but it was never added. There is no assign or reassign Custom API among the 15 registered, no plug-in writes `al_caseassignment`, the code app has no assignment surface, and DEV holds zero rows. This is the missing link in the BR-004 Tax-then-AQS route: a case parks at `Queued` and nothing supported moves it to `Assigned`, which is why the plan's verification scenario 2 had to patch `al_casestatus` directly. Tracked as OD-029. | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-041 | In-app permission administration is added as an application RBAC layer that **records** access intent in Dataverse and is **enforced server-side**, never UI-only. Two user-owned tables: `al_UserRoleMapping` (one work-email → app-role assignment, AD-010 canonical key) and `al_PagePermission` (one app-role → resource-key → access-level rule). App roles reuse the AD-020/AD-031 vocabulary (Tax Checker, AQS Checker, Adviser, T&C Manager, Outcome Testing Manager, Administrator). Resource keys cover navigable pages (`page.cases`, `page.reports`, `page.exports`, `page.admin.questions`, `page.admin.security`, …) and capabilities (`command.regrade`, `command.signoff`, `command.assign`, `remediation.complete`, `question.retire`, `export.generate`). Access levels are None/View/Edit/Manage. Writes to these tables and every gated capability run as server-side Custom API commands (AD-003) that verify the caller against Dataverse privileges + the mapping and write an immutable Audit Event (BR-012, NFR-AUD-01). The React app reads the effective permission set for the current user to **show/hide and enable/disable** UI only; it is never the security boundary. | User direction 2026-08-27: managers must assign pages and edit/remediation rights in the app. The architecture invariant (copilot-instructions.md / AGENTS.md) forbids security that lives only in UI code; this design honours it because the app layer is advisory and the actual gate is the server-side command checking Dataverse privileges plus the mapping. Reusing the existing role vocabulary avoids inventing roles (rule 4). Modelling permissions as data (role × resource × level) rather than code lets an Administrator change access without a deployment, which is the "manage in-app" requirement, while the immutable Audit Event preserves the who/when/why. This supersedes the earlier note that making Security configuration editable needed a fresh OD: the decision is now recorded here under delegated authority, isolated to two tables, their commands and the client permission reader, so it can be revised in one place. Option value blocks 1209107: app-role 60–65, access-level 66–69, export-batch status 70–72, new audit commands (AssignUserRole 73, SetPagePermission 74, CreateExportBatch 75, GenerateExport 76, RetireQuestion 77). | 2026-08-27 **Amended 2026-09-09:** withdrawing a permission rule takes the access away on both tiers. `PermissionHelpers.MaxLevel` has always resolved from active `al_pagepermission` rows alone; the client overlaid stored rules on `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS`, so a withdrawn rule fell back to a coded default the server never had and the app offered pages and commands the gate then refused. The client now resolves from the stored rules alone once any exist (`rulesInForce`); the seed matrix applies only to an environment with no stored rule, which is the bootstrap the first administrator needs. To deny explicitly while keeping the row visible, set the level to None. |
| AD-042 | Resolving OD-004/AD-039 into build, the Trail Light export is added as two user-owned tables: `al_ExportBatch` (one manual, on-demand production run — `al_exportbatchcode` alt key, `al_batchstatus` Draft/Generated/Delivered, `al_generatedon`, `al_rowcount`) and `al_ExportRecord` (one row per exported case holding the AD-039 20-column contract as typed columns, `al_exportrecordcode` alt key, `al_outcomecaseid` lookup, `al_exportbatchid` lookup). Generation is a server-side command `al_CreateExportBatch`/`al_GenerateExport` (AD-003, AD-034 manual only, no SFTP) that snapshots each in-scope Outcome Case into an Export Record and writes an Audit Event. | AD-039 fixed the 20-column contract and AD-034 fixed manual-only production, so the export tables were unblocked and only needed authoring. Snapshotting into Export Records (rather than computing the file live) preserves exactly what was delivered for reconciliation and audit (BR-012). Restrict delete on both relationships keeps the export history under its batch. | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-043 | In-app case management (UAT feedback, 2026-08-27) is added as server-side commands (AD-003), not client record edits. The first command `al_UpdateCaseDetails` lets a manager amend the editable case header — status, review route (the AD-036 Tax↔AQS reassignment), priority and due date — with a mandatory reason. Two case-header fields are added to `al_OutcomeCase`: `al_priority` (Low/Normal/High/Urgent, values 120910780–783) and `al_duedate` (date). Authorization reuses the AD-041 model: the caller must hold Edit on `page.cases`; the write runs as the initiating user so Dataverse privilege remains the platform gate. The command enforces optimistic concurrency and idempotency and writes an immutable Audit Event (command value 120910778) recording the before/after of each changed field (BR-012, NFR-AUD-01). The manager assign/reassign-to-named-user command (`al_AssignCase`, the AD-040 `systemuser` assignment target) is the next slice and is not yet built. | Testing found the worklist was view-only; managers must maintain cases after creation. Modelling the edits as an audited server-side command honours the invariant that security is enforced in Dataverse, never only in UI (AGENTS.md), and keeps the full who/when/before/after trail. Priority and due date were absent from the schema and are justified by direct product-owner feedback in this UAT round; status and route editing reuse the settled AD-036 manager-controlled transition. The client `CaseEditPanel` only shows/enables when the user holds `page.cases` Edit and is advisory; the Custom API is the authoritative gate. | 2026-08-27 |
| AD-045 | RBAC configuration becomes editable, not append-only (UAT feedback, 2026-08-28), via three server-side commands (AD-003): `al_SetRoleAssignmentActive` (audit 120910788), `al_SetPermissionRuleActive` (audit 120910789) and `al_UpdateRole` (audit 120910790). All three require `permission.manage` Manage and write an immutable Audit Event. Edits are **deactivations, never deletions** (AD-037/OD-010): the withdrawn row and its history survive. Changing a person's role is grant-then-withdraw, because `al_userrolemappingcode` embeds the role. `al_rolecode` is never editable — it is the key `al_userrolemapping` and `al_pagepermission` reference, so renaming a role must not orphan them. Retiring a role **cascades**: `al_UpdateRole` also deactivates the assignments and permission rules holding that code, because neither `PermissionProvider` nor `PermissionHelpers` consults the role registry when resolving access, so retiring the `al_role` row alone would look like a revocation while still granting everything. `al_pagepermission` level changes already upsert on (role, resource) and need no new command. | Administrators could create assignments, rules and roles but never amend or withdraw them, so a mis-assignment was permanent and the tables only grew. Deactivation preserves the audit trail the regulator needs while making the configuration correctable. The cascade closes a real privilege-escalation gap that would otherwise have been introduced by a retire action that did nothing. | 2026-08-28 |
| AD-046 | Idempotency keys are held per user **intent**, not per attempt. `useIntentKeys` (`app/src/hooks/useIntentKey.ts`) caches one key per intent token in a ref and releases it only on confirmed success; the per-command `new*IntentKey()` factories were removed so the per-attempt pattern cannot return. `/cases/:caseId/remediation` is gated by `RequirePermission resource="page.remediation"`, and the oversight roles (Outcome Testing Manager, Administrator) gain `page.remediation` View so the new gate does not silently withdraw access they had while the route was ungated. `al_UpdateUser` now receives `ExpectedRowVersion` from the Users page. | Every call site minted a fresh GUID inside the submit handler, so the plug-ins' `FindAuditByKey` dedupe could never fire: a retry after a timeout that had actually succeeded server-side wrote a second time. The remediation route was the only content route with no permission gate. Both were found by the 2026-08-28 CRUD audit. | 2026-08-28 |
| AD-047 | Case history is surfaced as a **History** tab on case detail (FR-033), reading `al_auditevent` for the case plus its checks, remediation actions, outcomes and sign-offs. Read-only: Audit Events are immutable (BR-012, NFR-AUD-01), so no edit or delete affordance exists. Read access is governed by Dataverse privilege on `al_auditevent`; a caller without it sees an explicit unavailable state rather than a misleadingly empty trail. The former `/cases/:caseId/audit` placeholder redirects to the case, which now owns the trail. | The command trail existed but nothing read it, so "who changed what, and when" was unanswerable in the app. Joining the related records' events gives the whole case story in one place rather than only the events that happened to target the case row. | 2026-08-28 |

## Approved exceptions to the no-hardcoded-identifiers rule
`AGENTS.md` rule 7 forbids hardcoding tenant or environment identifiers. AD-007 grants one narrow exception.

| Item | File | Why it is allowed | Boundaries |
|---|---|---|---|
| `environmentId` | `app/power.config.json` | Written by `pa app init`; the CLI has no environment-variable indirection for it | DEV identifier only. TEST and PROD values must be supplied at deployment time, never committed |
| `appId` | `app/power.config.json` | Written by `pa app push`; identifies the published app for subsequent pushes | Same as above |

Boundaries that still apply:
- Neither value is a secret, but both are environment-specific, so `power.config.json` must not be copied between environments.
- No other file may hardcode an environment ID, tenant ID, org URL, connection ID or group ID.
- Runtime configuration such as URLs, queue identifiers and export locations continues to use Dataverse environment variables, not this file.
- The DEV values currently recorded are environment `d50d27e8-cb3b-e718-b6e2-30aa92d944aa` (Env_AQ_Dev), solution `c7f447ab-35a1-f111-b8dd-e4fade069307` (OutcomeTesting) and app `5d9fc475-ee75-4386-917e-fc182307b0c2`.
- Revisit this exception if the CLI later supports token substitution for `power.config.json`.
| AD-044 | **Supersedes AD-002.** Power Pages is reinstated as an operational front end. The solution runs two front ends over one Dataverse dataset, split by persona rather than by feature: the Code App serves managers and administrators (oversight, allocation, imports, question administration, security administration, audit, MI and export); the Power Pages portal serves Tax checkers, AQS checkers, advisers and T&C Managers, and is where checks and remediation are performed. Existing Code App screens for cases, dashboard and reviews are re-framed as management oversight views, not retired. Business logic stays in shared server-side commands (AD-003) and is never duplicated per front end. | The signed scope `OPP-41186` item 13, "Power Pages access for operational teams", is explicit approval and postdates the assumption AD-002 rested on. Confirmed by the product owner 2026-08-29. A persona split avoids two UIs competing over the same workflow while keeping one system of record. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-045 | The whole remediation loop runs in the portal: the adviser responds and the T&C Manager attests, both in Power Pages. | Keeps the BR-006/BR-008 loop and its audit trail in one place rather than splitting one workflow across two platforms. Consequence: portal Contacts are required for every adviser and para-planner, not only the check teams, which is the volume OD-011 licensing had to clear. Confirmed 2026-08-29. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-046 | No evidence is uploaded to or stored by the portal. Advisers reference existing Intelligent Office documents, and PP-14 reduces to a reference field. | Preserves the standing data principle that documents remain in Intelligent Office and are referenced, not stored. Removes SharePoint or Dataverse file storage, MIME and extension validation, size limits, file retention and the prohibited-file-type negative test from scope. Closes the evidence-repository item raised in the Power Pages requirements pack section 26. Confirmed 2026-08-29. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-047 | Portal identity is the Dataverse `Contact`, provisioned by Entra group sync, joined to the existing application identity on **work email** (OD-003, AD-010; `al_User.al_workemail`, `al_UserRoleMapping.al_useremail`). `systemuser` remains the owner of every operational row so Code App views are unaffected. Portal access is granted by Contact-scoped table permissions over three new lookups — `al_ReviewInstance.al_assignedcontactid`, `al_RemediationAction.al_assignedcontactid`, and a case-access mechanic pending OD-022 — with `al_Response` and `al_Outcome` reached as child permissions of the review. Global read is granted only to `al_ChecklistVersion`, `al_Section`, `al_QuestionVersion` and `al_FailReason`. No portal role gets Global access to case, review, response, outcome, remediation or audit data; the Anonymous role gets no table permission. | Power Pages table permissions resolve through Contact and Account relationships and cannot read `owninguser`, so the user-owned schema (AD-024) is unreachable from the portal as built. Contact lookups on the work records enforce the FR/PP separation of Tax and AQS structurally — each review instance carries its own assigned contact — rather than by UI convention, which NFR-SEC-01 forbids. Verified against Microsoft Learn `power-pages/security/table-permissions` (updated 2026-07-07): access types are Global, Contact, Account, Self and Custom; Parent is implemented as child permissions running parent-to-child only; polymorphic lookups are unsupported in those chains. Custom access (FetchXML) is excluded while it remains preview. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-048 | Power Pages metadata lives in `powerpages/` at the repository root, a sibling of `app/` and `brand/`, and never under `src/`. Portal styling consumes the existing `brand/` tokens rather than defining a second colour system. | `src/` is the unpacked Dataverse solution root and is packaged in full, so portal metadata or design resources placed there would ship inside the managed solution. `app/` and `brand/` already sit outside `src/` for this reason. Supersedes the layout proposed in the Power Pages requirements pack section 12, which placed both under `src/`. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-049 | **Supersedes AD-009 for the portal.** Roundo is the typeface, used for both headings and body, served from Fontshare in Light 300, Regular 400, Medium 500 and SemiBold 600. The stock Power Pages studio palette is rebranded by redefining `--portalThemeColor1` to `--portalThemeColor9` to Ascot Lloyd values rather than overriding each component. | The `brand/` typography sheet names Roundo as the single primary font — "for clarity we've selected just one primary font" — and gives Fontshare as the download source. OD-015 retired Roundo on the understanding it required licensing; the brand sheet's own source is free for commercial use, so that premise was wrong. Confirmed by the product owner 2026-08-29. `portalbasictheme.css` drives the stock chrome from `--portalThemeColorN` custom properties, so redefining those rebrands the navbar, footer, buttons and every `data-component-theme` component at once, instead of chasing each with a more specific selector. Both the Fontshare stylesheet and the Contentful logo are external references; self-hosting both as web files would remove the dependency and any CSP exposure, and is not yet done. The Code App still uses Outfit and Inter under AD-009; aligning it is a separate change. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-050 | Implementing the AD-047 identity model, `al_ReviewInstance` and `al_RemediationAction` each gain an `al_assignedcontactid` lookup to Contact, with relationships `contact_al_reviewinstance` and `contact_al_remediationaction`, both `RemoveLink` on delete. Imported and published to Env_AQ_Dev on 2026-08-29 and confirmed by solution export. `systemuser` ownership is unchanged. The equivalent mechanic for `al_OutcomeCase` is deliberately **not** built and remains OD-022. | Power Pages table permissions resolve through Contact relationships and cannot read `ownerid`, so the user-owned schema (AD-024) is unreachable from the portal without these. Putting the lookup on the work records rather than the case enforces the Tax/AQS separation structurally: each review instance carries its own assigned contact, so a Tax checker's Contact-scoped access cannot reach the AQS instance on the same case (PP-08). `RemoveLink` rather than `Restrict` because deleting a portal contact must never remove review or remediation history (BR-012). The case needs to be readable by the assigned checker, the adviser and the T&C Manager at once, which a single lookup cannot express, so it waits on OD-022 rather than being guessed. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-051 | **Corrected 2026-08-29, superseding the original wording below.** Custom APIs **are** carried by `pac solution pack`: they pack into a top-level `customapis/` folder in the solution zip, not into `customizations.xml`. A solution import therefore creates the API, its request parameters and its response properties. What the import does **not** supply is the plug-in binding, unless `customapi.xml` carries a `<plugintypeid><plugintypeexportkey>`. An API imported without it exists, accepts calls, and executes nothing. Both paths are needed: the solution import for the definition, and `Register-<Command>.ps1` for the assembly push, the plug-in type row and the binding. Every `customapi.xml` must carry its `plugintypeexportkey`. | The original entry claimed custom APIs were not packed at all. That was wrong, and wrong for an avoidable reason: the check grepped only `customizations.xml`, which is not where they live. Listing the zip's full contents showed all thirteen APIs under `customapis/`. The practical consequence was real — `al_SubmitReview` was created by the import and sat unbound, and the registration script's idempotent "exists" check then skipped it, so the command looked deployed while executing nothing. The script now asserts the binding rather than inferring it from existence. Kept rather than deleted because the failure mode it describes is silent and worth a standing warning. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-052 | `pac plugin push` refuses to update an assembly while the environment holds a registered plug-in type the new assembly does not contain, so a single orphan blocks every plug-in update for the whole solution. Three orphans existed in Env_AQ_Dev — `SetPermissionRuleActivePlugin`/`al_SetPermissionRuleActive`, `SetRoleAssignmentActivePlugin`/`al_SetRoleAssignmentActive` and `UpdateRolePlugin`/`al_UpdateRole` — none with source in the repository. All three were removed on 2026-08-29 using `plugins/deploy/Remove-OrphanedPluginTypes.ps1`. `pac plugin push` also does not create plug-in type rows for classes the environment has not seen, so a new command must have its type registered explicitly. | The three orphans had no source file, no git history and no reference anywhere in the repository; the Code App invokes eleven custom APIs, all string literals, and none was among them. They were prototypes deployed from a working copy that never reached version control — a traceability gap against BR-012 and NFR-AUD-01 worth addressing at process level, since deployed commands with no source cannot be reviewed, tested or rebuilt. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-053 | Portal answers are written directly to `al_response` over the Portals Web API rather than through a command and cloud flow. A synchronous pre-operation `ResponseGuardPlugin` enforces the PP-11 submission lock, the AD-023 typed-column match, the permitted option subset and AD-020 section ownership, and stamps `al_responsecode` so `al_ResponseCodeKey` makes a replayed create collide. A post-operation `ResponseProgressPlugin` advances Assigned to Review In Progress. Authorization is Contact-scoped table permissions (AD-047), never plug-in identity. | The Portals Web API supports create, update and associate but explicitly not actions, so answering needs no flow while submission does; a flow per answer would also spend a Power Automate run on every debounced edit. Putting the rules in a plug-in rather than the page keeps them unbypassable by a hand-edited URL or a direct PATCH (NFR-SEC-01). Authorization is excluded from the plug-in deliberately: Power Pages Web API writes arrive under the site's application user, so `InitiatingUserId` is not the checker and a check against it would look like security while enforcing nothing. Decision logic lives in a dependency-free `ResponseRules` class so it is unit-testable without a Dataverse fake, which is what let this land with the repository's first plug-in tests. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-055 | `al_QuestionVersion` carries no per-question option list, so a `Single select` question's options are fixed by its response type. Q-TAX-02 (Tax check outcome) is retyped from `Single select` to `Pass / Fail / Insufficient evidence` in `data/v8-seed`, leaving `Single select` used only by Q-GR-02 (Primary root cause) and therefore unambiguous. | Two seeded questions used `Single select` with different vocabularies, and nothing in the schema distinguished them, so a portal control could not know which options to render. The resolution is read from the questions rather than invented: Q-TAX-02's own options in `knowledge/checklist-v8.md` are PASS, INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE, FAIL, which is precisely the existing `Pass / Fail / Insufficient evidence` scale; the "SingleSelect" label described cardinality, not a vocabulary. Retyping avoids adding an option-list column that AD-022 deliberately excluded. This is a correction to unpublished DEV seed data re-imported under AD-027, not an amendment to a version any review has been issued, so BR-013 and PP-09 immutability are not engaged. A future question needing its own list would need the schema change AD-022 declined. **Amended 2026-09-13 — Q-TAX-02's middle option is reworded to "Pass with issues".** Project owner direction. Wording and nothing else: the response type stays `PassFailInsufficient`, the stored value stays `120910302`, `ResponseRules.PermittedChoices` is untouched, and `OutcomeRules.TaxResultRequiresRemediation` still sends that value to remediation — so no case changes meaning and no existing row needs migrating. The rename cannot sit on the scale, because `PassFailInsufficient` is shared with the suitability grid (S-E1 to S-E5, S-CRP), which still reads 120910302 as Insufficient evidence; renaming the option would have relabelled six AQS sections nobody asked to change. It is applied instead at the Tax-scoped label surfaces only: the `al_outcomecase.al_taxoutcome` local option set, the inline rendering path in `checklistForm.ts` and `OT Answer Options` (Q-TAX-02 is the only question either draws inline), and the Tax badge on `OT Case List`. **Extended 2026-09-13:** a grid also heads 120910302 "Pass with issues" on a Tax review, through `optionsFor(scale, isTaxReview)` and the matching branch in `OT Review Detail`. That reaches only a section added through checklist administration (AD-123) whose questions all share `120910006` - Q-TAX-02 itself is drawn inline - and is keyed on the review's discipline rather than the section's owner role, because a Both-owned section answered on a Tax review is a tax check and a Tax-owned section never appears on an AQS review. Without it a Tax section added later would have silently kept the old wording. `SubmitReviewPlugin` now reads the remediation reason's label from `al_taxoutcome` rather than from the shared `al_response.al_answerchoice`, so the adviser reads the wording the checker ticked. This does contradict the source Checker Checklist, which still reads INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE: the reference document is left as supplied rather than rewritten, and `checklistDocument.test.ts` carries the divergence as its third deliberate difference. Out of scope and deliberately untouched: `al_iooutcome`, the Intelligent Office task outcome the case import carries in, which is a separate four-value set whose text is an inbound contract with IO and which already has its own `Pass with issues` at 120910611. | 2026-08-29, amended 2026-09-13 |
| AD-054 | **Superseded by AD-096 (2026-09-09).** The fail-reason picker appears on any non-pass answer — Fail, Insufficient evidence or Potential harm — not only on the file quality outcome question. | The V8 document files the 20 seed reasons under "File Quality fail points", but their categories span AML, Breach, Record Keeping and Tax check, which is wider than any one question; read literally, the AML, Breach and Tax-check rows would be unreachable seed data. Attaching on any non-pass follows BR-006, under which every non-pass outcome requires remediation and therefore needs a recorded reason for the adviser to work from, and FR-013, which permits several reasons on one answer. Confirmed by the product owner 2026-08-29. Recorded rather than assumed because the source document does not state the trigger. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-056 | **Supersedes the AD-047 clause "No portal role gets Global access to case, review, response, outcome, remediation or audit data" for the two reviewer roles only.** Tax and AQS Reviewers get Global **read** on `al_OutcomeCase`, `al_ReviewInstance` and `al_Response`, and **write only on reviews assigned to them**, expressed as a Contact-scoped `al_ReviewInstance` permission with `al_Response` as its child carrying create and write. Power Pages unions table permissions, so the union is read-everything, write-own. Advisers, T&C Managers and every other portal role are unchanged and remain out of scope. | Product owner direction 2026-08-29: reviewers must be able to see all cases and change only the ones assigned to them. The previous Contact-scoped read model made a reviewer's worklist show only their own assignments, which prevents the PP-04 case search and PP-17 drill-down from being usable and would have blanked the portal wherever `al_assignedcontactid` is unpopulated. The edit boundary is unchanged and still structural: write is reachable only through the Contact-anchored parent chain, so PP-08's no-cross-discipline-overwrite and the "cannot alter another reviewer's response" negative test both still hold. Consequence to accept, and it is a real one: a reviewer can now read every client name, adviser name and vulnerability flag in the system, so the portal design's negative test "a user cannot view an unassigned case by editing the URL" is **deliberately retired for reviewer roles** and must not be reported as a regression. Restricting that again is a permission change in one place. | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-057 | The canonical case lifecycle is enforced as a transition table on both tiers: `CaseLifecycle` in the plug-in assembly, gating every `al_casestatus` write in `al_UpdateCaseDetails`, and `CASE_STATUS_TRANSITIONS` in `app/src/types/domain.ts`, which restricts the status control to what the command would accept. Closed and No Check Required are terminal; `No Check Required` is reachable only up to Review In Progress; a rejected sign-off returns Awaiting Sign-off to Awaiting Remediation; a corrected Validation Failed returns to Ready for Allocation or closes. **Amended 2026-08-30:** `Assigned` and `Review In Progress` also allow `Queued` — the Tax-to-AQS handoff, where a two-discipline case returns to the shared queue after one discipline finishes and another is still required. | Closes code audit finding I8. `al_casestatus` was written straight from caller input, so a case could jump from Imported to Closed — skipping validation, allocation, review and remediation — and then be collected by the export, which filters on Closed, and delivered with no review instance and a blank grade. The table encodes only transitions the requirements describe: the spine is the canonical lifecycle in `project-context.md`, the Validation Failed exits are BR-002 with AD-029 replacement linking, the sign-off return is BR-008, the bypass bound is AD-036's own wording ("cases that must not receive a grading outcome" — past Submitted one exists), and terminality is OD-007/AD-031, which makes reopening a privileged correction rather than a details edit. A path the requirements do not describe is refused rather than guessed at; adding one is a decision recorded here, not a wider table. That is what happens here: the outcome-creation design (spec §2.2) needs BR-004's sequential Tax-then-AQS route, which this table was written before and did not cover. The added edge is a handoff between disciplines, not a backwards step — allocation is manual (BR-003, AD-040), so the case must go back to the shared queue for a manager to assign the AQS checker rather than continuing on to a named person. | 2026-08-29 **Amended 2026-09-09 (OD-038):** `Awaiting Sign-off` also allows `Queued`, on both tiers — an approved remediation on a case whose route still owes an AQS review returns it to the shared queue for that check. |
| AD-058 | Deactivating an application user (OD-010) withdraws their application permissions. `PermissionHelpers` refuses a caller whose `al_user` row is present and inactive, and the client resolver mirrors it; the role mappings are left in place as history, so reactivation restores the person to the roles they held. A caller with no `al_user` row at all is unaffected. `al_CreateUser` refuses a work email belonging to a deactivated user rather than upserting it back to active. | Closes code audit findings I4 and I12. Deactivation is the sanctioned alternative to deleting a leaver, so it has to be what actually removes access — previously it flipped `al_isactive` and nothing read it, leaving a departed T&C Manager able to overwrite a final outcome for as long as their Dataverse account existed. Deciding on the registry row rather than deleting mappings keeps deactivation reversible and keeps the assignment history intact for audit. Staying permissive when no `al_user` row exists is deliberate: `al_user` is an application registry a Dataverse user can legitimately predate, and refusing there would lock out anyone it has not caught up with. Registration was the other half of the same hole: upserting on work email reactivated a leaver under a new display name, audited only as "Created". | 2026-08-29 |
| AD-059 | Power Pages component record ids are hand-minted in the `a1000000-0000-4000-8000-0000000000NN` space, banded by component type, and `powerpages/Check-ComponentIds.ps1` must pass before every `pac pages upload`. Bands in use: `10-1d` web templates, `20-2a` page templates, `30-36`/`40-46` web pages, `50` web files, `60-75` table permissions, `70`/`80-88` web links. | In the Enhanced data model every component is a row in the single `powerpagecomponent` table, so two components minted with the same id are one row and an upload silently replaces one with the other. The loser is not reported; it stops existing, and records pointing at it resolve to a component of the wrong type, which the portal answers with a generic error page and an error id. This happened twice undetected: `OT Answer Options` (web template, ...0021) destroyed `OT Case List Page` (page template), taking `/cases` down, and four table permissions (...0071-0074) destroyed the `Cases`, `Tax reviews`, `AQS reviews` and `Remediation` primary-navigation links. Both were repaired by re-minting the destroyed component — `OT Case List Page` to ...002a, the links to ...0085-0088 — leaving the component DEV already held in place, because changing an existing row's component type is not a supported upload. The check is a script rather than a convention because the failure is silent at author time and only visible as an outage. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-060 | A Power Pages web file is two records — the `adx_webfile` and the annotation holding its bytes — and the annotation must carry its own id. `objectid` equals `adx_webfileid` because it points the note at the file; `annotationid` must not. `powerpages/Check-ComponentIds.ps1` enforces this and also fails a web file with no `adx_partialurl`. | `outcome-testing.css` was authored with `annotationid` equal to `adx_webfileid` (both `a1000000-...-050`), the only web file in the site where the two matched. The upload wrote the note and dropped the file's own metadata, leaving a component that held the CSS bytes but had no `adx_partialurl`, `adx_parentpageid` or publishing state. `/outcome-testing.css` answered 404 while every other web file answered 302, so every portal page rendered unstyled. Repointing `annotationid` at the note DEV actually held restored the full record, and the URL now behaves exactly like the known-good files. The check is in the same script as AD-059 because both are silent at author time and only visible as a broken site. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-061 | `data/plugin-register/` is deleted. Plug-in assemblies are deployed with `pac plugin push` (and promoted inside the solution), never through a `pac data import` package. | Closes code audit finding C8. The package upserted `pluginassembly` matched on name, at an unchanged version and public key token, carrying a 15,360-byte blob holding one plug-in type. Dataverse accepts that as an in-place replacement, so importing it would have left every other command pointing at a type absent from the assembly — regrade, sign-off, role assignment and case edits all failing at runtime with no schema change to explain it. The live assembly is now 75,776 bytes across 16 types, so the package had become more dangerous, not less. Nothing referenced it, and the blob also embedded a developer's absolute path. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-062 | **Extends AD-061 to the solution package.** The schema solution is packed from a copy of `src/` with `PluginAssemblies/` removed; the plug-in assembly reaches an environment only through `pac plugin push`. `docs/deployment/` carries the warning at the step that packs. | AD-061 closed the `pac data import` route into `pluginassembly` but left the same hole open one level up. `src/PluginAssemblies/` holds a committed DLL and `solution.xml` carries it as `RootComponent type="91" behavior="0"`, so `pac solution pack --folder src` puts it in the zip and an import replaces the live assembly with whatever was last committed, at the same version and public key token — exactly the in-place replacement AD-061 describes. Caught during the 2026-08-30 DEV deployment before the import ran: the committed DLL was 47,104 bytes with 11 plug-in types while DEV was running 16, so the import would have removed `SubmitReviewPlugin`, `ResponseGuardPlugin`, `ResponseProgressPlugin`, `SetUserActivePlugin` and `UpdateUserPlugin` from the live assembly, breaking five commands at runtime with no schema change to explain it. The schema-only import was verified to leave the assembly's type count and modified timestamp untouched. The durable fix is removing `src/PluginAssemblies/` entirely, which is the remaining half of OD-026. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-063 | Plug-in type creation and Custom API creation for any command are done by `plugins/deploy/Register-CustomApiFromContract.ps1`, driven by the command's `*.customapi.json` contract. It does not push the assembly. | Every field the command needs already lives in the contract, so a per-command registration script duplicates the contract in PowerShell and drifts from it. Making the script generic means a new command needs a contract, not a script. Pushing the assembly is deliberately excluded: it is an ordered deployment step of its own, and folding it in would let a caller register a command against an assembly older than the contract it was built from. Verified idempotent against DEV 2026-08-30 by running it twice over `al_SetFailAccountability`; the second run reported `exists:` for all ten components. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-064 | A Configuration Migration package writes a Boolean field as `value="True"` / `value="False"`. `1` and `0` are accepted by the importer and silently land as **False**. | Found on 2026-08-30 running the plan's verification scenario 2 against DEV. `data/route-seed/data.xml` used `1`/`0`, so all three `al_reviewroute` rows imported with `al_requirestaxreview` and `al_requiresaqsreview` both False, while the string and number fields on the same rows imported correctly. The import reported "3 of 3" and the row count check in the deployment runbook passed, so nothing distinguished it from success — but every route branch stayed inert, which is the exact condition the seed exists to remove. A Tax submit on `ROUTE-TAX-AQS` closed the case instead of queuing it for AQS, because `AqsStillToCome` reads the route flag first. `data/v8-seed` already used `True`/`False`, which is why its `al_ismandatory` flags were correct; route-seed had diverged from the convention rather than the convention being absent. Verifying a seed by row count alone does not show this — check the values the branch actually reads. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-065 | `al_response.al_answerchoices` is a multi-select choice column and is read as `OptionSetValueCollection`, never as `string`. | `SubmitReviewPlugin.HasAnswer` read it with `GetAttributeValue<string>`, which throws `InvalidCastException` the moment the column actually holds a value. The mandatory-question check calls it for every response on the review, so any review carrying an answered multi-select question could not be submitted at all — the cast threw before a single business rule ran, and the caller saw a type error rather than anything about their review. In the V8 checklist that is **every Tax review**, because `Q-TAX-01` is mandatory and multi-select. `ResponseGuardPlugin` had always read the column at its real type, so the two plug-ins disagreed about the schema and only the guard was right. Found on 2026-08-30 by running the plan's verification scenario 1 against DEV; unit tests had not caught it because `HasAnswer` was private and every test built responses with single choices. It is now public and covered by `SubmitReviewAnswerTests`. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-066 | Plug-in code reachable only through `IOrganizationService` is tested against `FakeOrganizationService` in the test project — an in-memory service supporting the queries the plug-ins actually issue (equality/inequality conditions, link-entity chains, `TopCount`, `Orders`). It throws `NotSupportedException` on anything it does not implement rather than returning a partial result. | The suite was 119 pure-logic tests because `OutcomeRules`, `ResponseRules` and `CaseLifecycle` are deliberately free of Dataverse types, and everything behind a service call was therefore untested. AD-065 is what that cost: `SubmitReviewPlugin` read a multi-select column as a string and made every Tax review unsubmittable, and no unit test could see it because the read sat behind `RetrieveMultiple`. Throwing on unsupported operators is the important half of the design — a fake that silently ignores a `ConditionOperator` it does not know would match the wrong rows and assert green against logic that is wrong in production, which is worse than having no fake. First use is `CaseTransitions`, now the single AD-057 enforcement point for both `al_SubmitReview` and `al_UpdateCaseDetails`. The private query helpers inside `SubmitReviewPlugin` and `GenerateExportPlugin` remain uncovered; reaching them means changing their visibility or extracting them, which is sub-project 2's call, not a gap this decision closes. | 2026-08-30 |
| AD-067 | Portal table permissions are bound to the built-in **Administrators** (`c53b2908-1fc1-4470-89cd-6f5b95c17ffe`) and **Authenticated Users** (`e24b50c5-1443-4725-84c9-70355724547f`) web roles. The purpose-built `AL Portal - *` roles carry no table permissions and are currently inert. | `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-29-portal-answering-module.md` line 1213 states those two ids are 'Tax Reviewer' and 'AQS Reviewer'. They are not, confirmed against DEV on 2026-08-31: both are type-11 web role components named Administrators and Authenticated Users, and the latter has `authenticatedusersrole: true`. Anyone following that line believes they are granting to two reviewer roles while actually granting to every signed-in portal user. Under OD-022 that is now the intended outcome for `al_outcomecase`, so nothing is over-granted today — but the mapping was wrong before the decision made it right, and it stays a trap for any table that should not be world-readable. Recorded rather than silently corrected because it also means OD-019's new Planner role and OD-021's Regional Manager removal change nothing on their own: no permission binds to those roles either. | 2026-08-31 |
| AD-068 | Page permissions are introduced for the portal: one Restrict Read rule on Home (`a1000000-0000-4000-8000-0000000000b0`), scoped to exclude direct child web files so `outcome-testing.css` still serves on the sign-in page, binding all seven `AL Portal - *` roles. Per-page Restrict Read rules narrow the role-specific branches — Tax reviews (`…0b1`), AQS reviews (`…0b2`), Review (`…0b3`) and Remediation (`…0b4`) — each a subset of Home's role list, per the AD-059-band subset rule `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1` now enforces. `Access Denied`, `Page Not Found` and `Default Offline Page` are exempted **in the gate**, `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1`, from needing a Restrict Read rule of their own — they are not public in the deployed site. All three are children of Home and therefore inherit Home's `…0b0` rule like every other page under it, which is why the runbook's role matrix names a possible redirect loop for a role-less contact sent to Access Denied: that page is itself behind the same rule it is trying to explain. An earlier draft of this decision called them "allow-listed as public," which commit `a82da34` corrected in the design spec; this row previously reintroduced that same error. The pre-existing `Grant Change to Administrators` (`563ee258-2962-4440-a6e8-d25296ac40bb`) sits on Home with All-content scope and **overrides Restrict Read site-wide for Administrators** — this is a pre-existing grant, not something this decision adds, and it means no test may assert an Administrator is denied any page. The inert `Grant Change to Content` rule (`7f9846c2-9af9-4dae-a52d-4b106ec11302`), which bound no role and granted nothing reachable, is deleted. | Resolves the "no page permissions of any kind" gap recorded in the security closure design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-portal-security-closure-design.md`, section 1) and closes PP-01/PP-02/NFR-SEC-01/NFR-SEC-02 for page-level access. Before this, every page under Home was reachable by URL regardless of role, because Power Pages treats a page with no rule as public. Excluding direct child web files from Home's scope, rather than restricting the whole subtree, is what keeps the sign-in page styled — a blanket Home restriction was tried first and produced an unstyled sign-in page, since `outcome-testing.css` is served as a child web file of Home. The subset requirement on the branch rules is enforced structurally by `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1` assertion 4, not by convention, because Power Pages makes a page unreachable for any role granted at a child rule but absent from the ancestor rule. | 2026-08-31 |
| AD-069 | Write-scope table permissions bind to the purpose-built `AL Portal` roles, closing AD-067's finding that they were inert: `Review Instance - assigned to me` and its child `Response - on a review assigned to me` (Contact-scoped, `contact_al_reviewinstance`) bind Tax Reviewer and AQS Reviewer; the new Contact-scoped `Remediation Action - assigned to me` (`a1000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000006b`, `contact_al_remediationaction`) binds Adviser Remediation and Planner. Read-all for `al_OutcomeCase`, `al_ReviewInstance` and `al_Response` **stays bound to Authenticated Users**, per OD-022's direction that every authenticated portal user sees all cases and acts only on what is assigned to them — this decision does not move that read grant onto the purpose-built roles. | Supersedes AD-067's standing description of the `AL Portal - *` roles as inert: they now carry the write-scope grants that make the Contact-anchored edit boundary (AD-047, AD-056) actually reachable by a signed-in user rather than only by Administrators/Authenticated Users. Read-all deliberately stays where OD-022 put it rather than being narrowed to the purpose-built roles, because OD-022 is explicit that case-level Contact scoping was tried and rejected — narrowing read here would silently re-introduce the mechanic OD-022 closed. `…06b` is minted from the free tail of the table-permission band the AD-059 amendment below narrows to `60`–`6f`, mirroring `contact_al_reviewinstance`'s pattern for the other Contact-anchored parent. | 2026-08-31 **Amended 2026-09-09:** the adviser's response is editable only until it is submitted (project owner direction). `RemediationResponseGuardPlugin`, a pre-operation step on Update of `al_remediationaction` filtered to `al_adviserresponse,al_evidencereference`, refuses a change to either once the action is Completed; a rejected sign-off reopens it. Who may write at all stays with this Contact-scoped permission and the owner check on `al_CompleteRemediation`. The step needs registering: `registerstep <orgUrl> OutcomeTesting.Plugins.RemediationResponseGuardPlugin Update al_remediationaction 20 al_adviserresponse,al_evidencereference,al_clientcontactrequired,al_recheckrequired,al_changesadvice` (widened to five columns under AD-095 on 2026-09-09; the live DEV step was updated with `setstepfilter`). |
| AD-059 (amended) | The AD-059 band table is extended to two component types already in use and one this closure adds: `90`–`97` web roles, `a0`–`af` site settings (both previously undocumented), and `b0`–`bf` page access control rules (new, for AD-068's rules). The table-permission band is narrowed from `60`–`75` to `60`–`6f`, because `70` is already a weblink set and `71`–`75` are permissions that predate this correction and are not relocated. | The original AD-059 table stopped short of web roles and site settings even though both were already populated in the `a1000000-...` space, and had no band at all for page access control rules before AD-068 needed one. The band table describes where *new* ids are minted; it does not relocate existing components, which is why `71`–`75` stay put rather than being renumbered into the corrected `60`–`6f` range. Recorded in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-portal-security-closure-design.md` section 9 and now in `powerpages/README.md`. | 2026-08-31 |
| AD-070 | **Corrects AD-069's authentication settings.** `Authentication/Registration/ExternalLoginEnabled` stays **`true`**. It is the site-wide switch for external identity providers, not a switch for external self-service accounts, and Entra ID OpenID Connect is such a provider — so with `LocalLoginEnabled` and `Registration/Enabled` both `false`, turning it off leaves no route into the portal at all. `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1` assertion 7 now asserts this setting is `true` alongside the three it asserts are `false`. | The value was set to `false` during the security closure on the reading that "external login" meant external sign-up, and would have locked every user out of DEV on the next upload. Microsoft Learn's Entra setup page (`power-pages/security/authentication/openid-settings`) settles it: if no identity providers appear, External login must be On. The site's own `AzureADLoginEnabled` description agrees, calling Azure AD "an external identity provider". An assertion that a hardening gate requires a setting to be *enabled* reads as a mistake at a glance, so it carries its reasoning inline — the next person to tidy it will read why first. Left open and routed to the DEV runbook rather than guessed: whether `Registration/Enabled: false` blocks a first-time contact from associating their external identity, which is how every new joiner arrives. Sign-in is not registration and contacts are provisioned by Entra sync (AD-047), so an existing contact is unaffected; the first-time case is a step-4 test with the remedy named. | 2026-08-31 |
| AD-071 | The `<plugintypeexportkey>` in `src/customapis/*/customapi.xml` is an environment-specific plug-in type id, not a stable contract value. Re-creating a plug-in type invalidates it, and it must be re-read from the environment before the next solution pack. | Found 2026-09-02 registering the three RBAC commands. `src` carried the ids from the environment where AD-045 was first deployed — the same ids AD-052 removed from DEV as orphans on 2026-08-29 — so the committed value pointed at a plug-in type that no longer existed anywhere. The failure mode is the one AD-051 already describes and is worth naming again because nothing reports it: an import binds the Custom API to a missing type, the API is created, accepts calls and executes nothing. The three keys were corrected to the ids `registerall` had just created before packing. The durable fix is the AD-013 round trip, which re-reads them from the export; the trap is only live in the window between creating a type and round-tripping. | 2026-09-02 |
| AD-072 | Manual allocation is one server-side command, `al_AssignCase`, that resolves **both** identity systems from a single work email and refuses unless both exist. `al_CaseAssignment` carries `al_assigneduserid` (systemuser) and `al_assignedcontactid` (contact), both `RemoveLink` on delete; the command writes a new assignment row, releases the prior active one, stamps `al_ReviewInstance` with the contact, the owner and `Assigned`, and moves the case `Queued -> Assigned` through `CaseTransitions`. | Resolves the build half of OD-029. The two front ends cannot share one identity: the Code App reads `ownerid` and Power Pages table permissions resolve only through Contact relationships and cannot read `ownerid` at all (AD-047). Setting one and not the other produces a case that reads as allocated in one front end and is invisible in the other, which is worse than a refusal — so the command resolves both from the AD-010 canonical work email and fails with a `PRECONDITION:` naming the missing half. `RemoveLink` rather than `Restrict` because removing a leaver's user or contact record must never destroy allocation history (BR-003, BR-012). The assignment code is stable per (case, review, assignee) so a replayed create collides on the alternate key instead of writing a duplicate history row. Optimistic concurrency is applied to the review instance rather than the case, because that is the row two allocators would actually race for. Deliberately unbuilt: reassignment is the same command run again — it releases and re-writes — so no separate reassign command exists; and no team scoping, per OD-029. | 2026-09-02 **Amended 2026-09-09:** `al_AssignCase` opens the review instance when the case carries no open check, using the same `ClaimCasePlugin.OpenNextReview` the portal self-claim uses. Until then only a self-claim created instances, so a manager could not allocate a case nobody had claimed, nor the AQS leg of a Tax-then-AQS case once its Tax check had returned it to the queue - BR-003's named allocation was reachable only after a checker had taken the case themselves. |
| AD-073 | **Supersedes `docs/submit-review-flow.md`.** Portal submission does not use Power Automate. The review page writes one allowlisted boolean, `al_reviewinstance.al_submitrequested`, over the Portals Web API, and a synchronous post-operation plug-in `SubmitRequestPlugin` runs the submission. Both entry points — the `al_SubmitReview` Custom API and this one — call the same `SubmitReviewPlugin.Submit`, which takes a `requireCallerOwnsReview` flag: true for the Custom API, false for the portal. | Extends AD-053 from answering to submitting, so the whole portal reaches Dataverse the same way and needs no Power Automate licence. **The flow route was not merely inconvenient, it was broken**: `EnsureCaller` compares `InitiatingUserId` to the review's `ownerid`, but a cloud flow runs under its own connection identity, and allocation (AD-072) now sets `ownerid` to the assignee — so every portal submit would have returned `UNAUTHORIZED:`. The authorization model had to change either way. For the portal the boundary is the Contact-scoped `Review Instance - assigned to me` table permission, which moves from `adx_write: false` to `true`; reaching the plug-in at all means the platform already allowed the write on a review whose assigned contact is the signed-in user (AD-047, AD-056). The site setting allowlists **only** `al_submitrequested`, so status, submitted-on and the assigned contact stay unwritable from a browser.
