Consequences accepted: (a) a Dataverse user with write privilege on `al_reviewinstance` can now trigger a submission they do not own, where the Custom API refused that — the audit event records the actor and names the assigned contact, so acting on someone else's behalf is visible rather than hidden; (b) `ExpectedRowVersion` is not sent on the portal path, so the optimistic-concurrency check does not run there — the state guard (submittable only from Assigned or Review In Progress) and the idempotent already-submitted branch still cover the double-submit race, which is the one that actually occurs. The idempotency key is derived as `portal-submit-<reviewid>`, stable per review, because a browser cannot carry one across page loads. `OutcomeTesting/Flow/SubmitReview` is left in place and empty rather than deleted, since an upload does not delete site settings. | 2026-09-02 |
| AD-074 | The T&C attestation is a **create** on `al_signoff`, not a trigger column on `al_remediationaction`. `SignoffGuardPlugin` (synchronous pre-operation Create) validates and stamps; `SignoffProgressPlugin` (synchronous post-operation Create) applies the consequences. The portal permission is Parent-scoped under `Remediation Action - read all` via `al_remediationaction_signoff`, bound to `AL Portal - T&C Supervisor` alone. | The Portals Web API supports create, so unlike AD-073 no trigger column is needed — and that difference is what makes the permission model sound rather than merely convenient. A trigger column on `al_remediationaction` would sit behind the single site-wide `Webapi/al_remediationaction/fields` allowlist, which is shared with the adviser, so an adviser could set the decision on their own action and self-approve their remediation. Authorization for the portal path cannot be a caller check (AD-053), so the only boundary available is the table permission — and a permission can only distinguish the adviser from the T&C Manager if the two write to different tables. Splitting guard from progress follows AD-053's response guard/progress split: validation must run before the row exists and consequences after it, and one step cannot be both. The page sends only decision, notes and action; name, business code, case and timestamp are stamped server-side so the browser cannot choose a key or backdate a sign-off. **Scope correction worth recording:** this was first authored Global-scope with create, which `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1` assertion 9 refused — Global must never carry write, create or delete. Parent scope achieves the same reach through the parent chain without the blanket grant. The gate caught an over-grant that reasoning had not. | 2026-09-02 |
| AD-075 | A closed **Tax-only** case is exported to Trail Light with AD-039 columns 10 (File Quality Grade) and 15 (Advice Quality Grade) blank, rather than refusing the batch. `GenerateExportPlugin.DescribeIncompleteRow` takes an `aqsExpected` flag and asks its two missing-value rules — no Outcome, no Q-FQ-01 answer — only when an AQS review was due. The flag is read from the case's `al_reviewroute.al_requiresaqsreview`, falling back, where the case carries no route, to whether an AQS review instance exists. | Resolves OD-031. Both graded columns are sourced from the AQS review, so on a Tax-only case they have no source and never will: refusing was not a prompt to act, it was a permanent block on every batch containing one such case. Excluding the case instead would make the delivered file silently disagree with the case list. The route rather than the presence of an answer decides, so a genuine AQS case that reaches Closed ungraded is still refused — the distinction being drawn is "never due" against "due and missing", which only the route knows. The no-route fallback repeats `SubmitReviewPlugin.AqsStillToCome` so a legacy case predating the route seed is not reclassified as Tax-only by accident. **Deliberately not relaxed:** the OD-024 accountability rule. An Outcome that exists is a fact about the case whatever its route, so a non-pass with no accountability recorded still refuses the batch. | 2026-09-03 |
| AD-076 | Checkers pick their own work out of the shared queue from the portal. A "Run checks" button on the Tax and AQS review pages opens a confirmation dialog; continuing POSTs an `al_caseassignment` row carrying the case and the signed-in contact, and the synchronous pre-operation `ClaimCasePlugin` resolves the Dataverse user from the contact's work email (AD-010), opens or attaches to the review instance, stamps the assignment row, sets `al_outcomecase.al_checkername`, moves the case `Queued -> Assigned` and writes the audit event. The portal permission is **Contact-scoped** on `al_caseassignment` through `contact_al_caseassignment`, create and read, bound to the two reviewer roles. | Extends AD-040, which had manual manager allocation as the only route out of `Queued`, leaving the queue readable but not actionable. Modelled on AD-074 rather than AD-073: a create, so no trigger column, and — the substantive reason — a table of its own carries a permission of its own. Contact scope is what supplies identity, because authorization on this path cannot be a caller check: a Power Pages write reaches Dataverse as the site's application user, so `InitiatingUserId` is not the checker (AD-053). The discriminator against the AD-072 command writing the same table is `al_assigneduserid`, which `al_AssignCase` always sets and the browser cannot — the allowlist is the case and the contact alone. Four rules carry the safety: the case must be at `Queued` (stated explicitly, because `CaseLifecycle` treats `Assigned -> Assigned` as allowed and a second claimant would otherwise pass); the review instance must have no assigned contact; the discipline comes from the route, not from the page, so a Tax-then-AQS case opens its Tax check wherever the button was pressed (BR-004); and the review instance code is deterministic per case and discipline so a double-click collides on the alternate key instead of opening two checks. **Deliberate scope decisions:** the claim creates the review instance when none exists, because nothing else in the solution creates one and a queued case would otherwise never be claimable — it is stamped with the checklist version in force by date (BR-013), and a claim is refused outright when no version is in force, since a review instance without one renders no questions; the prior active assignment is released first, because a case returned to `Queued` for the Tax-then-AQS handoff still carries the Tax leg's row; and the audit event reuses the AD-072 allocation command value rather than minting a new one, keeping the change free of any option-set edit, with the details line recording that the checker took the case themselves. **Residual risk accepted:** if Power Pages does not enforce Contact scope on *create* as it does on read and write, a checker could post a claim naming another contact. That is work-planting, not privilege escalation — the claimant must still be a real contact with a matching Dataverse account, the case must be genuinely free, and the audit event names who it went to. **Deployment (2026-09-03): the queue list is live in DEV, the claim action is not.** The button, dialog and script are gated on the site setting `OutcomeTesting/Claim/Enabled`, deployed as `false`, and the `Case Assignment - claim from the queue` permission was deliberately held out of the environment. Both because `ClaimCasePlugin` cannot be compiled on the authoring machine (no .NET SDK) and without it the Web API create still *succeeds* — the columns the plug-in stamps are only *business* required, which Dataverse does not enforce through the API — so the page would report success while the case stayed queued, leaving assignment rows AD-037 forbids deleting. Switching the feature on is therefore: register the plug-in, create the permission, set the site setting to `true`. | 2026-09-03 **Amended 2026-09-09:** a claim is refused unless the claimant's web roles include the discipline's reviewer role (`ClaimCasePlugin.EnsureDisciplineRole`: Tax Reviewer for a Tax check, AQS Reviewer for an AQS check). The queue pages filter by discipline but both reviewer roles are bound to the same claim permission, so a reviewer who reached the other page could take its work. Manager allocation (`al_AssignCase`) is unchanged; OD-029(c) still stands there. **Amended 2026-09-10 (AD-112):** the claim asks the route which discipline is due before it looks for a review instance, instead of taking the earliest unsubmitted instance as the check due. A case whose route changed after its checks were opened otherwise strands: the discipline the route newly requires is never created and the premature instance blocks the claim that would create it. **Amended 2026-09-10 (AD-113):** the queue is AQS only. A Tax check is allocated to a named checker by the Tax lead and is never picked up, so the Tax page carries no queue, no claim button and no claim script; a Tax-then-AQS case still reaches the AQS queue for its AQS leg once its Tax review is submitted. The queue also stops naming a checker, because `al_checkername` carries the upload file's "Checker name" as much as the allocated checker. |

| AD-077 | BR-002 import validation runs server-side as the Custom API `al_ImportCases`, which takes the raw extract as text and does the whole import — batch, cases, exceptions, audit event — in one transaction. The Code App keeps its parser purely to preview rejections before upload. | Closes the one place the solution broke its own AD-003 invariant: `useCaseUpload.ts` created `al_importexception`, `al_importbatch` and `al_outcomecase` rows directly over OData, so BR-002 existed only in the browser and anyone posting to the Web API bypassed it entirely. The extract is sent as text rather than as pre-parsed rows on purpose — if the client parsed and the server only stored, BR-002 would still be a client rule with a server-shaped wrapper round it. The Code App now has **no** direct Dataverse writes at all. Two defects were found and fixed while moving the rule: the date parser fell through to a month-first reader, so `01/13/2026` imported as 13 January rather than being refused, and the client's written-out-date path used `toISOString()` on a locally-parsed date, landing an advice date a day early anywhere east of UTC. Row numbers also no longer shift when a blank line is skipped, so a rejection points at the row the user actually has. | 2026-09-03 |
| AD-078 | Closing an import exception is the Custom API `al_ResolveImportException`: `Open -> Resolved` or `Open -> Ignored` with a mandatory note, recorded on a new `al_resolutionnote` column and an Audit Event. Reopening is not offered. | FR-002/FR-003 describe returning invalid cases with a reason; the return half existed (the validation report) but nothing could close the loop, so `/imports` rendered exceptions read-only for ever. The two closures are the ones the deployed option set already carries — no third status is invented, and "Returned" is explicitly refused rather than coerced onto one of them. The note lives in its own column so closing an exception never overwrites `al_reason`, which is why the row failed; and the command refuses an exception that is not Open, because a second closure would overwrite the first one's note and timestamp and AD-037 forbids deleting the row to put it back. | 2026-09-03 |
| AD-079 | The OD-018 remediation clock reset is a new `al_clockstartedon` column on `al_remediationaction`, stamped by `SignoffProgressPlugin` on a rejected sign-off. `createdon` keeps the original start, so the two columns together give the previous period and the current one. | OD-018 required that a rejected sign-off reset the clock while preserving the previous timer, and said it needed each period stored on the action. Storing only the current start plus the original one answers the case OD-018 actually describes — a case that goes round twice — without a second table: `createdon`-to-`al_clockstartedon` is the timer that ended and `al_clockstartedon`-to-now is the one running. The ten-working-day threshold measures the **current** period, which is the point of a reset: banding on the merged age would report a freshly reworked action as breached before the adviser had had a day on it. A third round's earlier boundaries live on the `al_signoff` rows, which is where OD-018 puts the audit trail; the helper reports the two periods the columns can answer for and does not guess at the rest. | 2026-09-03 |
| AD-080 | `al_Notification` is built as an outbox with the **five** events AD-035 names, not nine. A row is written in the same transaction as the state change and rests at `Pending`; the drain is not built. | Project owner direction 2026-09-03, answering OD-030 gap (a): ship the five that are enumerated and add the other four when someone names them, because adding option values later is additive and safe while inventing four business events is not reversible. Emitters are hung off record creation (`NotificationEmitterPlugin`, synchronous post-operation Create on `al_caseassignment` and `al_remediationaction`) rather than off the commands, because allocation reaches `al_caseassignment` by three routes — `al_AssignCase`, the portal self-claim and a manager writing the row — and **nothing in the solution creates remediation actions at all**, so a command-side emitter would miss every one of them. Sign-off approval and rejection are emitted from `SignoffProgressPlugin`, keyed on the sign-off rather than the action so a case that goes round twice notifies twice. The deterministic `al_notificationcode` is the table's alternate key, so a retry collides instead of queueing a second email. The drain is deliberately absent: server-side email needs an approved, tested mailbox that has not been confirmed on DEV, TEST or PROD, and `Pending` is the honest resting state — the outbox records that the event happened and nothing claims an email was sent that was not. | 2026-09-03 |
| AD-081 | The PP-15 outbox drains through an **asynchronous** post-operation Create step on `al_notification` (`NotificationDrainPlugin` -> `NotificationDrain`), sending through Dataverse server-side email from the account the step is registered to run as. **Registering the step is the switch**: there is no separate enable setting. | Completes AD-080, which shipped the outbox with the drain deliberately absent. Asynchronous is the design, not a performance choice: the emitters write the row inside the state change's transaction, and the drain runs after it commits, so a mailbox refusal can never roll back a submitted review and a rolled-back review can never leave an email behind. It also removes the need for a scheduler — Dataverse has no native cron and Power Automate is excluded everywhere after AD-073, so the platform's own out-of-band execution is what makes a connector-free drain possible. **The registration is the environment gate.** An environment whose server-side mailbox is not yet approved simply does not get the step; rows rest at `Pending`, exactly as they did before this shipped, and nothing claims an email was sent. That is why no `OutcomeTesting/Notifications/Enabled` setting was introduced — a second switch that can disagree with the first is worse than one. `registerstep` gained optional `sync|async` and run-as arguments to support it; the run-as sets `impersonatinguserid`, which is how the service account is named without an address appearing in code (AGENTS.md rule 7). **Every terminal state is written, including failure.** A row that cannot be sent ends at `Failed` with `al_failurereason` populated and stays retryable, because a `Pending` row is indistinguishable from one the drain has not reached yet — the exact 'looks healthy, nothing arrives' failure OD-030 warns about. `al_DrainNotifications` (Manage on `permission.manage`, batch clamped to 250) is the recovery path for rows queued before the step existed, rows that failed against a mailbox since approved, and rows whose recipient has since been resolved. `Sent` rows are never re-drained. | 2026-09-04 **Deployed 2026-09-04 (`9390177`), switched off.** Assembly, both plug-in types, `al_DrainNotifications` and its solution membership are all in `Env_AQ_Dev` and verified by query. The drain step is **not** registered, because the DEV mailbox is not approved (see OD-030) — which is exactly the behaviour this decision designs for: the gate is the registration, so an environment without a mailbox simply does not get the step and its rows keep resting at `Pending`. |
| AD-082 | `Review submitted` notifies the **para-planner named on the case**, resolved from `al_outcomecase.al_paraplanner` to a Contact by full name. An ambiguous, missing or unemailable match queues the row with no recipient, and the drain then marks it `Failed` saying so. | Resolves OD-030(ii) on project owner direction 2026-09-04 ("each case has a paraplanner field"), and is what makes BR-009 deliverable: the para-planner hears that the check on their case is finished, by email, without a licence, a web role or any operational access — precisely because the recipient is resolved as a Contact and not as a system user. **The weakness is named rather than hidden.** The case carries the para-planner as a *name* and an Intelligent Office *code*; neither is an address, and Contact is unmodified in this solution, so full name is the only link between the two that exists in the model. The lookup therefore reads two candidates, not one, and returns nothing unless exactly one active Contact with a work email matches. Two people called J Smith, a contact with no work email, or no contact at all all leave the row unrouted. Sending a client's advice outcome to the wrong para-planner is a data-protection incident; a `Failed` row a person can see is an operational one. **If this proves too weak in practice the fix is a column, not a looser match:** an `al_paraplanneremail` carried from the Intelligent Office extract, which is a schema and import change and was not taken now. | 2026-09-04 |
| AD-083 | PP-17's portal drill-down lives on **Home**, as five outcome cards — the four BR-005 grades plus Not yet graded — each linking to `/cases?outcome=<slug>`, with a matching Outcome filter and column on the case list. Counts are org-wide. | Project owner direction 2026-09-04 placed it on Home rather than on My Work or a new page. Home was already the router and My Work is deliberately personal (PP-03), so org-wide reporting counts sit better beside the router than beside a worklist whose every other number is scoped to the signed-in user. Org-wide is permitted under OD-022, where every authenticated user may read every case; full MI and export stay back-office per PP-17. **Grade precedence is written once.** A case's grade is final where recorded, otherwise initial (BR-007), which in FetchXML is an OR whose second branch is an AND, expressed identically in the four card aggregates and in the list filter. The join is safe to make because there is exactly one `al_outcome` row per case: only the AQS branch of `SubmitReviewPlugin` writes one, on a review instance whose code `ClaimCasePlugin` makes deterministic per case and discipline, and a regrade updates `al_finaloutcome` on that same row. The link-entity is **outer**, so an ungraded case is not silently dropped from the whole worklist. **No option-set number is ever rendered.** Six small numeric aggregates were chosen over one grouped query specifically so the page cannot depend on how an aliased choice column renders in Liquid, and the shared `OT Outcome Label` template resolves a grade to words with a numeric map as fallback. URLs carry slugs (`pass`, `harm`, `none`) rather than option values, and an unrecognised slug is cleared rather than ignored — ignoring it would drop the condition and show every case under a "Filtered cases" heading. | 2026-09-04 |
| AD-084 | Power Pages component ids are one keyspace, not one per component type. `OT Case Detail Page` is reminted from `a1000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000022` to `...002b`, and `Check-ComponentIds.ps1` runs before every `pac pages upload`. | `/case-details` returned the generic Power Pages error because the page had no page template. `OT Case Detail Page` (page template) and `OT Outcome Label` (web template, added by PP-17) were both minted as `...0022`. In the enhanced data model every component — web template, page template, web page, table permission, web link — is a row in the single `powerpagecomponent` table keyed by `powerpagecomponentid`, so the two were **one row**: the web template won, the page template stopped existing, and both `case-details` web pages were left pointing at nothing. Confirmed by query before the fix — `OT Case Detail Page` absent from `mspp_pagetemplate`, `...0022` present as a web template, both web pages carrying a null page template. **This is the second occurrence.** `OT Case List Page` and `OT Answer Options` collided on `...0021` and broke `/cases` identically; `Check-ComponentIds.ps1` was written then, for exactly this, and was not run before the 2026-09-04 upload. The guard is therefore the decision, not the reminting — the id band comment in the script already says the ranges "are not enforced anywhere", so nothing but the guard stands between a hand-minted id and a deleted component. The **page template** moved rather than the web template because `{% include 'OT Outcome Label' %}` resolves by name, so neither choice required a reference change, and moving the page template avoids depending on replace semantics against the row already in the environment. Diagnosis order is worth keeping: the page's three FetchXML queries were run verbatim against DEV and every web template it includes was confirmed present *before* concluding it was an id fault, which is what ruled out Liquid, missing includes and table permissions. | 2026-09-04 |
| AD-085 | The application user registry is **`contact`**, not `al_User`. `emailaddress1` carries the AD-010 work email, `fullname` the display name and `statecode` the active flag. `al_CreateUser`, `al_UpdateUser` and `al_SetUserActive` write contacts; `ContactRegistry` holds the mapping and the naming rules. `al_User` is **decommissioned, not deleted** (OD-037). | Project owner direction 2026-09-06. The app carried two lists of people that nothing joined: `al_User`, an admin registry keyed on work email, and a People view built from the adviser/paraplanner/checker name strings on `al_OutcomeCase`. A DEV probe found **three contacts and ten `al_user` rows with no overlap at all** — the registry was entirely fictional seed data. Contact is what Power Pages resolves permissions through (AD-047) and what `AssignCasePlugin` already demands alongside the systemuser (OD-003), so keying the app to it leaves one set of people. Safe because authorisation never read `al_User`: `PermissionHelpers` resolves roles from `al_userrolemapping` keyed on `al_useremail` and treats a missing registry row as permitted — and the environment's only role mapping was for an email that had no `al_user` row. **Supersedes the AD-058 mechanism**: deactivation is now `contact.statecode`, not `al_user.al_isactive`; the rule it states is unchanged. Note `contact.fullname` is calculated and unwritable, so a display name is split into `firstname`/`lastname` with the last token as the surname. | 2026-09-06 |
| AD-086 | People and Users merge into **one directory at `/people`**: the contact registry is the spine, caseload is joined onto it by the name recorded on the case, and the admin actions sit behind `permission.manage`. `/admin/users` redirects. Someone named on a case who is not in the registry is still listed and flagged, not dropped. | Project owner direction 2026-09-06, following AD-085. The two screens answered different halves of one question and shared no key — `peopleDirectory.ts` said in its own comment that it "does not claim to be a user directory", because the case carries no email to join on. Giving both views one keyed source is what the merge is; keeping the unregistered names visible preserves the workload picture the People page exists to give, and flagging them is what makes "cannot be allocated work until added" legible. | 2026-09-06 |
| AD-087 | Application roles are the **Power Pages web roles**. The role list is read live from `mspp_webrole`; assignment associates the contact through `powerpagecomponent_mspp_webrole_contact` **and** mirrors into `al_UserRoleMapping`; permission rules key on the web role name through the existing `al_rolecode` column. `al_CreateRole`/`al_UpdateRole` write web roles. The `al_approle` picklist is no longer offered but is **still read**, so nothing already assigned is silently revoked. `Anonymous Users` and `Authenticated Users` are excluded from the vocabulary. | Project owner direction 2026-09-06, choosing full replacement of the six AD-020/AD-031 role names over a mapping layer, after the lockout risk was put to them. No schema changed: `al_rolecode` is the free-text column AD-044 already reserved for custom roles and which outranks the picklist. This environment runs the **enhanced** portal data model, so the table is `mspp_webrole` over `powerpagecomponent` and there is no `adx_webrole`; the contact link is reached from the contact side because `mspp_webrole` carries no many-to-many of its own. Role resolution is the **union** of the associations and the mapping table — reading only the associations would withdraw access the mapping had, reading only the mapping would make the web roles decorative — and that union is what makes replacing the vocabulary safe. Feasibility was proved, not assumed: a probe created, renamed, associated, disassociated and deleted a web role before any of this was built. Seven of the eight business web roles map onto the AD-020/AD-031 roles they replace; the eighth, `AL Portal - Planner`, is settled by **OD-019** — it shares remediation routing with `AL Portal - Adviser Remediation`, so it carries the same authority rather than a lesser one. | 2026-09-06 **Amended 2026-09-09:** `al_UpdateRole` refuses a rename. For a web role the name is the code every `al_userrolemapping` and `al_pagepermission` row references, and `PermissionHelpers` resolves web-role associations by name, so a rename orphaned every assignment and rule and the retire cascade then missed them. The same review found the command still writing `al_description` and `al_isactive`, columns `mspp_webrole` does not have, so re-describe and retire faulted; they now write `mspp_description` and statecode. A role that needs a new name is a new role with its assignments moved across. |
| AD-088 | `al_rolecode` **outranks** `al_approle` when the server resolves a caller's roles, matching AD-044 and the client. | 2026-09-06, correcting `PermissionHelpers.GetActiveRoles`, which read the picklist first and skipped the code whenever it found one. Not harmless: `al_approle` carries a schema default, so every mapping row written with only a role code came back **also** carrying that default, the picklist won, and every web role assignment resolved as `Adviser` rather than the role it named — three assignments to three different roles all resolving identically. Both halves are fixed: the precedence is inverted, and both writers now set `al_approle` explicitly null so a code-based assignment stops picking the default up. Found by reading back what the seed wrote rather than trusting it wrote what was intended. | 2026-09-06 |
| AD-089 | A portal-side role assignment **grants access and is visible immediately, and is not authoritative until an administrator adopts it.** The union stands (AD-087): nothing is silently revoked. `al_GetRoleHolders` reports the four-cell truth table (mapping × association) through pure `classifyHolder`, and `al_AdoptRoleAssignment` writes one of two convergent decisions — Adopt (mapping active, associated) or Revoke (mapping withdrawn, disassociated) — each producing an Audit Event naming the administrator, the role and the person. | Spec `2026-09-06-role-assignment-conflict-rule-design.md`, closing the two items AD-087 left owed (`docs/2026-09-04-outstanding-work.md` §5). **Rejected: auto-reconcile on a schedule** — not deferred, rejected outright, because it writes audit events recording decisions nobody made, attributed to a job, and leaves a latency window in which the app still lies; in a trail that exists to show who decided what, an automated adoption is worse than a visible gap. **Rejected as a substitute, not on the merits: refusing portal-side assignment** by stripping web-role membership rights — an organisational control that system administrators route around, heals nothing already granted, and cannot fix the client/server split on its own; recorded as a non-goal, worth doing later as a complement. | 2026-09-06 |
| AD-090 | Role resolution **ignores any web role auto-granted to everyone**, following the `mspp_authenticatedusersrole` **flag**, not the role name. `GetActiveRoles` resolves each held role through `WebRoleRegistry.FindByName`, reads `AuthenticatedAttr`, and excludes it; `Anonymous Users` stays excluded by name. `IsSystemRole`, previously dead code called from nowhere, is extended to take the flag into account and is now the enforcement point. | **OD-033 is the evidence**: `Administrators` carried `mspp_authenticatedusersrole = Yes` in DEV on 2026-09-04, which in Power Pages auto-grants the role to every authenticated contact — with `GetActiveRoles` unioning in whatever web roles a contact holds, unfiltered, every contact then resolved as `Administrators` server-side. Not a hypothetical: it happened, and nothing in the resolver contained it. Confirms and generalises AD-087's exclusion of `Authenticated Users` by name to a rule that survives a differently-named role carrying the same flag, so drift of the OD-033 kind cannot grant application permissions to every signed-in contact again. | 2026-09-06 |
| AD-091 | **A question version is in force from the start of its effective-from day until the start of its effective-to day, and that is the only version of a question a review is read against.** The reference day is the review's submission day once submitted, otherwise today. `ResponseRules.IsVersionEffective` is the rule; `ResponseGuardPlugin` refuses a new answer on a version not in force, `SubmitReviewPlugin` owes mandatory answers only on versions in force and reads a question's answer from the version in force first (falling back to any version, newest first), the review page filters its question FetchXML by the same dates, and the Code App's review detail hides answers on other versions. Statecode is not consulted: a retired version stays Active so the answers it holds keep resolving (BR-013). | AD-015 gave versions effective dates and `RetireAndSucceedQuestion` stamps them, but nothing read them. Found 2026-09-09 on the one submitted Tax review in DEV: Q-TAX-01 and Q-TAX-02 each had three Active versions, the page rendered all of them, the submit gate demanded an answer on each, and `AnswerFor` took the most recently modified answer - the retired Q-TAX-02 v1's "Insufficient evidence" over the current v3's "Pass". That review's case was unaffected only because Remedial action required? was Yes, which routes to remediation regardless; with a No, a superseded answer would have decided the case. Date comparison rather than deactivation keeps FR-030/FR-031 history intact and makes the retire day unambiguous. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-091 | **A submitted review raises the `al_remediationaction` BR-006 implies.** Two triggers, either sufficient: the outcome is not a Pass, or the checker answered Yes to the checklist's mandatory "Remedial action required?" (`Q-FQ-03` on AQS, `Q-FQTAX-03` on Tax). `SubmitReviewPlugin.FinaliseReview` raises it inside the submit transaction, keyed `REM-<caseRef>-<sequence>` on the `al_remediationactioncode` alternate key so a replay finds the row it already raised. An existing code means do nothing at all — an upsert would reset the status to Open and overwrite the adviser's response. | **Nothing had ever created one.** The case status said remediation was owed and always had, but an unfiltered fetch returned zero `al_remediationaction` rows for the whole environment, so the adviser response, `CompleteRemediationPlugin`, the T&C sign-off, the BR-010 clock and `NotificationEmitterPlugin`'s registered `Create` step were each correct and each unreachable. The checklist flag is not a restatement of the outcome: it carries the case the grade cannot express, a file passed that still has something to put right. Project owner direction, 2026-09-09. Record: `docs/deployment/2026-09-09-remediation-raising.md`. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-092 | **Raising an action decides the case status: anything that raises remediation sends the case to Awaiting Remediation.** A flagged AQS Pass goes there rather than Closed, and a flagged Tax pass is held there before AQS rather than handed to the queue. `NextCaseStatusForAqs` and `NextCaseStatusForTax` keep their old signatures and delegate to flag-aware overloads, so the change is additive. | Closing a case with an open action against it strands the action: AD-057 permits no transition out of Closed, so the adviser could never work it and the sign-off could never land. An open action always sits on an open case. Holding a flagged Tax pass applies the reasoning OD-027 already gives for a Tax non-pass — AQS must not review a file with something on it still unaddressed. Project owner direction, 2026-09-09, answering the routing conflict the two AD-091 triggers create. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-093 | **A case that has a review route and sits at Imported or Ready for Allocation is queued automatically**, Imported -> Ready for Allocation -> Queued through `CaseTransitions.MoveThrough`. Applied by `ImportCasesPlugin` (which now derives the route from the file's "Tax check required" answer via `DeriveRoute` and queues the case on create) and by `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin` after every save that leaves a routed case short of the queue, unless the caller set a status in the same call. The rule is state-based: editing any detail of a routed case at Ready for Allocation queues it. Pure rule in `CaseQueueing`, tested. One-off `queueroutedcases` verb re-saves pre-existing cases through the command so their audit events are the command's. | Nothing moved a case into Queued except a hand-back from a Tax check or a sign-off, so the AD-076 self-service queue never offered fresh work and AD-040's "a case sits in the queue" was only true after a lead edited the status by hand, twice for an imported case. Found on 2026-09-09 when two AQS-only DEV cases with no reviewer (IO-000124 at Imported, IO-000125 at Ready for Allocation) did not appear in the queue. Project owner direction: automatic rather than an explicit "send to queue" command. Ready for Allocation stays in the lifecycle as the hop the walk passes through, so AD-057's no-skipping rule and the app's `CASE_STATUS_TRANSITIONS` mirror are unchanged. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-094 | **Change tracking is enabled on every Dataverse table the portal reads: `al_OutcomeCase`, `al_ReviewInstance`, `al_Response`, `al_Outcome`, `al_RemediationAction`, `al_QuestionVersion`, `al_Question`, `al_Section`, `al_FailReason`, `al_ReviewRoute`** (solution `Entity.xml` flag, and the `setchangetracking` registration-tool verb for a live environment). The review page no longer reloads after a submit: it disables its own controls and shows the confirmation in place. | Power Pages renders from a server-side cache; a website write clears that cache only for the record it wrote, everything else refreshes within a 15-minute SLA, and Microsoft's Site Checker guidance names a table without change tracking as the cause of stale data on that table. Every write that matters here is made outside the website: `SubmitRequestPlugin` stamps `al_submittedon`, `AnswerWriter` creates the `al_response` rows, the app and the lifecycle plug-ins move cases. Found on 2026-09-09 when a reviewer who had just submitted IO-100001's AQS review was shown it editable with no answers, while Dataverse held the submission at 11:49:16Z and all ten answers. Not reloading fixes the page in hand regardless of cache; change tracking fixes the next visit. Verified against learn.microsoft.com/power-pages/admin/clear-server-side-cache and site-checker-cache-invalidation. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-095 | **The remediation form is the adviser's, filled on the remediation action.** Three new choice columns on `al_remediationaction` — `al_clientcontactrequired` (Yes 120910793 / No 120910794 / Potentially 120910795), `al_recheckrequired` (Yes 120910796 / No 120910797), `al_changesadvice` (Yes 120910798 / No 120910799) — carry the form's questions; the adviser's free-text response is the "Remedial action"; owner is the assigned contact, target date the due date, adviser sign-off the completion, supervisor sign-off the `al_signoff` decision, "All remedial actions checked and approved" the derived state of every action's latest decision, and "Regraded outcome" the `al_outcome` final grade. `RemediationResponseGuardPlugin` locks the three with the response once the action is Completed, and its step filters on all five. The worklist renders the form with the write panels; the case page renders it per case, read-only; the app's remediation tab shows the same columns. | Project owner's form and direction 2026-09-09 ("filled in by the adviser at the remediation action"). Columns live on the action rather than the case because that is where the adviser writes and where the Contact-scoped write permission already reaches (AD-069); a case-level block would need a new write path. Values from the free tail of the 1209107xx band (770-792 were used). "All checked and approved" is derived rather than stored so it cannot disagree with the sign-offs it summarises. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-096 | **File Quality fail points are one standalone block of the checklist, not a picker under each non-pass answer.** On the review page (portal) and the review detail (app) the block sits where the document puts it, between the AML and CRA checking points and the File Quality outcome (after the Tax check for the Tax team), lists every reason in the team's category set with a tick, and is always on screen. The ticks are recorded on the **File quality outcome** answer (Q-FQ-01 for AQS, Q-FQTAX-01 for Tax) through the existing `al_failreason_response` intersect (AD-025) and the existing answer-request path, so no schema or plug-in change; the app reads the block as the union of reasons linked to any answer on the review, so reasons attached under AD-054 stay visible. Supersedes AD-054. The app's review detail also now mirrors the document block for block: the case header fields in document order, every question of every team-owned section whether answered or not, tick grids where a section shares one scale, the "Outcome lens" line as each suitability block's footer, and remediation and escalation at the end. | Project owner's direction 2026-09-09: "The fail quality reason is not meant for all the yes no fails sections. It is its own standalone section. Treat it as that", and "Mirror the form as it appears, the sections and everything else". AD-054 had read the block's category spread as meaning a per-answer trigger; the owner's reading is that the categories are the block's own vocabulary. Anchoring on the outcome answer keeps the response-keyed intersect and the portal's allowlisted write path unchanged, and is the answer the reasons explain - a fail on the file. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-097 | **A remediation action's "Issue / fail reason" is prepopulated with the non-pass items from the check.** When a submit raises the action (BR-006), `Remediation.NonPassItems` reads every answer of Fail, Insufficient evidence, Potential harm or No on a question version in force, in section then question order, as "question: answer", followed by every File Quality fail point ticked on the review as "Fail point: category - reason", and `Remediation.Describe` leads the description with that list, then the checker's observation, then the standing instruction. One action per review still; the items are lines in its description, and the portal and app render the description with its line breaks. | Project owner's direction 2026-09-09: "The Issue / fail reason fields on the remediation need to be prepopulated with the items selected as non pass from the checks." Read from the responses rather than a fixed question list so a checklist change (FR-030) reaches it without code. Kept as one action rather than one per item because completion, sign-off and the OD-038 "remediation approved" gate all count actions, and splitting them would change what "done" means for the case; that remains open if the owner wants a line per item. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-098 | **The review pages lay the checklist out in the document's blocks, read from the section code.** The model holds Section then Question with no grouping level, but the Checker Checklist groups E1 to E5 under "Suitability core checks" as one table headed "Suitability test point", and titles its other blocks "File Quality - Tax check section", "File Quality - AML and CRA checking points", "File Quality – Fail points", "File Quality Outcome", "Centralised Retirement Proposition", "Consumer Duty overlay", "Checker judgement and grading". Both pages fold sections into those blocks by section code (`checklistForm.ts` `formBlocks`; the same `case` in the OT Review Detail template), in section order, with a subsection row and outcome-lens row per E section, the document's intro line per block, and a tick grid with one column per scale value where the block is a grid - on the portal a radio per cell, the table row being the autosave root. The portal review page also gained the document's first and last blocks: the case header fields in document order and a read-only Remediation and escalation block per case. A section code the mapping does not know keeps its own name. | Project owner 2026-09-09: "the Suitability test point section is not there" and "ensure all the pdf sections are included, and appear in the order they are shown". Mapping in code rather than a new subsection table because the grouping is presentation of one approved document and the requirements pack (4.1, 4.2) can still be met later by a parent-section column that this mapping would then read instead; that schema change, a data write, is not needed to match V8 today. Not shown: the other discipline's blocks on a review page (the Tax check on an AQS review), since each review instance holds its own team's answers (AD-020); a read-only view of the sibling review is a follow-up. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-099 | **The portal sign-off is a trigger column on the signatory's own contact, not a create on `al_signoff`.** The page PATCHes `contact.al_signoffrequest` (JSON: actionId, decision, notes) through the Self-scoped contact permission, now bound to the two reviewer roles and the T&C Supervisor role; the synchronous post-operation `SignoffRequestPlugin` (Update of contact, filtered on the column) checks the contact's web roles server-side for `AL Portal - T&C Supervisor`, clears the column, and creates the `al_signoff` with only the decision, notes and action, so `SignoffGuardPlugin` and `SignoffProgressPlugin` run unchanged. Supersedes the AD-074 write path; the AD-074 permission stays for reads. In the same change the queue page opens the checklist by case (`/review?case=&type=`), which the review page resolves in Liquid, instead of a Web API lookup that the `al_reviewinstance` allowlist refused on every claim. | Found 2026-09-09 when a T&C Supervisor (the service account, web role verified live) was answered 403 on sign-off, and the environment held **zero** `al_signoff` rows: no sign-off had ever succeeded. The live permission bindings matched source, and the Parent-scoped child's own role binding is not what Power Pages evaluates. This was the third browser-side `@odata.bind` create on the site; the first two (answers, AD-053; claims, AD-076) were refused with 90040106 whatever the permissions said and were resolved the same way. The role check moves server-side because the contact allowlist is one list per table, so a reviewer who can write the claim column can reach this one; the plug-in, not the page, is the boundary (NFR-SEC-01). The queue lookup failed because `Webapi/<table>/fields` governs reads as well as writes and widening it would hand the reviewer the status column. | 2026-09-09 |

| AD-100 | **The fail reason list is offered to both disciplines in full; the category groups the reasons, it does not scope them by team.** Every one of the twenty reasons renders for a Tax reviewer and for an AQS reviewer. Each team's ticks still hang off its own File quality outcome answer (Q-FQ-01 for AQS, Q-FQTAX-01 for Tax) through the response-keyed intersect (AD-025), and `AnswerWriter.ReconcileFailReasons` only disassociates ids the calling page rendered, so the two sets cannot collide. Removes the category filter from the portal's `failreasons` fetch and from `failPoints()` in `checklistForm.ts`. Refines AD-096, which had scoped the block per team. | Project owner 2026-09-09: "Fail quality reasons (Both Tax and AQS)". The V8 document draws one undivided twenty-row table; the `AML` / `Breach` / `Record Keeping` / `Tax check` prefixes group its rows and say nothing about who may tick them. The previous split was an inference from those prefixes and lost data at the edge: a Tax reviewer who found a record-keeping failure had nowhere to record it, and an AQS reviewer could not record a tax failure. No server rule changed - `ResponseGuardPlugin` enforces the submission lock on a fail-reason association but never checked the team, so this was a page-level filter with nothing behind it. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-101 | **The checklist renders the document's own casing, order and box shape.** Where the Checker Checklist draws an outcome scale inline rather than as a tick column it sets it in upper case (`PASS  FAIL`, `YES  NO`, the four-value grade) and orders the tax check outcome PASS, INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE, FAIL; the same values stay title case where they head a grid column. Primary root cause renders as the document's 3x3 grid. Every option box is a square, single-select included - the input stays `type="radio"` so the browser enforces one-of and is restyled square. Every checklist cell is ruled. Only "Outcome lens:" is emphasised on a lens row. The per-answer "Evidence or observation" box is removed: it was never on the form. `outcome-testing.css` gains an `@media print` block so "Save as PDF" produces the document rather than the page chrome. | Project owner 2026-09-09: "Ensure that the checklist form matches what we have in the pdf exactly", "remove the Evidence or observation fields on every fail as it is not on the form or a requirement", and the Power Pages build spec supplied the same day (its §0 rule 1 and §6). The casing is contextual in the source, so it belongs to the rendering rather than to the option, and only the inline path applies it. Seed corrections in the same change: S-E4's name to a hyphen, S-CD's help text to the full sentence including the broken "section H" reference (reproduced verbatim per the spec's §8.3 rather than repaired), and Q-CRP-04's `al_Name` to the document's full wording. | 2026-09-09 |

| AD-102 | **E2's outcome lens tick box is built, as a question, and it is the one non-mandatory answerable question in the checklist.** Seeded as `Q-E2-LENS` (`YesNo`, display order 4 in S-E2). Both review pages render it as a single box on the lens row itself - not as a test point of its own, and not as a Yes / No pair - because that is what the document draws: ticked records Yes, untouched records nothing. Recognised by the `-LENS` code suffix rather than by section, so a second could be seeded without a code change. `FormRow` and `QuestionRef` gain `code` to carry it. | Project owner 2026-09-09: "E2's stray lens tick box: build", reversing the earlier reading of it as a formatting artefact (AD-096 era) and matching the build spec's §8.2. Not mandatory, against AD-019: a lone attestation box that had to be ticked before the section could be submitted would not be a judgement, it would be a toll gate. Modelled as a question rather than a column so the tick is a versioned, recorded answer like every other, and needs no schema change. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-103 | **A fail reason's `al_Name` holds the document's whole row, category prefix included, and is rendered as stored.** "AML - ID verification issue" is the name; the label is no longer built from `al_Category` plus a separator, in the app (`failPoints`), the portal, or `Remediation.NonPassItems`. `al_Category` remains the grouping, for MI and ordering. | Project owner 2026-09-09: "Fail point 20's en-dash: build". The document does not punctuate its twenty rows consistently - rows 1-19 take a hyphen, row 20 an en-dash, and the two Tax check rows run on in lower case - so a label composed from a category label and one separator cannot reproduce it. The alternatives were a per-reason separator column, which is a schema change for punctuation, or storing the row as written, which is none. The category prefix now appearing in `al_Name` is duplication of a sort, but it is the document's row and the document is the authority on it. | 2026-09-09 |

| AD-104 | **The reference build file is checked into the repository and the checklist is tested against it, not against a transcription of it.** `docs/reference/checker-checklist.html` is the Power Pages build reference the project owner supplied: the form's headings, its section splitting, its column headings, its inline option casing and every repeating row, the last generated from data arrays at the foot of the file. `app/src/features/reviews/checklistDocument.test.ts` reads that file and `data/v8-seed/data.xml` and asserts that the blocks `formBlocks()` builds off the seed are the blocks the document draws - titles in order, intro lines, grid column headings and tick labels, the E1-E5 subsection headings, each outcome lens and which lens carries a tick, every question's text, the inline option runs, and all twenty fail reasons verbatim. Two differences are asserted as deliberate rather than left to drift: Remediation and escalation is not a block of this form (AD-095), and the case header opens it (checklist-v8.md). | The form had been reported as not matching the document three times (AD-098, AD-100, and again 2026-09-09), each time found by reading the two side by side and each time fixed only where someone happened to look. The document is machine-readable, so the comparison does not need a person: making the reference file the executable spec turns "does this still match?" from a review into a test run. It found one live defect on its first run - S-E1's outcome lens was seeded with an ASCII apostrophe where the document sets U+2019, the seed having reproduced the document's other non-ASCII punctuation (fail reason 20's en-dash) faithfully. | 2026-09-09 |

| AD-105 | **The portal's questionnaire iterates sections outer and that section's questions inner, and no loop variable is named `block`.** Two faults, both in `OT Review Detail`, both found from the project owner's Tax and AQS portal PDFs of 2026-09-09. (a) The questions fetch asks for section-then-question order with two root-level `order` elements carrying `entityname`. The SDK honours that; the page's Liquid fetch applied only the question order, so every section's question 1 came back before any section's question 2 and the form arrived interleaved. The rows are now grouped against a separate `al_section` fetch ordered by a plain root-entity order, matched on section code, so the sequence no longer depends on cross-entity ordering working at all. (b) The loop's block key was a variable named `block` - a reserved word in this engine, since the whole template body sits inside `block content` under `extends 'OT Layout'`. Assignments to it did not stick, so it never varied: the block boundary fired once for the entire form and the fail points block, which hangs off `block == 'S-FQ'`, never rendered. It is `blk` now, with `blk_title`, `blk_layout`, `blk_rt`, `blk_subsections` and `current_blk` renamed with it. `scode | default: sid` became an explicit if at the same time, for legibility rather than because the filter was proven at fault: the same template's summary card uses `default` half a dozen times and renders correctly, and the `block` collision explains the symptom on its own. | The Code App was correct throughout and the portal was not, which is what made this look like caching or a stale deployment for two rounds; the live template was byte-identical to the repository and the DEV data was correct, so neither was the cause. What settled it was the owner's PDFs: the row sequence in them is question-display-order across every section, and there is exactly one heading and no fail points block on either discipline - which is the signature of a block key that never changes. Both faults are engine behaviour that a Liquid renderer other than the portal's does not reproduce, so neither would have been caught by rendering the template locally; they are recorded here because the same two traps sit in wait for any future template. | 2026-09-09 |

| AD-106 | **A remediation action's prepopulated issue list is drawn from the pass/fail test points only - Pass / Fail and Pass / Fail / Insufficient evidence - and from the ticked File Quality fail points. The yes/no scales and the grade are excluded.** `Remediation.IsNonPassAnswer` now takes the question's response type as well as the answer, and `NonPassItems` reads `al_responsetype` off the question version to apply it. A No on an AML and CRA checking point or a Consumer Duty outcome no longer becomes a line the adviser is asked to put right, and neither does an Insufficient evidence recorded on Consumer Duty. The ticked fail points are unchanged and are still read across every answer on the review whatever that answer was, so the AML, Breach and Record Keeping failures still reach the action through the fail point list, which is where the document puts them. | Project owner, 2026-09-09: remediation "is its own standalone form under remediation prefilled with fail responses", and "this only applies to PASS INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE FAIL or just pass and fail, not yes or no questions". The scale has to decide this rather than the answer, because Insufficient evidence is one option value (120910302) shared by the suitability scale and the Consumer Duty Yes / No / Insufficient evidence scale - the answer alone cannot tell a suitability test point from a Consumer Duty judgement. The grade is excluded for a different reason: it is already the reason the action gives, so listing it as an issue repeats it. **This is a behaviour change in the plug-in assembly and needs a push to take effect.** `IsNonPassAnswer` is called only by `NonPassItems`, so when a remediation action is raised at all (BR-006, driven by `OutcomeRules`) is untouched - only what its "Issue / fail reason" is prepopulated with. | 2026-09-09 |
| AD-107 | **A review raises one remediation action per thing the checker marked down, not one action carrying a list.** Actions are coded `REM-<case>-<sequence>-<n>` (`Remediation.ActionCode` gains an index overload); a review whose item list is empty keeps the un-indexed code it has always had, so nothing already raised is renamed. Each action carries the full provenance in its own description, so a row read straight out of Dataverse still says where it came from; the three renderers print that provenance once per run of actions sharing it rather than under every row. Replay safety is **per item**: an item already raised is returned as it stands and is not written again. Three consequences were fixed in the same change rather than left to surface later. (i) **Completing an action no longer completes the remediation** — `CompleteRemediationPlugin.AnyOutstanding` holds the case at `Remediation In Progress` and moves it to `Awaiting Sign-off` only when nothing on it is outstanding; without this the first item finished put the whole case in front of the T&C Manager with the rest untouched. (ii) **`SubmitReviewPlugin.RemediationApproved` now requires every action on the review to carry an approved sign-off**, not any one of them, or approving the first would open the OD-038 AQS gate on an unremediated file. (iii) **The `Remediation assigned` notification is keyed on the review rather than the action**, so a submission raising five actions sends one email — the outbox code derives from the target, so the later creates hit the alternate key and are treated as already queued, while a later review is a different target and still tells the adviser. Migration for actions raised before this: the `splitremediation` verb in `plugins/OutcomeTesting.Registration`. Resolves OD-040; supersedes the one-action half of AD-097, whose reading of *what* prepopulates an action (AD-106) is unchanged. | The agreed remediation form carries a remedial action, an owner, a target date and a sign-off **against every numbered row**, and all four are single-valued columns on `al_remediationaction`. One action holding the whole item list could therefore render the rows but never let the adviser answer them one at a time, which is the substance of requirements pack §6.1 and §6.3 — and §6.1's seven per-item fields cannot live in a text description at all. AD-097 had kept one action precisely because completion, sign-off and the OD-038 gate all count actions; that reasoning was correct, which is why the three counting rules are changed here in the same change rather than discovered afterwards. Built 2026-09-10 in `d8ee4c4`, tooling in `efb4636`. **Recorded 2026-09-10, after the fact:** the build landed across six commits that logged no decision entry, and this project's rule is that a decision of this size is not carried by a commit message. | 2026-09-10 |

| AD-108 | **The review's outcome is the remediation's Outcome, not one of its numbered issues, and the description's standing sentence and checker observation are not drawn at all.** `Remediation.NonPassItems` leaves out the three questions that record a result - Q-TAX-02 (Tax check outcome), Q-FQ-01 and Q-FQTAX-01 (File quality outcome) - for the reason the grade scale was already left out. The outcome is read back out of the description's standing sentence (`...submitted (Tax check: Insufficient evidence).`) and shown as an Outcome field beside Client, Adviser and Case on the portal's remediation page and case record, and in the Code App. The standing sentence and the checker's observation are read past by every renderer; both stay in `al_description`, which is the record Dataverse keeps and the only place the outcome is written down. A case whose only non-pass answer was its outcome now lists no items, and `Raise` falls to the single un-indexed action it has always raised for a grade with no test point behind it. `dropoutcomeactions` is the backfill: it deletes an already-raised action whose one item is an outcome, strips the item list instead when it is the case's only action, and leaves alone any an adviser has touched. | Project owner, 2026-09-10: "Tax check outcome: Insufficient evidence should not be in the fail/issue table as it is the outcome, and everything else highlights the outcome reason", then "remove this text on both platforms" of the standing sentence. The outcome is the result every other row is a reason for, so numbering it among them read as one more thing the adviser had to put right - the same objection AD-095 already recorded against listing the grade. No schema change was needed because the reason has been written into every description since remediation was first raised. | 2026-09-10 |
| AD-109 | **A plug-in never absorbs an `OrganizationService` fault and carries on; `NotificationOutbox.Queue` reads the outbox before writing.** The platform aborts the whole transaction of a plug-in that catches a fault from a service call and continues - "ISV code reduced the open transaction count" - which `OptionLabels` had already recorded for its metadata read. `Queue` was letting a duplicate create fail on the `al_notificationcode` alternate key and swallowing the fault; it now queries the code first and returns without writing when the row exists. A race between two transactions can still lose to the key, and that fault is left to propagate. | The swallow was harmless while a transaction only ever queued one notification: the duplicate arose on a replayed submit, which is a fresh transaction. AD-107's one-action-per-item changed that - `NotificationEmitterPlugin` fires on each create and the outbox code is keyed on the review, so the second create collided inside the same transaction and the platform killed the work. It broke portal review submission (HTTP 400, Common Data Service error 9004010D) and any case edit that reassigned several unassigned actions, which is how the project owner found it: "the edit error seems to be when I change the Adviser on a case that is already in the remediation stage". Diagnosed 2026-09-10 after AD-110 made the class of failure visible. | 2026-09-10 |
| AD-110 | **`PluginBase` wraps every exception it catches as `InvalidPluginExecutionException`, prefixed `UNEXPECTED:` and naming the plug-in and the error but never the stack.** Only `FaultException<OrganizationServiceFault>` was caught before, so every other exception type left the pipeline unclassified. Power Pages returns an `InvalidPluginExecutionException`'s message to the browser and reports anything else as "A Common Data Service error occurred", code 9004010D, carrying no detail at all - so a fault in any plug-in on any write reached the user as a page saying nothing, and the pages' branching on `PRECONDITION:` / `CONFLICT:` / `UNAUTHORIZED:` could never fire. The stack stays in the trace log and `InnerException`, where PP-16 wants it. The five portal handlers read their message out of the OData error body rather than scanning raw JSON for a prefix, and show what the server said where they cannot classify a refusal; the Code App classifies `UNEXPECTED` alongside the rest. An unclassified cause is still not shown - it is infrastructure text and has carried a host and an internal address before now (NFR-OBS-01). `PluginBase`'s constructor is `protected` rather than `internal` so the pipeline's exception path can be tested. | Project owner, 2026-09-10, on a submit that refused with only "We could not submit this review... quoting status 400": "Ensure all errors show non-generic messages and actual error messages". The message named neither the cause nor anything support could quote, and the cause was being discarded by the platform before any of our code saw it. | 2026-09-10 |
| AD-111 | **The portal regrade is a trigger column on the supervisor's own contact, and the regrade's rules move into `RegradeCasePlugin.Regrade` so both front ends call one method.** The page PATCHes `contact.al_regraderequest` (JSON: outcomeId, finalOutcome, reason, expectedRowVersion) through the Self-scoped contact permission; the synchronous post-operation `RegradeRequestPlugin` (Update of contact, filtered on the column) checks the contact's web roles for `AL Portal - T&C Supervisor`, clears the column, and calls `Regrade`. The Custom API `al_RegradeCase` calls the same method after the platform has checked the caller's privileges. The control sits beside the sign-off on `OT Remediation`, offers the four BR-005 grades and a mandatory reason, and renders wherever the case carries a graded outcome. | Resolves OD-041. A Custom API cannot be invoked from a Power Pages page at all, so the regrade was reachable only from the Code App while the sign-off that leads to it was a portal act — a supervisor holding only the portal could approve every action, move the case to Awaiting Recheck, and then have no way to set the final outcome that closes it. This is the fourth browser-driven write on the site and it takes the shape the first three settled on (AD-053 answers, AD-076 claims, AD-099 sign-off), for the same reason each time: a Power Pages write arrives as the site's application user, so authorization cannot be a caller check (AD-053) and the contact's web role, checked server-side against the row actually written, is the boundary. Extracting `Regrade` rather than reimplementing it is what keeps the mandatory reason (AD-031), the preserved initial outcome (BR-007), the refusal to regrade an ungraded outcome, the audit event and the Awaiting Recheck close identical on both paths. `ExpectedRowVersion` is carried from the page's own read so a regrade against a stale view is refused rather than overwriting another correction; where the portal cannot read `versionnumber` the check is simply absent, which is how the command already behaves when the parameter is omitted. The control is not hidden by role: hiding it in Liquid would look like access control and enforce nothing (NFR-SEC-01), so it renders for anyone and a user without the role is refused on save. | 2026-09-10 |
| AD-112 | **A claim asks the route which discipline is due before it looks for a review instance, and confines the search to that discipline.** `ClaimCasePlugin.ResolveOrCreateReview` calls `NextDiscipline` first and then finds or creates the unsubmitted instance for the discipline it names; the pre-route behaviour — the earliest unsubmitted instance whatever its discipline — is kept only for a case carrying no `al_reviewrouteid`, which `NextDiscipline` refuses outright. | Closes the BR-004 defect found on IO-SEED-AQS-04 on 2026-09-10. Reading "the earliest unsubmitted instance" as the check that is due only holds while the route has not changed since the instances were created, and a route changes whenever a case is edited: `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.DeriveRoute` re-derives it from the tax-check answer and the Tax team's disposition. That case was seeded AQS-only and allocated, opening an AQS instance at sequence 2, then edited to require a Tax check — so its route became Tax-then-AQS while its only instance stayed AQS. The consequences compounded: the Tax check the route now required was never created, so it appeared nowhere on the case and the portal showed the AQS check alone; the AQS check was open and fully answerable but its submit is refused by `SubmitReviewPlugin.EnsureTaxPrecedesAqs` until Tax is in; and because the premature AQS instance already carried an assigned contact, a claim was refused as "another checker has already started checks" before `NextDiscipline` was ever consulted — so no Tax checker could pick the case up either. The case could not progress by any route. BR-004 itself was never breached in the data: no AQS outcome can be recorded before Tax, and the server-side guard was verified to refuse. The defect is a deadlock, not a bypass. | 2026-09-10 |
| AD-113 | **The self-service queue is AQS only, it names no checker, and the edit modal's checker controls follow the route's order with no exception.** Three changes to one direction (project owner, 2026-09-10). (a) `OT Review List` renders the "Available cases" card only when `reviewtype` is AQS — a Tax check is allocated to a named checker by the Tax lead and is never picked up, so the Tax page carries no queue and the queue's `fetchxml` is skipped there rather than rendered empty. The Tax branch of the queue filter is deleted; the AQS branch is unchanged, so a Tax-then-AQS case still reaches the queue for its AQS leg once its Tax review is submitted. `can_claim` is assigned only inside the AQS guard, which is what also withholds the claim script and dialog from the Tax page. (b) The queue drops its **Checker** column, and its caption no longer says the cases in it have no checker. (c) `caseCheckers.isAllocatable` no longer treats an existing review instance as an exception to the route's ordering: a discipline is offered when it is the one the case owes next and its check is unsubmitted, and otherwise stated. `CaseEditPanel` names who holds a withheld check instead of calling it "not open yet", which it may not be. | (a) resolves the report that the Tax page offered IO-000126 and IO-000128 out of a queue Tax work never uses. (b) resolves the same report's second half — that the two cases were "assigned". They were not: neither carried an assignment row or a review instance and both sat at `Queued`, verified against DEV. What made them read as assigned is that `al_checkername` is the upload file's "Checker name" column (`ImportRules`) as much as it is the allocated checker (`AssignCasePlugin`, `ClaimCasePlugin`), so a case nobody has ever been given still carries a person's name — IO-000127 carries one at Ready for Allocation, never having been allocated. Printing that under a caption promising "no checker" made unclaimed work read as somebody else's. The name is case data and stays on the case record; what decides availability is the status and the absence of a claim, which is what the filter already asks. (c) resolves the report that the AQS Checker control appeared on some Tax-then-AQS cases and not others. Whether the AQS review instance happened to exist was the whole of the difference: a case routed AQS only, given an AQS review, then answered Yes to Tax check required carries one while BR-004 still owes Tax first, and the control appeared. The exception was added on 2026-09-10 so a started check could be reallocated, and that survives — the check a case owes next is offered opened or not, so an open Tax leg stays reallocatable. Only a check the route has not reached is withheld, which is also what stops `AssignCasePlugin.ResolveReviewInstance` putting an AQS choice on the Tax leg when the app sends no `ReviewInstanceId`. **Not a defect:** the AQS queue showing nothing. Verified against DEV that the filter admits a Tax-then-AQS case whose Tax review is submitted; it returns no rows because no case is at `Queued` and AQS-eligible — every AQS-eligible case is Assigned, Awaiting Sign-off or Awaiting Recheck. The empty state now says so rather than claiming every queued case has a checker. Amends AD-076, which built the button on both pages. | 2026-09-10 |
| AD-114 | **The remediation of each check is its own remediation: signed off as a whole, scoped to the review instance, and collapsed off the form once approved.** Three changes (project owner, 2026-09-10). (a) `SignoffProgressPlugin.MoveCase` takes the check the signed-off action belongs to and refuses to advance the case while any action on that check is still undecided (`AnyAwaitingSignoff`); an action counts as decided once a sign-off row exists against it, since `SignoffGuardPlugin` refuses a second. A rejection is unaffected — it returns the case to Awaiting Remediation at once, because that is the whole check going back. (b) `CompleteRemediationPlugin.AnyOutstanding` is scoped the same way, so the leg reaching Awaiting Sign-off does not wait on the other leg's actions. Both fall back to the whole case where the action carries no `al_reviewinstanceid`, which is what rows written before that link existed look like. (c) `OT Remediation` groups the numbered rows by check, names the check above its run of rows, and **omits a check whose every action has been approved**, saying under the table what was settled and when. | (a) is a defect found on 2026-09-10 while checking IO-300005, and it is independent of the redesign: `MoveCase` ran on every sign-off but only tested that the case was *at* Awaiting Sign-off, so the **first** approved sign-off moved the case and every later one found it already moved and silently did nothing. That case's AQS check carried eighteen actions; approving one of the eighteen sent the case to Awaiting Recheck. It had gone unnoticed because the sign-offs were made in a batch seconds apart, so the end state looked right. The completion side had been given exactly this gate earlier the same day — its comment reads "the first item finished would put the whole case in front of the T&C Manager with the rest untouched" — and this is that rule one step later. (b) and (c) answer the project owner's "a case with a remediation for both Tax and AQS should be treated as separate remediations instead of stacking up the forms and fail reasons". The data already carried the distinction: `Remediation.Raise` stamps `al_reviewinstanceid` on every action, and IO-300005's twenty-three rows were correctly five against the Tax check and eighteen against the AQS check. Only the reading was case-wide — the form drew all twenty-three as one numbered list, and both gates counted actions across the case. Scoping them is what makes the two legs independent rather than merely displayed apart. Collapsing an approved check rather than showing it read-only is the project owner's call: what is left on the form is the remediation in hand, and the case record keeps the rest. **Case status stays single-valued**, which BR-004's ordering makes workable — the legs run in sequence (Tax remediation, sign-off, back to `Queued`, AQS, sign-off, `Awaiting Recheck`) — and the scoping is what stops one leg's actions being counted against the other's gate when a rejection puts both in play. Revises the transition timing AD-057 describes: a case now leaves Awaiting Sign-off when a check's remediation is fully decided, not on its first approval. | 2026-09-10 |

| AD-115 | **Every table a web template queries must carry a read permission, and the release gate asserts it.** `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1` gains an eleventh assertion: it scans every `*.webtemplate.source.html` for `<entity>` and `<link-entity>` names — stripping `{% comment %}` blocks first, so a table named in prose is not counted — and fails when one has no table permission granting read. Many-to-many intersect tables are exempt, because Power Pages permissions those through each end of the relationship rather than in their own right, and both ends are asserted in their own right anyway. A scan finding no templates, or no table references at all, fails as a broken scan rather than passing as a clean site, the same guard shape assertion 3 already had. The permission the assertion was missing is added with it: `Review Route - read` (`al_reviewroute`, Global, read only, Administrators and Authenticated Users, id `…063`), which makes sixteen. | Table permissions fail closed, so a table with no permission does not error — the query returns nothing and the feature built on it renders as though there were no data. Nothing in the gate's other ten assertions notices, and it has now gone wrong twice. `al_outcome` was queried by the Home outcome tiles, the case list grade column, the case detail, the regrade panel and the sign-off final outcome with no permission at all: every one drew empty, and AD-111's regrade panel was reported as built and deployed for as long as it had never once rendered. `al_reviewroute` was the same shape and worse, because the AQS claim queue in `OT Review List` joins it `link-type="inner"` — an unreadable table in an outer join blanks a column, but in an inner join it collapses the result set, so the queue was empty for every user and **no AQS checker could claim a case through the portal at all**. Case IO-300002 was sitting in that queue, invisible, when this was found. The route filter on the case and review lists was inert for the same reason, failing safe to "no filter" because the page validates the requested route id against a list that was always empty. Both faults were invisible to every existing check: the gates passed, source and DEV matched byte-for-byte, and the data was correct. Found 2026-09-11 while auditing permissions, relationships and role visibility end to end. | 2026-09-11 |
| AD-116 | **A web template's deployed `content` is verified against source by query, not assumed from a passing gate.** `OT Review List` was found on 2026-09-11 holding a **null** `content` column in DEV — not drift, an empty record — last modified 2026-09-10 15:15. Restored from the repository with `pushwebtemplate`, and all sixteen `OT *` templates then verified byte-identical to source. | `/tax-reviews` and `/aqs-reviews` are both drawn by that one shared template, so both pages were rendering the layout around an empty body. Nothing reported it: `Check-ComponentIds.ps1` and `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1` read the repository, where the 31KB source was intact, and `Deploy-Portal.ps1 -VerifyOnly` checks that table permissions exist, not that a template still has a body. The near-miss is the part worth recording — `src/powerpagecomponents/…016` still held the good copy only because the last DEV→`src` round-trip predated the blanking; a round-trip run after 15:15 on 2026-09-10 would have carried the null into source control and the content would have been lost outright. A round-trip copies what DEV holds, including damage. | 2026-09-11 |

| AD-117 | **A portal write whose effects a plug-in makes on other tables cannot promise the reload will show them, and the message says so.** The three writes on `OT Remediation` — sign-off from the row panel, sign-off from the decision panel, and the regrade — each PATCH a trigger column and then reloaded after 800ms. The delay is now 2500ms and each success message carries the caveat that the page may not have caught up. `.ot-field textarea` is styled in the same change: `.ot-field input, .ot-field select` carried the border, padding and background and `textarea` was never added to the selector, so the regrade **Reason** and the sign-off **Notes** drew as unmarked areas of page. The `.ot-checklist` textareas stay borderless — that component replicates a paper form. | Reported against IO-300005 on 2026-09-11: a regrade "showed that it was done successfully, but the status did not change". It had fully succeeded — `al_finaloutcome` Pass, `al_regradedon` and `al_finalisedon` stamped, case `Closed`, audit event `portal-regrade-36d49fef…` written, initial outcome preserved — and the reload re-rendered every value unchanged. The rows a regrade writes are `al_outcome` and `al_outcomecase`, and **the portal did not write them**: it wrote `contact.al_regraderequest`, and `RegradeRequestPlugin` did the rest. So the portal's own render cache is not invalidated by the write path, and it learns through Dataverse change tracking (AD-094) — which is polled and eventually consistent. Change tracking makes the portal *able* to notice, not synchronous about it, and it was verified enabled on all eleven portal-read tables while this was being diagnosed. A longer delay narrows the window and cannot close it, which is why the fix is in what the page claims rather than only in the timing. **Not a fault, checked in the same pass:** the regrade panel still rendering on a closed case is the AD-031 correction path, which `RegradeCasePlugin` supports deliberately; and "Every issue raised on this case has been remediated and approved" replacing the numbered rows is AD-114(c) collapsing a check whose actions are all approved. | 2026-09-11 |

| AD-118 | **A settled check keeps its numbered rows. The collapse in AD-114(c) is reversed; the approval is named in the check's heading instead.** `OT Remediation` draws every action on the case, settled or not, with `· every action approved, closed` appended to the heading of a check whose actions are all approved and that heading muted.
