The `ot_drawn == 0` fallback ("Every issue raised on this case has been remediated and approved") is removed with the collapse that produced it, and the note under the table changes from "Settled and not shown" to "Closed", since the rows it refers to are now above it. The reset that exempted a one-check case from collapsing goes too: `ot_settled` is a label now, not a filter, and a single approved check should still say it is closed. | Project owner, 2026-09-11: "The supervisor needs to see the issue/fail reason records as well to have visibility of the remedial actions." AD-114(c) collapsed a check whose every action had been approved, which on a case where **all** checks are approved left the form with no numbered rows at all — IO-300005 showed the fallback sentence and nothing else, so the T&C Supervisor recording the final outcome could not see the issue or fail reason behind any of the twenty-three remedial actions they were signing the case off on. The collapse's original purpose stands as a reading of the form — AD-114(c) was answering "stacking up the forms and fail reasons" — but it was traded against the sign-off's own evidence, and the sign-off wins. Cheap to reverse because nothing in those rows is an input: the adviser's answers and the sign-off and regrade controls are separate panels below, so a settled row was already read-only and the only question was whether it was drawn. Supersedes the collapse half of AD-114(c); the per-check scoping of `MoveCase` and `AnyOutstanding` in (a) and (b) is untouched and is what still keeps the two legs independent. | 2026-09-11 |

| AD-119 | **An outcome grade is rendered through `OT Outcome Label`, never through `.label` on the option set, and the stylesheet's version querystring is bumped on every CSS change.** The four remaining raw reads — the Regraded outcome cell and the regrade panel's hint on `OT Remediation`, and the Regraded outcome row on `OT Case Detail` — now go through the include, passed `final` alone so they stay the *regraded* grade rather than falling back to the initial one under the shared precedence rule. `OT Layout` moves to `?v=11`, and its comment now says to bump on **every** change rather than a "material" one. | Reported 2026-09-11 on IO-300005: "the regrade outcome on the portal still shows nothing. But shows the updated status on the app". The regrade had recorded correctly — `al_finaloutcome` Pass, case Closed, audit event written — and the portal drew the cell **blank rather than as a dash**, which is the signature: `{% if outcome.al_finaloutcome %}` passes on the object, and `.label` yields an empty string because the platform does not always supply a formatted value for an option set. `OT Outcome Label` exists for exactly this and carries a numeric fallback — its own comment records that "an aliased column from a link-entity does not always carry its formatted value" — but only `OT Case List` was using it, so the two pages that matter most to a supervisor closing a case were the two reading raw. The Code App reads Dataverse directly and so was never affected, which is what the report's second half was saying. **The CSS half is a self-inflicted repeat:** a border fix for the Reason and Notes fields was deployed twice and reported as not showing both times, because the version querystring the layout carries for this exact purpose was not bumped. A deploy succeeds either way and the browser is never told, so the omission is indistinguishable from a rule that does not work. The word "materially" in the instruction is what made skipping it feel safe; it is gone. | 2026-09-11 |

| AD-120 | **The release gate asserts that every web template parses: no real Liquid tag inside a `{% comment %}` block, and no unbalanced block tag.** Assertion 12 in `Check-PortalSecurity.ps1`, checked by walking the tags in order rather than counting them. | `OT Remediation` shipped with `{% if outcome.al_finaloutcome %}` written inside a comment as an illustration, and the remediation page answered **unknown tag 'endcomment'**. DotLiquid tokenises tags inside a comment block, so the unclosed `if` consumed the `{% endcomment %}`; the error names a tag that is not the problem, which is what makes it expensive to find. The templates already wrote `(% if %)` in prose for exactly this reason — the convention was real and undocumented, so it was followed by imitation until someone added a comment without noticing. Both gates passed and the upload succeeded, because neither reads the template as Liquid. The counting approach is specifically rejected: opens against closes balances on a file whose tags interleave wrongly, and stripping comments with a regex first pairs the first `{% comment %}` with the first `{% endcomment %}` and conceals the fault — that is how it was missed by hand before the gate existed. Introduced and fixed the same day, 2026-09-11, while fixing AD-119. | 2026-09-11 |

| AD-121 | **A `.ot-field` control keeps its own styling inside `.ot-checklist`, by specificity rather than by source order.** The three `.ot-field` form-control rules also list `.ot-checklist .ot-field ...`, taking them to (0,3,0). `?v=12`. | The regrade **Reason** and sign-off **Notes** boxes still drew bare after AD-119, and gained a border only on click. `OT Remediation` wraps its whole form in `.ot-checklist` (line 400 to 1041, so both panels are inside it), and that component deliberately strips its own fields — `border: 0`, transparent background, `padding: 1px 0` — because it replicates a paper form. `.ot-checklist textarea` and `.ot-field textarea` both matched the Reason box at **identical specificity (0,2,0)**, and the checklist block sits later in the stylesheet, so it won. What looked like a border appearing on click was only the focus outline, which is why the field seemed half-styled rather than unstyled. Naming `.ot-checklist` in the `.ot-field` selectors settles it by specificity, so the answer no longer depends on where the blocks sit in the file; the checklist's own paper-form inputs carry no `.ot-field` and are untouched. The first fix (AD-117) added `textarea` to a selector that was already losing, and the second (AD-119) bumped the cache version so the losing rule finally reached the browser — which is what made the real cause visible. | 2026-09-11 |
| AD-122 | **Checklist content is administered in the checklist version already in force, not by reissuing the checklist.** Adding, retiring or moving a question writes into the published version, carrying an effective-from date the administrator chooses. Six server-side commands (AD-003) do the writing; `src/` is never hand-edited. Eight question codes are load-bearing in compiled C# - `Q-GR-01`, `Q-TAX-02`, `Q-FQ-01`, `Q-FQTAX-01`, `Q-FQ-02`, `Q-FQTAX-02`, `Q-FQ-03`, `Q-FQTAX-03` - and retiring or moving one is refused by name. | Amends AD-016 and PP-09. AD-016 reads strictly as requiring a new checklist version for any structural change, but its concern is the rewriting of history, and effective dating already answers that concern in full: every read path is date-scoped and a submitted review reads as of its submission day (AD-091), so a row added today cannot change what any submitted review said. Reissuing a version would mean cloning roughly a hundred rows with tooling that does not exist, to buy a protection the date columns already give. The cost that is real is accepted and designed for: a mandatory question in force today is owed by every unsubmitted review of that discipline at its next submit, which is why every command exposes the effective-from date. PP-09's "administered outside the portal" stays true - this is the Code App, not the portal. The protected-code guard exists because retiring `Q-GR-01` stops every case being graded and retiring `Q-FQ-01` makes `GenerateExportPlugin` refuse the Trail Light row; neither degrades gracefully. | 2026-09-11 |
| AD-123 | **A section carries effective dates and an optional flag, and may be owned by Tax, AQS or Both.** `al_section` gains `al_effectivefrom`, `al_effectiveto` (both DateOnly and nullable) and `al_isoptional`; `al_ownerrole` gains **Both = 120910105**. `SectionRules` holds the two rules and both plug-ins call it. A section out of force renders nowhere and is owed nothing; an optional section renders, marked, and is owed nothing; a Both section is rendered to and owed by each discipline, which answers its own copy because responses hang off the review instance. | Amends AD-019 and AD-020. AD-019 made every displayed question mandatory as a structural rule; an optional section is the exception, and it is a section-level flag rather than a bulk edit of per-question flags so that a question added later inherits it. AD-020 named Tax and AQS as section owners and already described the File Quality fail points as owned by "both" - this gives that word a value. **Owner role was matched by strict equality in five places**, every one of which had to become membership or a Both section would half-work: `ResponseGuardPlugin` (which would refuse every answer to a section that rendered correctly), the `SubmitReviewPlugin` mandatory query, two portal FetchXML conditions, and `useReviewDetail`. Dates are nullable so the 12 existing sections need no backfill - a null effective-from already means in force. `al_isoptional` is null rather than false on those rows, because Dataverse applies a boolean default to new rows only, so every reader treats absent as required and a query written `al_isoptional eq false` matches nothing. | 2026-09-12 |
| AD-124 | **The Intelligent Office task extract replaces the upload template, one task is one case, and the route comes from the paraplanner's checklist.** The `.xlsx` Pre-Advice Check task report is now the import format (`data/io-task-extract-sample.csv` is it transcribed). `TaskID` is the key, so two tasks on one service case are two cases; `ImportRules.Columns` and `caseUpload.ts` are rewritten under IO's own header names; `al_outcomecase` gains 17 columns, among them `al_iooutcome`/`al_iocompletedby`/`al_iocompleteddate` for the outcome the extract already carried, and `al_checklistitems`/`al_checklistcompletedby`/`al_checklistcompleteddate` for the selection. The Code App transcribes the workbook to CSV and posts it to the unchanged `al_ImportCases`, so AD-003's boundary is untouched: it resolves date serials using the cell styles and maps nothing. Two new BR-002 rejections — a row with no selected checklist item, and a stamped item whose name is not recognised. | Direction from the project owner and the client, 2026-09-11 and 2026-09-12. BR-001 always said "Excel extracts"; the 18-column CSV template was a stand-in derived from `checklist-v8.md` and existed nowhere in IO. Of its columns only five survive. **`DeriveRoute` is deliberately unchanged**: the client's rule (Tax or Trust Documentation means Tax; any other item means AQS; both start at Tax and the Tax team decides whether AQS is owed) is already exactly what `al_taxcheckrequired` plus the disposition override express, so the import sets that column and the existing engine does the rest. **The checklist is read by name and only where a completion stamp is present**, never by column number: it is not yet confirmed whether IO packs a partial selection into the first free slots or lists every item in fixed slots and stamps the chosen ones, and this reading is correct either way — the open question confirms the reader rather than shaping it. The two Tax items accept both the client's wording ("Tax", "Trust documentation") and the workbook's ("Tax Check", "Trust Documentation Check"). An unrecognised name **fails the row** rather than routing on the items that were recognised, which is what keeps that open question from changing routing silently. Rejecting a row with no checklist is deliberate for the same reason: `DeriveRoute` returns early on a null tax answer, so such a case would be created at Imported with no route and nothing downstream to surface it — `IOA07028411` in the supplied workbook is exactly this. The IO outcome is stored but is **not** the grade (BR-005 stays with the checker), and the client's address, postcode, phone and email are **not imported** pending confirmation the checking process needs them. The thirteen columns that lose their source are kept for manual entry rather than dropped. **Amended the same day, on the client's answer that the header fields stay and are completed by hand:** `AssignedBy` feeds `al_paraplanner` (it is the paraplanner who raised the task) rather than a column of its own, so `al_assignedby` is dropped; the eleven remaining orphaned columns are kept and typed in, which `CaseEditPanel` and `al_UpdateCaseDetails` already support; and **Tax Reviewer and AQS Reviewer move from `page.cases` View to Edit** in `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS`, because at View the panel renders read-only and the command refuses the write, so the two roles named as completing those fields could not. That grant is the whole page, `al_casestatus`, `al_priority`, `al_duedate` and `al_taxcheckrequired` included, and the last of those re-derives the route; a narrower `case.header` key was offered and not taken. `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` is a seed, not a migration -- stored rules replace it wholesale, so an environment that already holds permission rows needs the two Edit rules added through the Security configuration screen. Note the checklist stamp names the task's `AssignedTo`, not `AssignedBy`, so `al_checklistcompletedby` is not the paraplanner and is described as what it is. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-12-io-task-extract-import-design.md`. **Open with the client and not blocking:** whether AQS confirms both teams' remedial actions (which would contradict AD-114), ~~IO's exact item wording~~ **(answered 2026-09-20: the real extract stamps only `Tax Check`, `Trust Documentation Check`, `High Risk Item 1`, `High Risk Item 2`, `Enhanced Supervision`, `Pre-CAS Adviser` and `Leaver` - all seven already recognised, now pinned by a test)**, the route for a checklist-less task, and whether completed rows should be sent at all. | 2026-09-12 |


## Recording rules
- Never move an OD to resolved without a named owner, a date and a source reference.
- When an OD is resolved, update `project-context.md` and the affected skill in the same change.
- If implementation is blocked by an open OD, state the OD ID in the work output rather than assuming a rule.
| AD-125 | **A rejected sign-off is not a decision anywhere. `SignoffGuardPlugin.AlreadySettled` (2026-09-12) lets a rejected action be decided again once reworked; the two readers that still counted any sign-off row as the decision are brought into line.** `SignoffProgressPlugin.AnyAwaitingSignoff` counts an action as decided only once an *approved* sign-off exists against it, and its replay key is the sign-off row rather than the action. `OT Remediation` offers the decision panel for a completed action unless an *approved* sign-off exists, so a reworked action comes back to the supervisor. Amends AD-114(a), whose premise ("an action counts as decided once a sign-off row exists against it, since the guard refuses a second") the guard change had retired without saying so. | Found by the removed-behaviour pass of the 2026-09-13 audit. With the guard relaxed and the two readers unchanged, a reworked action carrying its old rejection row read as decided: approving the check's other actions moved the case to Awaiting Recheck (or, with a final outcome, regraded and closed it) with that action never approved, and on the page the same action was missing from the decision panel, so the supervisor had no control to approve it at all - the IO-300003 deadlock, twice over. The progress plug-in's replay guard had the same premise and the same consequence: keyed on the action, the approval that followed a rejection found the rejection's Audit Event and returned before moving the case. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-126 | **A portal regrade's replay key is the outcome, the grade and the row version the page read, not the outcome alone; and the regrade panel confirms in place instead of reloading.** `RegradeRequestPlugin.DeriveIdempotencyKey` builds `portal-regrade-<outcome>-<grade>[-<rowversion>]` where the page sends no key of its own. The same request twice still replays (NFR-REL-01); a different grade, or the same grade against a later read, is a new intent and is recorded and audited. `OT Remediation`'s regrade panel no longer reloads on success: it rewrites its own summary line to the grade just recorded, disables its controls (its row version is now stale), and says that the rest of the page still shows the earlier read - the AD-094 shape, which the review page already had. Amends AD-117, which kept the reload and put the fix in the caveat text. | Reported against case 254398988 on 2026-09-13: **Record the final outcome** reported success and "does not save". Dataverse said otherwise - `al_finaloutcome` Pass, `al_regradedon` 16:58:33Z, audit event `portal-regrade-e1d92107…` with the reason typed - and the 2.5 s reload re-drew the old grade under the success message, exactly as on IO-300005 (AD-117): the caveat text was not read as an answer. The second click, thirty seconds later, was worse: its key matched the first regrade's Audit Event, so the plug-in returned the first result and wrote nothing while the page again reported success. The key was the outcome alone, so every regrade of an outcome after its first was silently discarded from the portal - the AD-031 correction, and the real regrade a supervisor owes after sign-off when an early one was made by mistake, which would have left this case unable to close by the portal at all. The plug-in's own comment said "one intent per outcome and grade"; the key did not. A test now regrades the same outcome twice with different grades and expects two Audit Events and the later grade on the row. The Code App's `al_RegradeCase` keys itself and was never affected. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-127 | **The final outcome may only be recorded where the case has reached its recheck: at Awaiting Recheck, or at Closed for the AD-031 correction. Refused everywhere else by `RegradeCasePlugin.EnsureCaseIsAtTheRecheck`, and the portal panel is not drawn where it would be refused.** The guard sits in the shared `Regrade`, so the Custom API `al_RegradeCase` (the Code App's recheck route) and the portal panel both obey it. `SignoffProgressPlugin` already gated its own call this way, and `CloseAfterRecheck` already moved no case from anywhere else; the two front ends were the gap. `OT Remediation` now reads the case status and, before the recheck, replaces the panel with a line naming the state and saying the final outcome comes after every remedial action is signed off. Hiding by state is not the role-hiding NFR-SEC-01 forbids: the role question is who you are, which Liquid must not appear to decide, while this is whether the case has got to the step at all - and the server refuses it regardless. | Reported on case **254398988** on 2026-09-13, in the same session as AD-126: "the buttons are showing at the same time, even before the initial outcome is saved... the form still editable, not showing the recorded remedial actions". The case was at **Awaiting Remediation** with all six remedial actions Open, no adviser response on any of them, no answers to the three questions and no sign-off row - nothing had been recorded at all, and the plug-in trace confirmed no `CompleteRequestPlugin` or `SignoffRequestPlugin` had ever run for it. Yet the page offered **Record the final outcome**, and took a Pass twice: a grade on work not yet done, against remedial actions nobody had attested to. It stuck because nothing refused it - `CloseAfterRecheck` correctly left the case where it was, so the outcome carried a final grade while the case sat in remediation, a state the lifecycle has no name for. The adviser's lost typing has the same root as AD-126: the regrade panel's 2.5 s reload discarded the unsaved form, which is why a supervisor who had typed remedial actions saw them gone and read it as a failed save. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-128 | **No write on `OT Remediation` reloads the page any more. Each one confirms in place, rewrites the two row cells it can be certain of, and offers a reload the reader presses.** The shared `window.otRemediationRows` helper holds it: `completed()` after the adviser's sign-off sets each action's badge to Completed and its decision cell to "Adviser completed <date>; awaiting supervisor"; `decided()` after the supervisor's writes the decision and date, and on a **rejection** also puts the badge back to In progress, because `SignoffProgressPlugin` reopens the action for rework (AD-125); `offerReload()` adds a **Reload the page** button instead of a timer. The cells carry `data-ot-row-status` and `data-ot-row-decision` so the scripts can find them, and the badge class mirrors `OT Status Badge` rather than improving on it, so a rewritten row cannot be told from a rendered one. Completes AD-126, which did this for the regrade panel only, and retires the reload half of AD-117. **The same rule now holds site-wide:** an audit of every portal write found one more page taking a reload it could not honour - `OT Review Detail`'s Tax header edit reloaded *immediately* after the write, and the route it can re-derive (BR-004) is drawn in the Review progress table above it - so that write confirms in place and offers the same button. No portal write reloads its own page any more. | Reported on case **254398988** on 2026-09-13: "Supervisor sign-off (complete remedial task in IO) does not show the updates... on the remedial page after it has been saved". Dataverse had all of it - six actions Completed at 17:34:35-39 carrying the adviser's response and the three answers, six Approved sign-offs at 17:35:09-15, the case moved to **Awaiting Recheck** at 17:35:16 and six `SignOffRemediation` audit events. The page reloaded 2.5 s after each write into the portal's render cache, which this write path does not invalidate (AD-094), and re-drew every action as it had been. Three reports in one session had the same root and the same shape of answer, so the reload is gone rather than re-tuned: a page that cannot know the new state must not claim to show it, and the parts it does know it can draw itself. A 13-check smoke test drives the helper against a fake DOM, including that no reload fires on its own. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-129 | **The site setting `Webapi/error/innererror` is `true`, and a page caps an error message only after it has read what it needs out of it.** Power Pages returns a plug-in's own message to the Web API caller only when that setting is on; its documented default is False and this site had never created it, so **every** refusal on the site - the unanswered-questions list, the edit-conflict prompt, the sign-off and regrade preconditions - reached the browser as the platform's `Common Data Service error occurred.` and a 400. Every `friendlyError` on the site reads `error.innererror.message` first and was written for a setting that did not exist. Safe to expose here because these messages are written for the person who hit them: a `VALIDATION`, `PRECONDITION`, `CONFLICT` or `UNAUTHORIZED` prefix, what to do next, and never a table name, query, id or stack trace (PP-16); anything else is collapsed and capped before display. | Reported 2026-09-13: submitting with questions unanswered showed the generic sentence "instead of showing which questions were not answered". The plug-in trace held exactly the right refusal, naming `Q-CRP-04` and carrying the question version ids in its bracketed tail. **A second defect sat behind it**, found while fixing the first: `serverMessage` capped at 300 characters and `friendlyError` then read the `[unanswered:…]` tail off the END of what survived, so from **four** unanswered questions up (311 characters) the ids were gone, the rows went unmarked and the text ended mid-identifier. One and three fitted, which is why the feature looked like it worked. The cap now belongs to each branch that puts text on screen. A smoke test drives the real parser against a genuine innererror body at 1, 3, 4, 6 and 10 unanswered - 29 checks, and 9 of them fail against the pre-fix template. Amends the unanswered-rows work of `757d646`, which could never have worked in a browser. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-130 | **Dropping a reload obliges the page to redraw everything that reload used to fetch, not only the control that was used.** `otRemediationRows.regraded(label)` rewrites the Regraded outcome cell - grade plus the regraded-on date as its own note, as the server renders it - and sets the case status badge to **Closed**, which is certain rather than assumed because AD-127 draws the panel only at Awaiting Recheck or at Closed. `badgeClass` is extracted and now mirrors the whole of `OT Status Badge`'s cascade in its own order, so a rewritten badge cannot disagree with a rendered one; it previously knew two labels out of eight. Completes AD-128. | Reported on **IO-300001** on 2026-09-13: "I've submitted a final outcome, yet it still shows Not recorded yet on the Regraded outcome and Awaiting Recheck on the Case status." The regrade had succeeded in full at 18:14:01 - `al_finaloutcome` Pass, both timestamps, the reason, and the case **Closed** in the same second - and a second click 26 seconds later carried the same outcome, grade and row version, so it replayed and wrote nothing, which is AD-126's key working. AD-128 had removed the reload that used to refresh those two cells and replaced it with the page drawing what it knows, but only for the panel's own hint and the sign-off's row cells. The two cells that report the same facts elsewhere on the page were left server-rendered and stale, so the page contradicted Dataverse under a success message - the very failure AD-128 existed to end, reintroduced one cell over. A smoke test covers the cell, the date note, the Closed badge and its class; it fails outright against the deployed template, where the function did not exist. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-131 | **Every cell a write changes is redrawn, and a cell the page cannot recompute is marked rather than left looking current.** Applying AD-130 to every portal write found seven more stale cells. Fixed where the page can be certain: the **Adviser sign-off** and **Supervisor sign-off** summary rows and the **All remedial actions checked and approved?** cell on `OT Remediation`, and the review **status badge** and **Submitted** date on `OT Review Detail`, which a submit left reading its old status and an em dash. Marked where it cannot: the **case status badge** is not rewritten with a guess, because completing or approving can move a case to Awaiting Sign-off, Awaiting Recheck, Closed or back to **Queued** where the route still owes a discipline (OD-038) - rules the page does not hold - so it gains `may have moved, reload to confirm`. A regrade is the exception and sets Closed outright, clearing the marker, because AD-127 draws that panel only at Awaiting Recheck or Closed. **And a promise was withdrawn:** the sign-off's success message said the case moves to Awaiting Recheck, or with a grade that the outcome is recorded and the case closed - both false on a Tax leg that still owes AQS, where approving returns the case to the queue and records nothing. | The sweep was asked for after IO-300001 (AD-130). Two of the seven are the rows the project owner had been watching when they reported earlier the same day that the supervisor sign-off "does not show the updates" - that report was answered by removing the reload, and these are the cells the reload had been refreshing, so the class survived its own fix twice. The lesson is the general one: a page that stops reloading owes the reader every value the reload used to fetch, and an honest marker for the ones it cannot compute. 29 checks cover the new cells and the marker, including that a regrade clears it; they fail outright against the deployed templates, where the functions did not exist. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-132 | **`Edit case details` (`.case-edit__field`) is the reference for a form field in the Code App, and the question library's modals follow it.** Fields, labels, all three control types, the fieldset and its legend, the button size, the outlined secondary button and the Cancel-then-action order in `SectionModal`, `QuestionModal` and `RetireModal` are now the reference's. A comment in `QuestionLibraryPage.css` names it and asks that the two be kept in step. | Project owner, 2026-09-13, with a screenshot: "the styling on the question library modals seems off", then "ensure it matches that of the edit case". `QuestionLibraryPage.css` styled **only the select**, and the app has no global control styling, so every text input, number input and textarea in those modals fell through to the browser's own - square corners, a different border, and a **monospace** textarea - directly above and below a select that was styled. Labels were muted and unweighted where the rest of the app uses 600 in the text colour. Four implementations of one idea had drifted: `case-edit` styles all three controls, `security` two, `recheck` two, `library` one, with three different paddings and two border colours between them. The security modals share the folder and looked right, which is why only these looked wrong. **Not** unified behind one shared field style: that is the real cause and is worth doing, but it reaches every feature's CSS and would risk regressions far outside the report. Checked by rendering the real stylesheets beside Edit case details in a browser, before and after. | 2026-09-13 |
| AD-133 | **The case status badge on `OT Remediation` is re-read from Dataverse through the portal Web API on every page load, rather than trusted from the Liquid render.** `Webapi/al_outcomecase/enabled` and a one-column allowlist `Webapi/al_outcomecase/fields = al_casestatus` expose the case to the browser for reading; the page GETs `/_api/al_outcomecases(<id>)?$select=al_casestatus` with the FormattedValue annotation and rewrites the badge only where the live label differs from the rendered one. The label rather than the option-set integer, so the page keeps no second copy of the lifecycle values to drift from `CaseLifecycle.cs`. Progressive enhancement: any failure leaves the server-rendered badge exactly as AD-131 left it. Read-only by construction — `Outcome Case - read all` (`a1000000-…-060`) is the sole permission on the table and carries `adx_write`, `adx_create` and `adx_delete` all false, so enabling the Web API here grants nothing beyond the read-all OD-022 already gives every authenticated user, and no PATCH can succeed whatever the allowlist says. | AD-131 marks a cell the page cannot recompute, and `regraded` corrects the badge outright after a regrade — but **only in the browser session that made the write**. Navigating back to the page afterwards re-renders the stale value from the AD-094 cache with no marker on it, because no write happened in that page load, and a stale badge with no marker reads as authoritative. Reported on 254398988 on 2026-09-14: the case page said **Closed** and the remediation page said **Awaiting Recheck** at the same moment, the case having closed at 08:02Z, two minutes earlier — two Liquid queries, two cache entries, one refreshed and one not. The fifteen-minute change-tracking SLA (AD-094) is the floor on how long that can last, and this is the third report of the same cache costing the project owner a false defect (IO-300005/AD-117, IO-300001/AD-130, this). Marking the value was not enough; reading the value is. The Web API is a different data path from the Liquid render cache, which is the whole reason the read goes through it. | 2026-09-14 |
| AD-134 | **The IO extract's `AssignedTo` is the paraplanner, and no checker name is imported at all.** `ImportRules.Columns` and the Code App's `caseUpload.ts` CSV_COLUMNS both map `AssignedTo -> al_paraplanner`. `AssignedBy` is no longer mapped to anything, and `al_checkername` is no longer written by the import. The checker is set manually: by allocation (`AssignCasePlugin`), by a claim (`ClaimCasePlugin`), or by editing the case (`UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin`'s "Checker" field). Supersedes the 2026-09-12 reading recorded against the extract design, and settles AD-113 rather than restating it. | Project owner, 2026-09-14: "the paraplanner is the Assigned to and not Assigned by column", then "the checker will be set manually". The 2026-09-12 mapping took the paraplanner from `AssignedBy` and the checker from `AssignedTo`, on the authority of `data/io-task-extract-sample.csv` — which is **synthetic**: its two names are literally "Paraplanner 1" and "Checker 4", so which column held whom was an assumption baked into anonymised sample data and then into two implementations. The real 2026-09-13 DEV import landed `al_checkername` = Matheau Frith and `al_paraplanner` = Jessica Bell on 254471517 and 254471891, with the paraplanner in the column named for the checker. **`al_checkername` is absent from the column table rather than remapped**, because only listed columns are written: were it mapped to any other column, a re-import of the same TaskID would overwrite whoever had since been allocated — which is exactly AD-113's complaint, that a name the file carried was never proof of allocation. The sample file's two columns were swapped in the same change so it stops asserting the reading it caused. | 2026-09-14 |
| AD-135 | **`Outcome Testing App User` and `App Admin` grant the business tables, not just the admin tables.** `GrantSecurity` now grants both roles read on the eighteen tables the Code App reads (`al_outcomecase`, `al_reviewinstance`, `al_response`, `al_remediationaction`, `al_signoff`, `al_outcome`, `al_auditevent`, `al_caseassignment`, `al_notification`, `al_failreason`, `al_section`, `al_question`, `al_questionversion`, `al_role`, `al_importbatch`, `al_importexception`, `al_checklist`, `al_checklistversion`, `al_reviewroute`) and create/write on the ten its commands write. `al_auditevent` is **create-only** for both: BR-012 wants an immutable record, and an audit event that can be edited is not an audit trail. Writing the people and role tables (`contact`, `mspp_webrole`, `al_role`, `al_section`, `al_failreason`) stays admin-only, extending the escalation-safe split `al_userrolemapping` and `al_pagepermission` already had. Depth is Global throughout, per OD-022. | The Code App has 21 Dataverse data sources and these roles granted six. The gap never surfaced because **nobody had ever held either role** — every user in DEV and TEST is a System Administrator, which grants everything and, in `PermissionHelpers.EnsurePermission`, short-circuits the application gate as well. Found 2026-09-14 when the project owner gave Zoe Ramwell the application Administrators role in TEST and she saw different pages from two System Administrators holding "the same role": she holds `Basic User`, so she could not read `al_pagepermission`, and `rulesInForce` silently fell back to `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` rather than the configured rules. **Write, not merely read:** 50 of the 51 registered steps run as the *calling* user, so a command that updates a case does it with the caller's privileges — read alone renders every page and fails every action. Granting System Administrator instead would have been the wrong fix twice over: it re-hides the gap, and the sysadmin short-circuit means such a tester never exercises the permission model at all. | 2026-09-14 |
| AD-136 | **"The rules could not be read" and "there are no rules" are different states, and the app says which.** `resolveRules(readSucceeded, stored)` in `types/permissions.ts` replaces a bare `rulesInForce(stored)` call in `PermissionProvider`: a failed read returns the coded defaults **flagged `unavailable`**, a successful read of an empty table returns them unflagged. `PermissionContextValue` carries `rulesUnavailable`, and `AppShell` renders a banner above the page content saying the menu is a default set rather than the access actually granted, that pages may be missing or may refuse, and that nothing is unsafe because the server checks every write. A read that failed is not trusted even if it returned rows — a partial result is not a rulebook. | Both states used to arrive as an empty array and both fell back to `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS`. For an unconfigured environment that is right and documented — the defaults are the seed. For a user who cannot **read** `al_pagepermission` it is a lie: they are gated by the coded defaults rather than the environment's configured rules, and the menu looks entirely plausible while being wrong. Found 2026-09-14 alongside AD-135: Zoe Ramwell held only `Basic User` in TEST and saw different pages from two System Administrators holding the same application role. AD-135 fixes why she could not read the table; this fixes the app's silence about it, which is what turned a glance into an investigation. The banner is deliberately NOT shown for a successful read of an empty table, which would cry wolf on every fresh environment, nor while still resolving, which would flash on every load. | 2026-09-14 |
| AD-137 | **A sign-off that parks a case at the recheck tells the signatory it is theirs to close.** `SignoffProgressPlugin.ParkedAtRecheck` asks, after `MoveCase` and `RecordFinalOutcome` have run, whether an approval has left the case at Awaiting Recheck; when it has, a `Recheck due` notification (`al_notification_event` 120910806, new) is queued to the person who signed off, naming the case and saying the final outcome is what closes it. Keyed on the **case**, so a check that signs off eighteen actions queues one reminder rather than eighteen. Sent to the signatory rather than the adviser, because recording the final outcome is the T&C Supervisor's step; the adviser's existing notification already tells them the case moved on. A row is queued even where the sign-off names no signatory — with no recipient, which the drain refuses to send — because a row someone can see in the outbox is better than the silence this exists to end. | "Leave for a separate regrade" is a legitimate choice on the sign-off panel and the case is *meant* to wait at Awaiting Recheck when it is made. What was wrong is that it waited **silently**: `RecordFinalOutcome` returns at its first gate when the sign-off carries no grade, so nothing closed the case and nothing prompted anyone. Case 254398988 sat there from 17:35Z on 2026-09-13 until the project owner noticed the next morning, and since the export collects Closed cases only it had not reached Trail Light either. The condition is read from the case **after** the move rather than predicted from the sign-off row, which is what makes it correct in every branch `MoveCase` has: a graded approval has already Closed the case, a Tax leg still owing AQS has gone back to Queued (OD-038), and a remediated Tax-only case with no Outcome was closed without a recheck. Only the parked case is still at Awaiting Recheck by then. Deliberately a prompt rather than a refusal: forcing a grade at sign-off would remove a choice the project owner asked for on 2026-09-11. | 2026-09-14 |
| AD-138 | **"Recheck required: No" skips the recheck: the case closes on the approval instead of stopping at Awaiting Recheck.** `SignoffProgressPlugin.RecheckWaived` reads `al_recheckrequired` across the check's remediation actions. Waived only when **nothing asks for a recheck and something declines one**: any action answering **Yes** keeps it, an action answering **nothing** neither asks nor declines. So a check whose actions are all unanswered keeps its recheck, and a No beside an unanswered older action waives it. Evaluated **after** `MoveCase` and `RecordFinalOutcome`, not inside `MoveCase` — a supervisor who both grades and waives has their grade recorded first and the case closed second. A waived case keeps whatever grade it has: the export reads `Outcomes.EffectiveOutcomeLabel`, which falls back from the final outcome to the initial one, so it exports the grade its check gave. Amends AD-095, which made the column a form question read by nothing. | Project owner, 2026-09-14, following their 2026-09-13 report on 254398988: "recheck required marked as No, yet status shows awaiting recheck". `al_recheckrequired` was captured, displayed on two pages and in the app, locked with the response by `RemediationResponseGuardPlugin`, and read by **no code that decides anything** — so answering No changed nothing and the case waited for a recheck regardless, which is not what the field's name leads anyone to expect. Absence is deliberately not treated as No: every action raised before AD-095 added the column carries nothing, and a case must not close itself on a question nobody was asked. Requiring *every* action to answer No was rejected for the opposite reason — it would make the rule unusable on any check predating the column. Closing without a regrade is the point rather than a side effect: "no recheck needed" asserts the original grade stands, and OD-041's concern (a remediated case sitting at Awaiting Recheck still showing its pre-remediation grade) does not apply to a case nobody intends to regrade. | 2026-09-14 |
| AD-139 | **Site visibility is gate 0, and it sits above the four identity requirements.** A private Power Pages site refuses anyone not on its Manage access list before Power Pages authentication runs at all, so a contact can hold a binding, an identity username, a security stamp, `logonenabled` and seven web roles and still never reach the site — `/private-mode-access-denied`, whose wording refuses them from the **site** rather than from a sign-in. It is held in the Power Pages management service and not in Dataverse: no `mspp_` table records it, `sitesetting.yml` does not carry it, no `pac` command and no verb of the registration tool reads or writes it, and `identities` cannot see it. Granting access is a manual act in the design studio, and an Entra security group is preferred to per-person grants so that provisioning stays two steps rather than three. **Holders of Dataverse System Administrator bypass the gate without appearing in the list**, which is what hid it: the reported failure for Zoe Ramwell on 2026-09-15 was investigated against Simunye Radingwana, who holds that role, so the comparison subject was never subject to the gate and her four requirements were already identical to his. Recorded as gate 0 in `docs/reference/portal-access-runbook.md`, together with the rule that a comparison between a working and a broken contact is only valid when both hold the same Dataverse security role. | Project owner, 2026-09-15, reporting `/private-mode-access-denied` for Zoe Ramwell against a TEST contact that passes all four requirements. | 2026-09-15 |
| AD-140 | **`Authentication/LoginTrackingEnabled` stays `true`, because the project's only sign-in verification depends on it.** The runbook's verification step is to read `contact.adx_identity_lastsuccessfullogin` rather than ask what the screen said. That column is only stamped when this setting is `true`, and it was **`false` in DEV and TEST** until 2026-09-15 — so the column was null for every contact in both environments, including the service accounts and Simunye Radingwana, who sign in constantly. A working sign-in and a broken one read identically, and the obvious conclusion from a null was the wrong one. Set `true` in both environments on 2026-09-15 and recorded in `sitesetting.yml` with the reason, so it is not read as a cosmetic flag and switched off again. **Any null reading of that column dated before 2026-09-15 is not evidence of a failed sign-in.** | Found by query, 2026-09-15, while verifying the six contacts bound that day. | 2026-09-15 |
| AD-141 | **First-time visitors are not auto-created as contacts; `OpenRegistrationEnabled` stays `false`.** Asked for on 2026-09-15 as "when a user accesses the site for the first time and they have not been added to contact, it should add them automatically with no role assigned". Declined on three findings and confirmed by the project owner. It helps **nobody** currently locked out: all six people who could not sign in already had contacts, and open registration only ever *creates* — for an email that already has one it refuses with "The email ... is already taken" and loops on the registration form, which is strictly worse than the failure they had. It reverses the 2026-08-31 security closure, already re-reverted once on 2026-09-08, after which any tenant account could mint a contact. And "no role assigned" is not "no access" on this site: `Authenticated Users` is the implicit role (`adx_authenticatedusersrole: true`) and carries 10 of 16 table permissions, including Global read on `al_outcomecase`, `al_response` and `al_reviewinstance`, so an auto-created stranger would read every case. The route that does work is unchanged — the four requirements per contact, plus gate 0 (AD-139). | Project owner, 2026-09-15, choosing to bind the existing contacts instead. | 2026-09-15 |
| AD-142 | **Every table the application permission gate reads is granted by the application's own security roles, and `prvReadRole` plus `prvReadpowerpagecomponent` are now among them.** `PermissionHelpers.EnsureAppPermission` reads eight tables — `role`, `systemuserroles`, `systemuser`, `contact`, `mspp_webrole`, `powerpagecomponent`, `al_userrolemapping`, `al_pagepermission` — and reads them **as the caller**, because every command behind the gate is a Custom API and a Custom API plug-in type has no run-as user (`PluginUserService` is `GetOrganizationService(context.UserId)`, which for such a step is the initiating user). Both `Outcome Testing App Admin` and `Outcome Testing App User` now grant read on all eight. `AppPermissionGateTests` drives the gate, records every table it touched, and fails if one is not granted by both role files — so a read added later cannot ship ungranted. | The gate's first act is the break-glass System Administrator probe, which queries the platform `role` table, and **neither shipped role granted `prvReadRole`** (`prvReadal_Role` is the custom `al_role` table, a different thing). So all 27 gated commands refused every caller who was not already a Dataverse System Administrator, on the gate's first line, before any application rule ran — the user got the platform's privilege fault, not a message they could act on. Adam Strumidlo hit it on Import Cases in TEST on 2026-09-16: the fault's `privilegeCount=125` is the App Admin role to the privilege, and its missing-privilege id `222a920a-2778-4564-85cb-e78dde8e4276` is `prvReadRole`'s own id in DEV. `powerpagecomponent` is the same defect one read later, on the contact-to-web-role join. Tolerating the fault in code is not available: AD-109 forbids absorbing an `OrganizationService` fault and carrying on, and `OptionLabels` recorded that for a read. 859 tests passed throughout because the fake answered every unseeded table with "no rows" — a clean negative where the platform gives a fault. | 2026-09-16 |
| AD-143 | **`al_RegradeCase` and `al_SignOffRemediation` enforce `command.regrade` and `command.signoff` server-side, and the adviser role holds neither.** Both plug-ins now call `PermissionHelpers.EnsureAppPermission` before doing anything, gated on the same resource keys the client gates the buttons on, so the two tiers read one rulebook. `RegradeCasePlugin.Regrade` stays public and ungated so `RegradeRequestPlugin` can keep calling it after its own web-role check — the portal path was never the gap. `al_pagepermission` is aligned to the documented model by the `fixpermissions` verb: one active row per documented (role, resource), the documented level exact for every role except the two administrator roles where it is a floor, no rule for the adviser on sign-off/regrade/reviews, and rows carrying no role code deactivated. | Both commands were **public Custom APIs with no `executeprivilegename`, no permission check and no ownership check** — unlike `al_SubmitReview` and `al_CompleteRemediation`, which refuse anyone who is not the assigned checker or owning adviser. Their class comments claimed Dataverse privilege was the control ("a caller without create-al_signoff privilege is refused ... the T&C Manager team is granted that privilege in the security configuration"). No team was ever granted it selectively: `GrantSecurity` puts `al_outcome` and `al_signoff` in `writeForEveryone`, so BOTH application roles hold create at Global depth — verified in TEST, where `Outcome Testing App User` holds `prvCreateal_Outcome` and `prvCreateal_Signoff`. Any app user could therefore overturn an outcome or sign off a remediation by calling the API directly, while the UI showed the buttons only to a T&C Supervisor. AD-031 gives corrections and attestation to the supervisor precisely so the person who did the work is not the person who signs it off, and the environments had each drifted into a different half of the same violation: DEV gave the adviser `command.signoff` = Manage, TEST gave them `command.regrade` = Manage and gave the supervisor no regrade rule at all. Found by the 2026-09-16 role and permission audit. | 2026-09-16 |
| AD-144 | **An assignee must hold an Outcome Testing Dataverse security role, and the commands now say so.** `AssignCasePlugin.ResolveAssignee` gains a fourth precondition — `EnsureCanHoldWork` — refusing an allocation whose assignee holds none of `Outcome Testing App User`, `Outcome Testing App Admin` or `System Administrator`, with a message naming the person and the role to grant. A new `checkassignable <orgUrl>` verb audits every holder of an application role against the same rule and exits 1 when anyone is blocked, so a batch of new people can be gated rather than only described. The three identity planes stay separate: `al_AssignUserRole` still writes the application plane only. | Allocation stamps `ownerid` on the review instance, and Dataverse will not make somebody the owner of a row they cannot read. **That check runs against the assignee, not the caller**, so a person provisioned perfectly on the portal side — contact active, web roles mapped, `al_userrolemapping` correct — still cannot be given work, and a System Administrator allocating to them is refused identically. The platform's fault names the assignee as the `Principal user` and the allocator only as `context.Caller`, which reads as though the person clicking Save lacks access: reported 2026-09-17 as "Adam cannot assign", and a full audit of Adam's roles, privileges, web roles and portal permissions found nothing wrong because nothing was — Clare Hook, the assignee, held `Basic User` alone. Four of the nine application-role holders in TEST were in that state, every one of them a reviewer. Checked by role **name** rather than by privilege because resolving `prvReadal_ReviewInstance` means reading `roleprivileges` and `privilege`, which neither app role grants — the check would throw the very fault it exists to prevent, and AD-109 forbids the catch that would make it survivable; `role` and `systemuserroles` are readable at Global depth under AD-142. **Fail-open on an empty result**, deliberately: "cannot see any role" and "holds no role" arrive identically, and refusing on the first would break allocation for every caller whose privileges cannot read the intersect. Granting the security role from `al_AssignUserRole` was rejected — a command gated by an application rule (`permission.manage`) must not confer platform privileges, which is the escalation-safe split `GrantSecurity` already documents, and portal-only people have no `systemuser` to grant one to. `ClaimCasePlugin` shares `ResolveAssignee`, so the portal self-claim gains the same message. | 2026-09-17 |
| AD-145 | **The inline answer path reads 120910302 by the review's discipline, as the grid path already did.** `inlineOptionsFor` takes an `isTaxReview` argument and applies `INLINE_ORDER` and `INLINE_LABELS` only when it is set; `OT Answer Options` takes an `is_tax` parameter and branches 120910006 on it, and `OT Review Detail` resolves the discipline once into `is_tax_review` and hands it to both the grid headings and the include. Display only — no value, no saved answer and no permitted-choice rule changes, and `ResponseRules.PermittedChoices` is untouched. | AD-055 amended (2026-09-13) reworded 120910302 to "Pass with issues" for the Tax check alone, and put the rename on the inline path rather than on the scale because 120910006 is shared with S-E1 to S-E5 and S-CRP. That placement rested on the inline path being Q-TAX-02's alone, which AD-123 checklist administration had already made false: retyping a question to this scale breaks its section's uniform grid, the section falls to the inline path, and an AQS question was drawn as a tax check — "PASS / PASS WITH ISSUES / FAIL" over the suitability scale's own values. Reported as a bug against the question-type editor (change Yes/No to Pass/Fail/Insufficient evidence, get Pass/Fail/Pass with Issues), but the response type and its permitted choices were always correct; only the wording drawn over them was wrong. A checker who ticked what read "PASS WITH ISSUES" saved 120910302, Insufficient evidence — a non-pass that raises remediation. The grid path had tested the discipline since AD-055 was amended; the two paths now make one reading. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-146 | **A seeded block's declared scale is a default, not a promise: where its questions have been retyped away from it, the questions head the block.** `formBlocks` tests each section's rows against `spec.scale` and falls to `sectionLayout` when they disagree; `OT Review Detail` makes the same test on `sec_rt`/`sec_mixed` after the known-code chain. Both read only the questions that answer on a TICK SCALE: a written, dated or formatted question has no cell under these columns and is drawn inline either way, and counting it would take Suitability off its grid on the strength of S-E2's 120910007 lens tick, which was never in it. Questions that agree re-head the grid; questions that disagree take the block to a meta table with no columns. Only a declared GRID is re-read, so S-TAX and S-GRADE - which declare no scale - cannot reach it and the AD-098/AD-104 reference blocks stay pinned. | A seeded block names its own scale and tick columns rather than deriving them, so the reference document can pin them. AD-123 checklist administration can retype the questions underneath it, and the declared scale then describes nothing: S-AMLCRA had three of its five questions moved to 120910006 and went on heading itself Yes / No / N/A, describing only the two it had left, while the three retyped rows fell to the per-row inline control and drew their own options inside a cell of a grid headed by a scale they do not answer on. A checker reading those column headers would have read three rows by the wrong scale. Found when the project owner retyped that section and sent the screenshot (2026-09-19); the AD-145 fix corrected the wording on those rows but not the columns above them. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-147 | **al_advicedate reads "Date of meeting - Client contact", and can never be in the future.** Display name only: the schema name, type, DateOnly behaviour and required level are untouched, so every plug-in, query, template and generated model that names the column keeps working. The rule lives on `CaseHeaderRules.ValidateAdviceDate` and is reached by BOTH front ends through `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.ApplyFields`, which `CaseHeaderRequestPlugin` already called - one enforcement point for the Code App and the portal. Mirrored client-side in `caseHeaderDates.ts` and in the portal's own script, as an affordance only. A new `setcolumnlabel` verb on the registration tool makes the metadata change, retrieving the attribute and sending it back with only the label replaced so an UpdateAttributeRequest cannot silently reset what it omits. | A meeting that has not happened yet cannot have been checked. Compared on the DATE and in the UK's day, not UTC's: al_advicedate is DateOnly so there is no time of day in the value, and the only clock in the comparison is the one deciding what today is - at 23:30 UTC on a British Summer Time evening it is already tomorrow in London, and a checker entering their own today would have been refused. The UK day was already defined for the BR-010 remediation clock (OD-018); `Remediation.UkDate` now delegates to the same function rather than keeping a second copy, because two definitions of when a day ends is exactly the drift AD-145 and AD-146 were about. The Trail Light extract is untouched and could not have been affected: it carries Check date, not this column. The import is untouched too - the IO task extract carries no advice date at all, so there is no import path for this rule to guard. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-148 | **The date of meeting cannot be later than the due date, and that is ONE comparison, not two.** `CaseHeaderRules.ValidateAdviceDate` gains an optional due date and returns at most one message, the future check first. `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.EffectiveDueDate` supplies the date the save leaves behind - the one being written where the payload carries it, otherwise the before-image - so the rule stays right on the day al_duedate becomes editable. Both front ends reach it through `ApplyFields`, as with AD-147; `caseHeaderDates.ts` mirrors it to the character, including the "(10 Jan 2026)" wording, so the two are one message. | Item 8 of the September batch asked for "not later than the Submission date, and not later than the Due date". There is no submission date on al_outcomecase and never has been: the nearest columns are IO's own task dates, and the review's al_submittedon belongs to the check, not the advice. The project owner settled it as the due date (2026-09-19), so the two comparisons collapse to one and no column is added. The future check is reported first because a date beyond today is usually a mistyped year, and naming the due date instead would send the user to look at the wrong field. An absent due date passes rather than refusing: every imported case carries one, but inventing a deadline to refuse against would be worse than not checking. The comparison is on the date at both ends - al_duedate is a timestamp stamped 72 hours after the upload, so a meeting on the due day at an earlier hour must not read as late. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-149 | **The primary root cause is owed on every advice quality grade but Pass, and a grade of Pass clears one already recorded.** `GradingRules` holds the rule: `RootCauseRequired` is true for every grade but Pass and false while the grade is unanswered, `RootCauseCleared` is true for Pass alone. `SubmitReviewPlugin` drops Q-GR-02 from the questions a submission owes when the grade does not require it, and `ResponseProgressPlugin` clears the recorded cause post-operation on al_response. Q-GR-02 joins the `ChecklistGuards` protected list, taking it to nine. Mirrored in `app/src/features/reviews/gradingRules.ts` and in `OT Review Detail`, which hides the 3x3 in Liquid on the stored grade and keeps up with changes in script. | Q-GR-02 is seeded mandatory, so every Pass review was held at submission by a question with no true answer: the checker had to invent a cause, and that invention then went out in the trend MI Q-GR-02 exists to feed (AD-022). The condition is applied at the gate rather than by unsetting `al_ismandatory`, because the flag is per question version and clearing it would excuse Q-GR-02 on every review at once, including the non-pass reviews the root cause is actually for. **The two predicates are deliberately not each other's negation.** An unanswered grade owes no root cause and destroys none either: the grade is mandatory in its own right so an ungraded review is refused anyway, and a checker who picks the cause before the grade - the two sit one above the other and nothing stops it - must not have it wiped on the way past. **Clearing rather than hiding**, because a hidden value is still a stored value: it would export as a live root cause against a passing case. The clearing sits in `ResponseProgressPlugin` rather than in `AnswerWriter` for the reason AD-053 gives about surfaces - AnswerWriter is the portal's path and a direct Dataverse write never touches it - and rides the six al_response steps already registered rather than adding two more, which is the same choice `StampCheckDate` made. Guarded cheaply then exactly: Pass is also the suitability grid's Pass, so the response type filters the forty-odd ordinary ticks out before the question code, which is the AD-122 contract, decides. **Both front ends draw the row only once the grade is given and is not a Pass** - the batch's wording is "visible and required only when the Advice quality grade is anything other than Pass", and nothing is not anything, so an ungraded review draws no root cause either. `checklistDocument.test.ts` compares what the app draws against the reference Checker Checklist from a form built with no answers at all, so its fixture now answers the grade Potential harm: a blank form cannot show a conditional row, and that test is about which questions the app draws rather than about what a blank form looks like. A first attempt inverted this - drawing the row on an ungraded review to keep that fixture untouched - and was corrected the same day. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-150 | **Insufficient evidence on a Suitability core check leaves only Insufficient evidence and Potential harm available as the advice quality grade.** `GradingRules` gains `IsSuitabilitySection` (section code `S-E` followed by a digit), `GradeAllowedWithInsufficientEvidence`, `SuitabilityGradeRefusal` and `GradeClearedBySuitability`; `ChecklistQueries.HasSuitabilityInsufficient` is the one read all three enforcement points share. `ResponseGuardPlugin` refuses a Pass or Pass with issues written against such evidence, `ResponseProgressPlugin` clears one already recorded the moment a core check turns Insufficient, and `SubmitReviewPlugin` refuses a review that still carries one. `OT Answer Options` gains a `locked` parameter and `OT Review Detail` greys the two values out and clears an invalid grade with a prompt. | The batch's item 10. **The section is keyed on `al_sectioncode`, not on its name or id**, as asked: the name is editable through `al_UpdateSection` and the id is per row, while the code is carried forward across checklist versions. A prefix-plus-digit rule rather than a fixed list of the five, so an `S-E6` added through AD-123 belongs to the family without a code change - the same reading `isOutcomeLens` already makes client-side. **S-CRP and S-CD deliberately do not match** although they also offer Insufficient evidence; they are separate blocks on the Checker Checklist and the batch named the core checks alone. **The tick wins over the grade, not the other way round**: writing the grade against existing evidence is refused, while writing the evidence against an existing grade clears the grade and prompts - the checker is recording what they found on the file, and the finding is not the thing to argue with. The grade is not overridden to a value nobody chose, because Insufficient evidence versus Potential harm is a judgement only the checker can make; it is the two contradicting values that stop being available. The submit-time check exists because deploying a guard cannot reach back and re-guard rows already saved. The portal's own test for a Suitability section is a substring rather than a prefix, because this site's Liquid has no prefix filter worth relying on (see the recorded filter limits); it is advisory, the server's rule is exact, and the two agree on every section that exists. **No TypeScript mirror**, unlike AD-149: the Code App's review page is a read-only document (OD-007), so there is no grade control there to restrict. **Existing data: DEV holds two submitted reviews carrying four Suitability Insufficient answers, and both are already graded Insufficient evidence - no review violates this rule, and nothing was changed.** | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-151 | **The case header carries a Tax Checker and an AQS Checker, each stamped by the allocation of that review, and neither is editable.** Two new text columns on `al_outcomecase` - `al_taxcheckername` and `al_aqscheckername` (100 chars) - replace the single `al_checkername` on every read surface. `CheckerNames.AttributeFor` maps a review type to its column; `AssignCasePlugin.StampCheckerName` takes the review's discipline and writes only that one, and `ClaimCasePlugin` calls the same method. `al_checkername` is removed from `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin`'s editable fields and from `CaseHeaderRequestPlugin`'s portal allowlist, so neither surface can write it. Mirrored client-side in `app/src/features/cases/checkerNames.ts`, which also owns the empty states. Backfilled from existing review assignments by the `backfillcheckernames` verb. | The batch's item 2. **The defect:** a case taking both checks has two checkers and the header had one column, so whichever discipline was allocated second overwrote the first - the header named one checker, gave no hint it had ever named the other, and a worklist search found the case under one person and not the other. The write side was already per-discipline (`CaseEditPanel` allocates per Tax/AQS through `al_AssignCase`); it was only the stamp that collapsed them. **Stamped rather than derived**, because the worklist is one flat row per case with no reviews to read, and because `al_checkername` was already a denormalised stamp - this keeps the existing pattern rather than adding a query per row. The two write paths that maintain it are the same two that always did. **No longer editable on either surface**: each column now REFLECTS an allocation, so free text would be a second source of truth for the same fact - and typing a name into the old field had already been mistaken for allocating the check once (2026-09-09). **Three empty states, not one**, because an absent name means opposite things: `named`, `unallocated` (the case takes this check and nobody holds it) and `not-required` (it takes no such check). The app reads the route to tell them apart and treats an unknown route as requiring both, so an unallocated check reads as work outstanding rather than work nobody owes; `OT Case Detail` reads the review instances instead, which is the stronger fact. `OT Review Detail` shows only `Not yet allocated`, having neither to hand. **`al_checkername` is kept and left dormant**, not dropped: what it holds is the last allocation made under the old rule, which is evidence about how a case was handled. Deleting the column is the project owner's call once the two have run long enough to be trusted. **Also affected, and changed:** the app's worklist search (both names), its CSV export (one column became two), the people directory (positions are de-duplicated by name so a case is not counted twice against someone holding both its checks), and the shared case header, which now draws 19 fields where the reference Checker Checklist draws 18 - the fourth deliberate difference `checklistDocument.test.ts` carries. The Trail Light export is NOT affected: it carries no checker column. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-152 | **`al_outcomecase.ownerid` is labelled "Uploaded by" wherever the CASE's owner is shown, and nowhere else.** The Dataverse display name is changed on the attribute (schema name untouched), and `UPLOADED_BY_LABEL` in `app/src/types/domain.ts` is what the four app surfaces render: the case detail summary, the worklist column, the worklist CSV header, and the import batch table. Field names, the `owner` property and every filter are unchanged. | The batch's item 4, which asked for a label change "unless you find the column is showing the wrong user". **It is showing the right user, but incidentally rather than by design:** `ownerid` is the Dataverse record owner and it reads as the uploader only because nothing in the solution ever reassigns it - every `ownerid` write targets `al_reviewinstance` (AssignCase, ClaimCase) or `al_remediationaction`, never the case. Recorded because the label is true by omission: assigning a case in a model-driven app, a bulk reassign, or an owner-team change would each make it a lie, and nothing would catch that. A dedicated `al_uploadedby` column stamped at import would be true by construction, and was not built because the batch asked for a label change and the column is correct today. **Four "Owner" columns were deliberately NOT renamed**, because they are different people: the reviews table on a case and the review page's summary show the REVIEW's owner (the checker), and the remediation tables in both front ends show the ACTION's owner (the adviser). Renaming those would have replaced a vague label with a false one. **The people directory's `Owner` role is also unchanged**: the role is a URL segment (`/people/:role/:name`), so renaming the key would break existing links, and it is read in sentences - "recorded as the owner" - where "Uploaded by" is not grammatical. If that role should change, "Uploader" is the word that fits and it needs a key/label split. **The portal needs no change at all**: it never reads `ownerid` on a case. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-153 | **The due date defaults to 72 calendar hours after the upload, and only a manager may move it, only in the Code App.** The default is unchanged - `ImportRules.DueDateFor(uploadedAt)` stamped by `ImportCasesPlugin`, taken from the upload moment so every case in one file shares a deadline. What changed is that `al_duedate` re-enters `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.Editables`, gated by a NEW resource key `case.duedate` at Edit, seeded to T&C Supervisor, Outcome Testing Manager and Administrators. The legacy `DueDate` scalar parameter is folded into the `Fields` payload rather than applied beside it, so there is one door, one audit line and one value for item 8's comparison to read. The portal refuses `al_duedate` for everyone, which is the "in codeapps" half of the instruction. `CaseHeaderRules.ValidateDueDate` refuses a deadline moved back before the meeting already recorded, mirrored in `caseHeaderDates.dueDateRefusal`. | Item 6 of the September batch, and the project owner's clarification "3 days, only editable by managers in codeapps" (2026-09-19). **This supersedes an instruction given the same day** - "due date should not be edited and should always be set to 72 hours after the upload" (commit `dcaf167`) - which had taken the column out of the editable map entirely; the narrower direction came second and is the one in force. Its own resource key rather than a higher level on `page.cases`, because `page.cases` Edit is what lets a Tax or AQS reviewer complete the header fields the IO extract does not carry (project owner, 2026-09-12) - four roles hold it in DEV, two of them checkers - so raising that bar would have taken the whole header away from the people meant to fill it in. **Calendar time, confirmed, not working days**: the owner said "3 days", and remediation's `AddWorkingDays` deadline is a different deadline for different people. **Three defects were found and fixed on the way.** (1) The panel had offered a Due date box since before the lock, and a save touching it was refused with "Field 'al_duedate' cannot be edited." - taking every other field change in the same save down with it. (2) `humanDay` in `caseHeaderDates.ts` formatted through `Intl` with `en-GB`, which abbreviates September as "Sept" where .NET's invariant "MMM" gives "Sep", so item 8's refusal was worded differently on the two tiers for one month of the year; every test pinning that wording used January. It now formats from a fixed month table, which also stops the wording moving with the browser's ICU version. (3) `WebRoleSeed` still granted Tax and AQS Reviewer `page.cases` at View, which `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` changed to Edit on 2026-09-12 - a re-run would have silently withdrawn an access DEV has been granting for a week. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-154 | **`al_pagepermission` rows are keyed on the ROLE NAME RAW, as `al_SetPagePermission` builds it, not on `WebRoleSeed`'s slug.** The new `setpagepermission` registration verb writes `PP-<role>-<resource>` verbatim. | The two writers disagree and DEV carries the consequence: T&C Supervisor has TWO active `command.signoff` rules, `PP-AL-PORTAL--T-C-SUPERVISOR-command.signoff` from `seedwebroles` and `PP-AL Portal - T&C Supervisor-command.signoff` from the security page, because the page's upsert could not find the seeded one. Raw is the right side to be on, because the security page is how a permission is actually changed afterwards and `PermissionHelpers.MaxLevel` takes the HIGHEST level across active rows - a shadow row the page cannot see keeps granting what an administrator believes they have just revoked. **The duplicate rows already in DEV are not cleaned up here**, only reported: deactivating a rule somebody may be relying on is the project owner's call. Also recorded: these rows are DATA, not solution components, so they do not promote - TEST and PROD each need the verb run against them. | 2026-09-19 |
| AD-155 | **The due date is three days after the UK DAY of the upload, at midnight** - `CaseHeaderRules.UkDate(uploadedAt).AddDays(3)`, replacing `uploadedAt.AddHours(72)`. The constant is `DueWithinDays = 3`. | Audit finding 5 and decision D2 (2026-09-20). Two defects in one line. **The column cannot hold a time**: `al_duedate` is DateOnly (Behavior 2), so Dataverse discarded the time of day on write and every value in DEV reads back 00:00:00 - the rule said "72 hours", the record stored a date, and AD-153, the commit message and the deployment note all claimed the upload's time of day was preserved. A unit test asserted it off the function's return value, which is why it passed while the claim was false; a unit test can only pin what a function returns, so the function now returns what the column will keep. **And the day was the wrong day**: uploads are timestamped UTC and the users are in London, so through BST a file uploaded at 23:30 UTC on the 17th was uploaded at 00:30 on the 18th to the person who pressed the button, and adding 72 hours made it due a day earlier than the same file uploaded a minute later - a seam in the middle of the night that testing at noon would never find. Converting to the UK day first removes it, reusing `UkDate` rather than repeating the conversion. Calendar days, not working days, is unchanged and still deliberate: remediation's deadline counts working days because it belongs to different people under a different rule. Existing cases are untouched. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-156 | **Every SdkMessageProcessingStep in the solution carries `<StateCode>Enabled</StateCode>`, `round-trip-src.ps1` re-applies it after every export, and `verifysteps` proves it in the target environment.** `scripts/Import-Solution.ps1` always passes the activate-plugins flag and treats an import as unfinished until verification passes. | Audit finding 3 (2026-09-20). Dataverse imports a step DISABLED unless activation is requested, and the step XML carried no state element, so the solution could not express the intent. On 2026-09-02 an import without the flag switched off the six al_response steps in DEV and nobody noticed for a week: the code was present, the tests passed, the screens looked right, and only the step state was wrong. Those steps are where the September batch's server-side enforcement lives, so a silent deactivation turns every "enforced server-side" claim into UI-only. The round-trip hook is the part that makes it durable: `src/` mirrors the export by contract (AD-013) and the export does not emit StateCode, so without it the stamp would have survived exactly one round trip - the next run proved it, changing all 21 files before re-stamping them. It is the one declared divergence from "mirrors the export", and it prints what it touched so the divergence is visible in the run. Enabled was checked, not assumed: all 52 OutcomeTesting.Plugins steps in DEV are statecode 0. Two defects were found by testing rather than reasoning - the verb's first run reported all 21 steps missing because an unfiltered read of `sdkmessageprocessingstep` truncates at the page limit against thousands of platform rows, and the stamp comment originally named the flag with its dashes, which XML forbids inside a comment and which made `pac solution pack` refuse the whole file. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-157 | **A Tax fail on a Tax-then-AQS case does NOT go to remediation. The case proceeds to AQS, and whichever checks failed are gathered into ONE combined remediation raised by the AQS submit.** `NextCaseStatusForTax` returns Queued whenever AQS is still to come, whatever the Tax result; the new `TaxRaisesRemediationNow` carries the other half of the old answer - whether anyone is asked to DO anything - which one status value could not express. `NextCaseStatusForAqs` gains `taxFailDeferred`, so an AQS **pass** on a case whose Tax check failed goes to Awaiting Remediation rather than Closed: the one combination the old rule could never produce, because a Tax fail never reached AQS. `SubmitReviewPlugin.DeferredTaxFail` reads the verdict back off `al_taxoutcome` on the case, and `RaiseRemediation` merges both reviews' non-pass items and observations, Tax first. | **This supersedes OD-027 and the remediation half of BR-006** (project owner, 2026-09-20), and the 2026-09-09 direction that held a flagged Tax pass before AQS. Those said a Tax non-pass entered remediation whatever the route so that AQS never reviewed a file with something unaddressed on it. The cost was asking the adviser **twice about one file**: two action sets, two letters, two rounds of the same conversation. The trade was put to the owner explicitly and accepted - a serious Tax failure now waits for the AQS check - together with the consequence that the AQS checker sees the Tax result while they work, which they confirmed as intended. A **Tax-only case is deliberately unchanged**: nothing is coming that could carry the fail, which is why the rule takes `aqsStillToCome` rather than reading the route. **OD-038 is retired**: it said a case had to pass through remediation before it could reach AQS, and on this route there is now no remediation before AQS. **The action key is unchanged** at `REM-<ref>-<sequence>-<index>`, deliberately: the brief asked for a per-case key, but the sequence is what distinguishes one remediation ROUND from another, and a case that is remediated, signed off, re-reviewed and fails again would collide on a pure per-case key and overwrite the adviser's earlier responses. Combining is what makes it per case - one set, raised once, covering both disciplines - rather than the key. **No migration was needed**: DEV holds 0 cases in Awaiting Remediation or Remediation In Progress and all 61 active actions are already Completed, so there was no in-flight adviser work to preserve. One letter rather than two falls out for free, because `QueueRemediationAssigned` keys the outbox on the REVIEW: one submit, one review, one notification. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-158 | **The "Checklist" section on the review page and the case page is the IO task's ticked items, drawn as a flat read-only bulleted list — not the checklist version's questions and not grouped by section.** The requirement is corrected to what is built; the code is unchanged.
