The source is `al_outcomecase.al_checklistitems`, written by the import and the only input to the BR-004 route, and only the ticked items exist to draw. One implementation per front end, shared by both of that front end's surfaces: `app/src/features/reviews/ChecklistSection.tsx` for the Code App, the `OT Checklist Items` web template for the portal. | Decision D1 from the project owner, 2026-09-20, answering the Change 4 gap the September audit raised. The change batch asked for a section "driven by the review instance's checklist version, grouped by section, listing every item from the source sheets"; `76982e9` had already built the other thing on direction of 2026-09-19, and the audit could not call either side right without the owner. **The build is what was wanted**: the version's questions are the check itself and the review form renders them directly below, so listing them above would say the same thing twice, while the ticked IO items answer the question nothing else on the page answered — why this case is in front of this checker, and why it routed Tax or AQS. **The requirement was the stale artefact, not the code.** This entry exists because the rationale lived only in a commit message and in the template's own comment, where it read as an argument with a live requirement rather than a record of a decision; the comment on both front ends now points here instead. **Resolves the `al_checklistitems` half of OD-049** — the column is displayed on four surfaces; the other fifteen extract columns are untouched and OD-049 stays open for them. A test pins the corrected shape (`checklistRender.test.tsx`, "is a flat list of the ticked items"): the five that existed all passed against a section-grouped list carrying question codes, so nothing would have caught a drift back to the original wording. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-159 | **"Tax Remedial" is `Q-TAX-04`, a Rich text question at display order 4 in S-TAX, directly below `Q-TAX-03` "Case notes" at order 3 — which is the Change 7 requirement as written, already built.** It is NOT `Q-FQTAX-03`: that is "Remedial action required?", the mandatory Yes/No in S-FQTAX that AD-091 reads to decide whether a remediation is raised, a different question in a different section that can never hold markup. The two are told apart by section and response type, never by the word "remedial" in their names. On the Tax check the questionnaire renders both Tax questions in order; on every page that is NOT the Tax review the read-only `OT Tax Notes` panel carries them instead, which is why the panel is suppressed on the Tax review rather than missing from it. | The September audit read Change 7 as a placement gap needing the owner's ruling, and no ruling was needed: the audit had named the wrong question and read a deliberately suppressed duplicate panel as the field having nowhere to sit (2026-09-20, Phase 4). The conflation was not only the audit's — `OT Tax Notes` itself fetched `Q-FQTAX-03` and read `al_answerrichtext` off it, a column that question never fills, so the Tax checker's remedial text reached neither the AQS checker nor the adviser, and drew nothing rather than failing (audit finding 14). That matters more since AD-157 than it would have before: one combined remediation raised after the AQS check makes this panel the adviser's only sight of what the Tax checker asked for. AD-020 keeps both readers out of the Tax questionnaire, so there is no other route. Fixed in `powerpages/` source; **the portal upload that puts it in DEV had not been run when this was written**, and under finding 12 the tree is diffed against DEV before that upload. No test pins it: finding 8 stands, no Liquid template has one. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-160 | **The para-planner is imported from the IO extract's `AssignedBy`, and `AssignedTo` is not imported at all.** `ImportRules.Columns` and `caseUpload.ts` both map `AssignedBy -> al_paraplanner`; `ImportRules.ParaplannerAttribute` names the column once so the two cannot drift. The import also resolves the name against Contact as it writes and reports every row whose para-planner cannot be addressed, without failing the row. | Project owner, 2026-09-20, reversing the 2026-09-14 direction recorded in the AD-124 amendment, which had itself reversed the 2026-09-12 reading. The conflict was put to the owner explicitly before they chose: the 2026-09-14 note dismissed `data/io-task-extract-sample.csv` as synthetic and said the real extract did not bear it out, but that sample is **still the only extract in this repository**, it maps `AssignedBy` to "Paraplanner N" in all seven rows, and the written specification has said `AssignedBy` throughout. **Amended the same day, and the residual risk is closed: the real extract was supplied and it settles the question.** The Pre-Advice Check task extract of 2026-09-14, read on 2026-09-20, carries **`AssignedBy` populated on all 14 rows and `AssignedTo` blank on 7 of them**. Under the superseded mapping **half the cases would have imported with no para-planner at all**, and `al_paraplanner` is who receives the Pass and Remediation letters about a client's advice outcome - so the old reading did not merely name the wrong person, it named nobody on half the file. `AssignedTo` also equals `CompletedBy1`, the person who stamped the checklist item, on 6 rows, which is consistent with it being whoever actioned the task rather than whoever raised it. The extract carries every header the import reads, and 13 of its 14 rows import; the one failure is the already-documented row with no checklist item stamped. **The extract is not in this repository and must not be** - it holds client names, references, addresses, postcodes, email addresses and phone numbers. Only the checklist vocabulary is recorded, in a test. **`AssignedTo` is absent rather than mapped elsewhere** - AD-113 established that a name the file carried never proved a case was allocated, and only listed columns are written, so a re-import cannot overwrite whoever is allocated. The test fixtures were renamed with the mapping: calling the `AssignedTo` value "Pat Paraplanner" is exactly the assumption that got written into the column map twice, and a fixture that embeds the guess cannot catch it. **Existing rows are not corrected by re-importing** - BR-001 keys on `TaskID` and a second upload of the same task reports "Duplicate (skipped)", so any environment that imported under the old mapping holds the checker's name in `al_paraplanner` until it is corrected deliberately. DEV holds only test records; see the deployment note. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-161 | **A para-planner name that cannot reach anybody is reported at import, with the reason and the value, and never fails the row.** `NotificationOutbox.MatchParaplanner` classifies a name as NoName, NoContact, Ambiguous, NoEmail or Matched; `ParaplannerEmail` becomes a wrapper over it and the send path is unchanged. `ImportCasesPlugin` calls it per created row and adds `Imported (para-planner unmatched)` to the batch report. | Audit finding 7. Matching a person by `fullname` is weak and was designed to fail loudly, but the failure was only visible downstream: the notification queued with no recipient and the drain marked it **Failed**, and nothing watches Failed rows. The first anyone knew of a bad name was a letter that never arrived, weeks after the upload that could have been fixed on the day. **Reported, never fatal** - the case is real and the check must still happen; refusing the row would throw away work over a contact record that can be fixed afterwards, and the row is not dropped silently either, since it is named in the report with the reason. **The four failures were one null until now** and are separated because "unmatched" is not actionable while "two active contacts are named Sam Jones" and "Sam Jones has no work email" are different jobs for different people. **One deliberate tightening:** the old query filtered on the email being present, so two contacts of one name where only one had an email resolved to that one and sent; it now reads Ambiguous and sends nothing, because a missing email address is not evidence about which person the case means. A theory test pins that none of the four non-matches returns an address, so richer reporting did not become a looser send. Read as the system user, since the person running the import is not guaranteed read on Contact and a diagnostic must not fail for want of a privilege. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-162 | **An adviser is mapped to a T&C Manager by EMAIL in `al_advisermapping`, and that mapping decides who is TOLD a sign-off is waiting — never who may perform one.** New organisation-owned table with `al_adviseremail` as the alternate key and `al_tcmanagerid` as a Restrict-on-delete lookup to Contact. `TcManagerRouting` resolves a case through it; `CompleteRemediationPlugin` queues the new `Sign-off due` notification (event `120910807`) when the last remediation action on a case is completed. Maintained on a new Code App screen at `/admin/advisers`, gated on `page.admin.advisers`, granted Manage to Portal Administrator, Administrators and the Outcome Testing Manager. | Fixes 5, and the owner's decision of 2026-09-20 that the mapping is **routing only**. The remediation brief asked that it drive the Phase 1 table permission and that a test prove an unmapped T&C Manager cannot read the case; the owner had already said T&C Managers view all cases, so that test would assert the opposite of the access model Phase 1 built. **It is deliberately absent, and its absence is asserted in both test files' summaries** rather than left as a silent omission. `TcManagerRouting.Routing` carries a recipient and a reason and nothing a later change could mistake for a permission, and a test pins that shape so the argument has to be had there first. **The gap this closes is real:** nothing was sent at this point at all. The adviser was told when their remediation was approved or sent back, but the person who had to do the approving was never told there was anything to approve - a case reached Awaiting Sign-off and waited for somebody to notice. **Keyed on email, not name**, unlike the para-planner: the extract supplies `AdviserEmail` on every row and an email is exact, so there is no reason to repeat the weakness AD-161 exists to make loud. Because the key is enforced by the table, the resolver takes the first row without testing for ambiguity. **Restrict rather than RemoveLink** on the contact lookup: deleting a contact who is somebody's T&C Manager is refused rather than silently emptying the mapping, because a mapping that quietly became blank routes nothing and says nothing. **A missing mapping costs the notification and nothing else** - the adviser has just finished their work and the case has already moved, so failing their completion over a configuration row would punish the wrong person; the reason goes to the trace log so an administrator whose manager says "I was never told" can find out why. **No adviser is mapped yet**, so this sends nothing in practice until rows are created. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-163 | **Every notification's subject and body is editable data in `al_notificationtemplate`, keyed on a stable template code, and the copy compiled into the assembly is the FALLBACK rather than the source.** Twelve codes cover every letter the solution sends. `NotificationTemplates` holds the catalogue, the renderer and the token rules; `NotificationTemplateGuardPlugin` refuses a template that would send a broken letter, pre-operation on Create and Update; `seednotificationtemplates` writes one row per letter from the same catalogue and never overwrites an existing one. Administered at `/admin/templates`, gated on `page.admin.templates`, granted Manage to Portal Administrator, Administrators and the Outcome Testing Manager. | Change 1. **The scope was larger than the item implied**: `NotificationBodies.cs` held two bodies and two subjects, and the other eight letters were built inline across four more files - a table covering only the two would have looked finished and left most of the emails hard-coded. **Behaviour is unchanged and that is proved, not asserted**: all 1185 plug-in tests pass with every pre-existing notification test unmodified, and three tests compare the rendered letter byte for byte against `NotificationBodies`, which is kept precisely so the comparison is between two independent implementations rather than a thing and itself. **The fallback is what makes it safe to deploy before any row exists** - a missing, inactive or half-written row renders from the catalogue, so an environment holding none of these sends exactly what it sent before. **The copy has one home, and keeping it cost two decisions:** `NotificationTemplates.cs` is **linked** into the registration tool rather than referenced, because the plug-ins target net462 and the tool targets net8.0 and a seed carrying its own transcription would drift from the fallback the moment either was edited; and `RemediationLetter` exists as a separate class because reaching for `OutcomeRules` from the linked file broke the tool's build immediately, so the shared file stays free of everything but `Microsoft.Xrm.Sdk`. **Escaping runs in two directions**: the stored template is markup an administrator wrote and is used as-is, while token VALUES are case data and are escaped exactly as `NotificationBodies.Paragraph` escaped them - backwards, it either shows `&lt;p&gt;` to a reader or lets a client name rewrite the letter, and both directions have a test. **Branching letters are separate codes, not one template with conditionals**, because a template language with branching in it is a language and the person editing this is writing a letter. **The guard is server-side because the table is reachable from the Web API**, and its empty-body refusal is the subtle one: without it the row is ignored at send time and the compiled copy goes out instead, which is correct and indistinguishable from the save having worked. The Code App mirror `notificationTemplates.ts` is advisory and a drift test reads the C# and fails if the two disagree, the AD-041 pattern. **The button's label is deliberately not editable yet** and the comment says so rather than leaving it looking like an oversight. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-164 | **The attached PDF is generated inside the plug-in by a purpose-built writer, with no library, no connector and no cloud flow.** `PdfWriter` emits a valid PDF 1.4 from a list of blocks; `CompletedCheckPdf` builds the document for a case; `NotificationOutbox.QueueWithCompletedCheck` stores it base64 on the outbox row at QUEUE time; `NotificationDrain.Attach` creates the `activitymimeattachment` against the email before `SendEmail`. Two new columns on `al_notification`: `al_attachmentname` and `al_attachmentbody`. It rides on the para-planner's "Review submitted" letter. | Change 2, and the owner's choice of 2026-09-20 between an in-plug-in writer and a Word-template cloud flow. **Why no library:** a Dataverse plug-in runs sandboxed and in partial trust, so there is no file system and `System.Drawing` — which almost every net462 PDF library needs for font metrics — is unavailable; a library would also have to be ILMerged, because the sandbox loads one file. The documents here are a page of text, so the hard part of a PDF library (images, colour spaces, font subsetting) is the part nothing uses. **No embedded fonts:** Helvetica and Helvetica-Bold are two of the fourteen standard PDF fonts every reader must have, which keeps the file to ~2KB and removes font licensing entirely. **The widths tables are the whole trick** — wrapping needs string widths and there is no drawing library to ask, so Adobe's standard metrics are carried in the class; without them the choices are a monospace guess that wraps visibly wrongly, or no wrapping, which runs text off the page. **Generated at queue time, not send time**, so it is a snapshot of the case as the review was submitted and a re-drain sends the same document rather than a newer one under the same letter. **A document never costs a letter**: generation runs inside the transaction that caused the notification, so throwing would roll back a checker's submit because a PDF could not be drawn — and the drain swallows an attachment failure too, since throwing there would mark the row Failed and send the letter twice to fix a document. That is the failure-handling question the audit said had nothing to answer it. **It deliberately carried no answers and no grade** on the reasoning that the para-planner is not a checker and AD-020 keeps them out of the review page - **superseded the same day by AD-165**, which records that the premise was wrong: para-planners have no access to the system at all, so there was no screen for the attachment to route round. **Verified independently** — a generated sample was parsed with pypdf, which opened it and extracted every field, and a test walks the xref table asserting each offset lands on its object, which is the classic way a hand-built PDF becomes unopenable. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-165 | **The para-planner's attachment carries the completed check - every answer, grouped by section - and the case's remedial actions, with that section omitted entirely when there are none.** `CompletedCheck.Answers` reads a submitted review's responses and renders each as "question: answer", batching the version, question and section reads the way `Remediation.NonPassItems` does; `CompletedCheck.RemedialActions` reads the case's active `al_remediationaction` rows; `CompletedCheck.PlainText` reduces a rich-text answer to text, because the PDF has no HTML in it. `PdfWriter` gains a `Subheading` kind for the second level of structure a check has. | **Change 2's content clause and Change 7's PDF clause, neither of which was met**, found by re-auditing the delivered work against the batch text on 2026-09-20. Change 2 asked for "a PDF of the completed checks for that case, including remedial actions if any exist"; what was built was a case summary that deliberately withheld both. **AD-164's premise was wrong, not merely its conclusion**: it reasoned from AD-020 that a para-planner should not be handed a way round the review page, and the project owner's answer was that para-planners have no access to the system at all - the attachment is the only sight of the check they ever get. **The tell that the artefact was the wrong one** was the requirement's own third sentence, "if there are no remedial actions, omit that section rather than showing an empty one": it could not be evaluated against a document that had no such section in either state. That sentence is now a test. **Submitted reviews only** - a half-answered review is somebody's working copy, and a reader cannot tell a blank that was considered from one not yet reached. **An unanswered question is left out** rather than drawn as "not answered", which would read as an omission rather than a question that did not apply. **The old boundary test was replaced, not deleted**, because the assertion it made is now exactly backwards; the manual script's T7b step 4 said the same thing and would have certified the gap rather than catching it. **Verified independently again** - a two-page sample was parsed with pypdf, which extracted the content on both pages, so the hand-built xref survives the new block kind across a page break. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-166 | **The document is named for what it is: `CompletedCheckPdf`, titled "Outcome Testing - completed check", attached as `Completed check <reference>.pdf`.** `PdfWriter.Subheading` is drawn at 11pt rather than body size. | Follows AD-165. The document was called a case summary while it withheld the check; once it carried the check, the name understated it to the one person who ever reads it, and a para-planner filing "Case summary" would not know it held the findings. **The subheading size came from rendering a sample and looking at it** - a Field's LABEL is also bold at body size, so a section heading at 10pt was indistinguishable from the questions beneath it, which is the one job it has. That is not something the text-extraction assertions could have caught: they read the draw calls, not the page. The sample writer now builds through `CompletedCheckPdf` rather than a hand-written block list, so what a reader is shown is what a para-planner would be sent. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-167 | **The portal template gate also checks that every `OT ...` include resolves, and that every `entities[0]` has a `.size > 0` guard above it.** Assertions D and E in `powerpages/Check-PortalTemplates.ps1`, over all 50 templates rather than the one fetch block assertions A-C reach. | Audit finding 8, second pass. Both new assertions cover the same failure MODE as finding 14 - **failing silently** - from other directions: a mistyped include renders nothing at all rather than erroring, exactly as the finding-14 fetch drew nothing. **E found two live defects on its first run.** `OT Review Detail` indexed `review.results.entities[0]` and `by_case.results.entities[0]` unguarded; an empty result throws "Index was outside the bounds of the array" on this engine, and the index is evaluated BEFORE the null test that follows it - so the "Review not available" empty state, written for precisely the case of a review you cannot see, was unreachable. Fixed with the guard `OT My Work`'s tiles already used. **E is about the unguarded index, not the index**: the first draft flagged all seventeen uses including the eleven correct ones, which would have taught the wrong lesson and been switched off; it now looks for the guard on the same line or the three above. **Proved by reintroducing the defect** and watching the gate name it and its consequence, then restoring the template. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-168 | **Who a letter goes to is choosable as data, and an administrator can add letters of their own.** Three columns on `al_notificationtemplate`: `al_event`, `al_recipientkind` (Adviser / T&C Manager / Para-planner / Checker / a named contact) and `al_recipientcontactid`. `NotificationRecipients` resolves a kind against the CASE; `NotificationTemplateRows` reads the stored side; `NotificationOutbox.Queue` applies an override and then fans out the extra letters. Administered at `/admin/templates`. | **The fan-out is inside `Queue`, which every notification already passes through, so not one emit point changed** and all 1220 pre-existing tests kept passing untouched - the alternative was threading a token dictionary through seven call sites that each build a different one. **A custom letter may use only the tokens a case can answer whatever raised it** (`reference`, `adviser`, `client`, `caseLink`, `caseButton`): `grading` and `finalOutcome` exist only at the moment one particular event fires, so offering them would produce a letter that reads correctly on the screen where it was written and arrives with gaps. **Extra letters are queued AFTER the built-in is created and every failure is swallowed** - a letter somebody added must never take down the transaction that raised the event. **Two negatives carry their own tests because both fail quietly:** an override that resolves to nobody leaves the caller's own recipient alone rather than emptying a correctly worked-out address, and a row carrying one of the twelve codes is that letter's own wording rather than an addition, so it is never sent twice. **The checker is read from the allocation, never from `al_checkername`**, which arrives on the upload file and names whoever the spreadsheet said. **Four refusals are server-side** because each produces a row that looks like configuration and does nothing: no event, no recipient, a contact kind with no contact, and an empty body - the last being worse here than for a built-in, since there is no compiled copy to fall back to and the letter simply never sends. **Nothing here is a permission**, the same boundary `TcManagerRouting` draws: naming somebody as a recipient tells them a thing happened and gives them no access to the case. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-169 | **The stored notification body is reduced to `HtmlSanitiser`'s allow-list before it is written**, in `NotificationTemplateGuardPlugin` pre-operation, and a body that survives the clean as nothing is refused with a sentence saying so. | The caveat the September audit recorded against AD-163: an administrator editing a letter writes markup that lands in somebody's inbox, and the guard validated codes, tokens and emptiness while passing the markup through untouched. **The same treatment `ResponseGuardPlugin` already gives `al_answerrichtext`**, for the same reason - the table is reachable from the Web API, so a screen that only produces safe markup is not evidence that only safe markup arrives. **Every letter, not only the ones the catalogue marks HTML**: `NotificationDrain.Compose` puts the body straight into `email.description`, which renders as markup whatever the letter is called, so a tag in a "plain text" letter is live all the same - and escaping a literal `&` or `<` there makes it DISPLAY correctly rather than breaking it. **Cleaned before anything is judged**, so the refusals rule on what will actually be stored. **A body that cleans away to nothing gets its own refusal** rather than "it needs a body", which to somebody who has just written one reads as a bug in the screen. **Only when the write carries a body** - an update changing the subject alone must not rewrite a body it never mentioned, and a test pins that the Target is left without the attribute. **`{{caseButton}}` is untouched**: it is plain text in the row and becomes markup only at render, from a value the assembly builds. **Proved by disabling it**: 7 of the 11 tests fail with the call removed, and the 4 that still pass are the guards that should. The editor now says the cleaning happens rather than rewriting what somebody saved in silence. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-170 | **Every letter is edited in the rich-text editor, and a STORED body is escaped as markup at render whatever the catalogue calls the letter.** The `IsHtml` flag now governs only the compiled fallback. | Nine of the twelve letters are marked plain text, so nine of twelve opened with a bare textarea and "make the body rich text" looked undone. **The flag was never a real distinction for the reader**: `NotificationDrain.Compose` puts the body in `email.description`, which renders as markup whatever the letter is called - which is what the HTML letters and `{{caseButton}}` already rely on. So a stored body is markup, and its token values must be escaped or a client called "Smith &amp; Co" lands in the markup raw. **The compiled copy keeps its own flag** because it is code-authored, known safe, and compared byte for byte in `NotificationBodiesTests` - an environment holding no rows still sends exactly what it sent before, and flipping the flag outright would have changed that. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-171 | **`{{completedCheck}}` attaches the completed check to whichever letter carries it, and renders as nothing.** Allowed in every letter through `NotificationTemplates.AlwaysAllowed` rather than being named in any letter's own list; `Render` reports it on the `Letter`, and `NotificationOutbox` attaches after the row is created. | "Include PDF", asked for as one of the tokens. **A token rather than a checkbox** because the picker is already where an administrator looks for what a letter can carry, and a checkbox elsewhere on the form would be a second place to learn. **It prints nothing deliberately**: what the letter SAYS about the attachment is the administrator's to write, and a marker that inserted its own sentence would be wording they could not edit. **Stripped explicitly rather than left to `Substitute`**, which only replaces tokens it was given a value for - and this one never has a value, because it is an instruction about the letter rather than a thing the letter says. **Not in any letter's own token list**: it says what the letter carries rather than what it says, so naming it twelve times would invite the twelve to disagree; `tokensFor` adds it for the picker and the drift tests were taught the concept rather than loosened. **Additive only** - the para-planner's letter still attaches by its own call, so nothing that worked before depends on anybody editing anything, and a test pins that a letter without the marker is queued with nothing attached. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-172 | **A checklist stamp is read day-first even when it carries a time**, and a numeric date with a time attached is refused by `ParseDate` rather than guessed. `ParseDateTime` tries the UK forms explicitly after the ISO ones. | Found by building a DEV fixture from the supplied extract (`Pre-Advice Check Required Task 14-09.xlsx`). `ParseDateTime`'s comment assumed the Code App resolves the workbook's serials to ISO before posting - **true only for a cell the workbook stores as a date-formatted serial**. The supplied extract stores its `CompletionDate` cells as **text**, so nothing converts them and they arrive as Intelligent Office wrote them: `18/08/2026 13:54`. That fell through to `DateTime.TryParse` under **invariant culture, which is month-first**. **Two failures, and the quiet one is worse**: above the twelfth there is no such month, so the stamp came back null and the case read as a checklist nobody stamped; at or below it the value parsed **silently wrong** - `06/08/2026 14:49` became 8 June. **The existing guard did not catch it**: `IsNumericDateForm` was asked about the whole value, and the space and colon made it answer no, so the very ambiguity it was written to refuse walked past it whenever a time was attached. It now judges the date part, and requires a separator so `31 Jan 2026 10:00` - which says outright which part is the month - is not caught by it. **Hardened in `ParseDate` as well as `ParseDateTime`**, because `ParseDate` is public and feeds every mapped date column: the same misreading was available to any extract that ever wrote a time into `StartDate`. **5 of the 9 new tests fail without the change.** | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-173 | **The case table on both surfaces shows TWO checker columns, Tax and AQS, and an absent one says which of two absences it is.** The Code App reuses `checkerState`; the portal list spells the same two sentences out in Liquid, and a drift test reads the template and fails when they disagree. | Asked for as "include checker on the case table". **Two columns rather than one**, because a Tax-then-AQS case has two checkers and the single column these replace named whichever discipline was allocated second - the exact defect Fixes 2 removed from the case header, which one merged column would have put straight back. **Decided from the ROUTE on both lists**, not from the review instances: a list holds one flat row per case and has none to count. Case *detail* counts reviews instead, because it has them - a deliberate difference, not an inconsistency. **An unknown route is treated as owing both**, so an unallocated check reads as work outstanding rather than as work nobody owes; showing a check that may not be needed is the safe direction. **The portal cannot import `CHECKER_LABELS`**, so the wording is duplicated - and the drift test exists because two surfaces quietly disagreeing about the same case is the failure nobody spots until a checker and a manager are reading different screens. Proved by breaking the template: 2 of the 15 tests fail. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-174 | **`setcaseadviser` sets `al_advisername` alone**, and does not require the name to resolve to a contact. | `setcasepeople` could not be used: it writes the adviser, the para-planner AND the checker name from one value and refuses a name that is not an active contact. **An adviser name is not a contact** - it arrives on the extract as free text, it is what the adviser to T&C Manager mapping is looked up by, and AD-029 requires an imported name nobody registered to survive; demanding a contact would refuse exactly the values the extract carries. **`al_checkername` is deliberately left alone**: it is not an assignment (AD-113), the import stopped stamping it for that reason, and a verb that put a name back would undo it. Dry-run by default, and it reports which rows already held the value rather than counting them as writes. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-175 | **A person field resolves to a contact by EMAIL first and by name only where there is no email.** `NotificationOutbox.MatchPerson` is the one matcher; `MatchParaplanner` and the new `MatchAdviser` are its two entry points, and a match now carries the `Contact` it resolved to. | Project owner, 2026-09-20: "use adviser email to lookup a contact in the table as link, assign them as advisers; for the paraplanner use the names to lookup if there's no email". **An address identifies somebody, a display name describes them** - two people share a name far more often than they share a mailbox, so where a field carries both the email decides. The adviser carries both; the para-planner carries only a name, because the extract gives that field no address, which is why its fallback is the only path it has rather than a nicety. **Two rows are still fetched, never one**, for an email as much as a name: uniqueness is a convention here, not a constraint, and picking the first of two would look certain. **The stored adviser address is still written to when no contact holds it** - requiring a match would mean an environment whose advisers are not in the directory silently stopped receiving adviser letters, and a lost letter is worse than one sent to an address the directory does not happen to carry; the same judgement AD-168 made about an override that reaches nobody. What the match buys is the person, plus **a diagnostic at import**: an unmatched adviser is now named in the batch report on the day, exactly as an unmatched para-planner has been since AD-161. **Proved by disabling it**: forcing the matcher back to name-only fails 3 of the 13 new tests. | 2026-09-20 |
| AD-176 | **Emptiness in this site's Liquid is decided by capturing the value and comparing it to the empty string, never by `!= blank`.** | Verified against DEV on 2026-09-20: `{% if <null attribute> != blank %}` takes the TRUE branch and then renders the null as nothing, so both checker cells on the case list and case detail came out completely empty and neither empty-state sentence was ever reached. The drift test passed throughout, because the sentences were present in the template source - in branches nothing could reach. Tests now assert HOW emptiness is decided, not only what the unreached branch says. |
| AD-177 | **Case detail decides the two checker empty states from the ROUTE, with an existing review instance as further evidence, and an unknown route owes both.** | It used to count review instances alone, which reads "the AQS review has not been created yet" as "no AQS check is owed": a Tax then AQS case awaiting its AQS leg said "No check of this type" on the case header while the case list said "Not yet allocated" about the same case (DEV, 2026-09-20). Two surfaces disagreeing about one case is the failure AD-173's drift test exists to stop, and the rule is now the same on both. |
| AD-178 | **The checker name stamped on a case comes from the CONTACT, whichever path allocated it.** `AssignCasePlugin.ResolveAssignee` carries `ContactName` alongside `UserName`, with the same `contact.fullname ?? user.fullname` fallback `ClaimCasePlugin` already used. | A Dataverse user and the contact that is the same person need not carry the same fullname, and in DEV they did not: an allocation stamped "svc automate aq" while a portal claim stamped "Service Account", and both appeared in the checker columns of one case list beside an Adviser column reading "Service Account". `UserName` still carries the Dataverse name, because AD-144's provisioning refusal names the identity Dataverse itself would refuse. |
| AD-179 | **Where the portal's data cache can leave a page behind, the page says so and does not repair itself.** The review page remembers which answers it saved, per review, per tab, and raises a notice when an answer it saved comes back empty; it never re-applies the value. | AD-094: a plug-in write never invalidates the portal's cache, so answers saved through `al_answerrequest` can be missing from the form for up to fifteen minutes. Nothing is lost and a submit still succeeds, but an empty form after a save reads as lost work and invites re-entry. Re-applying the remembered value was rejected: an answer on screen that the server did not send is indistinguishable from one it did, and a reviewer could not tell which they were reading. |
| AD-180 | **A retrieve on an id the CALLER supplied answers `NOTFOUND:` with a sentence; a retrieve on an id read from a lookup keeps throwing.** `CommandHelpers.RetrieveOrNotFound` holds the guard. | F1, 2026-09-20: `al_SignOffRemediation` and `al_CompleteRemediation` took the TargetId straight to a retrieve, so a case id passed where an action id belonged answered `UNEXPECTED: ... Entity 'al_remediationaction' With Id = ... Does Not Exist` - an internal table name reaching the caller, which NFR-OBS-01 exists to prevent. The distinction matters: swallowing a missing row behind a lookup would hide a real referential fault behind a polite sentence. The message carries no id, because the caller already has the one they sent. The test fake now throws `FaultException<OrganizationServiceFault>` rather than `InvalidOperationException`, so a guard written against the wrong exception type cannot pass the tests and still leak in Dataverse. |
| AD-181 | **The check date is stamped by the SUBMIT and is editable by nobody.** `SubmitReviewPlugin.StampCheckDate` writes the day of the submit unconditionally, and `al_checkdate` is off both editable allowlists - `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.Editables` and `CaseHeaderRequestPlugin.CheckerEditable` - and off both front ends. | Project owner, 2026-09-21: "the check date has to be uneditable as it is automatically updated on submit". **This supersedes AD's 2026-09-19 rule on the same column**, which stamped it on the first answer saved and only when the column was empty, so that the import could supply one and a checker could correct it. Neither half survives: a column nothing may write is not a default, and "when someone starts checks" is not "on submit". **Latest submit wins**, chosen by the project owner on 2026-09-21 with first-wins put to them beside it: on a Tax-then-AQS case the column ends up holding the day the checking FINISHED rather than the day its first half did. **Written unconditionally**, which is what overwrites a date the import carried - deliberate, and the reason the column had to come off the editable lists in the same change: leaving a field on a form that the next submit silently overwrites is worse than removing it. Proved live in DEV: `al_UpdateCaseDetails` answers `VALIDATION: Field 'al_checkdate' cannot be edited.` and a control field on the same call still saves. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-182 | **"Recheck required?" and "Do the remedial actions change the advice?" are the T&C Manager's answers.** They travel on the sign-off request, `SignoffRequestPlugin.RecordFormAnswers` writes them across the check being signed off, and `RemediationResponseGuardPlugin.TcOnlyRefusal` refuses every other route to the two columns. | Project owner, 2026-09-21. **They were the adviser's, and one of them decided something the adviser does not own**: `SignoffProgressPlugin.RecheckWaived` reads `al_recheckrequired` to choose between closing the case on the approval and stopping it at Awaiting Recheck (AD-138), so the adviser was deciding whether their own remediation needed looking at again. **Enforced on the sign-off rather than by a UI role check**, for the reason AD-053 gives: a Power Pages write reaches Dataverse as the site's application user, so no guard on `al_remediationaction` can ask who the portal caller was. The sign-off is the one write on this site where the role has already been proved - the page PATCHes the signed-in user's OWN contact row, which the Self-scoped permission pins to them, and AD-099 checks the T&C Supervisor web role server-side. The guard's test is therefore "did this write come from inside a contact Update" (`CommandHelpers.IsWithinMessageOn`), which a browser cannot produce. **Refused on presence, not on change** - unlike the completion freeze beside it, where a restated value is a dropped-response retry - because allowing a restatement would let the column be probed for what the supervisor answered. **Zero means leave alone**, so a rejection does not erase what an earlier approval attempt recorded. Proved live in DEV: both columns refused on a direct PATCH made as a system administrator, with the adviser's own `al_clientcontactrequired` still writing on the same row. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-183 | **Trail Light columns B and D carry EMAILS, and column 21 is gone.** `al_exportrecord.al_paraplanneremail` is new; `GenerateExportPlugin` snapshots both addresses at generation time; `TRAIL_LIGHT_HEADERS` is back to twenty. | Project owner, 2026-09-21: "replace column B & D on the trail light exports with emails. rather than codes show emails". **Positions are unchanged**, so nothing downstream shifts - what changed is what columns B and D MEAN, which is a change a positional reader cannot detect for itself, and is why it is written here rather than only in the code. **Column 21 went with it**: Adviser Email was appended there on 2026-09-19 precisely because inserting it beside the adviser's name would have shifted eighteen columns, and with column B carrying it, 21 only repeated the value. Removing the LAST column is as position-safe as adding it was; the project owner chose that over the duplicate. **Column D is RESOLVED, not read**: the case has no para-planner email column at all - the import carries their name and nothing else (AD-160) - so the address comes from the Contact that name matches, through `NotificationOutbox.MatchParaplanner`, which refuses to choose between two contacts of one name. An unmatched name leaves the cell EMPTY rather than falling back to the name or the code: AD-039 reads by position, so column D is "Paraplanner Email" on every row or the file lies about the rows where it is something else, and a wrong address in a file that leaves this system is worse than a blank one. **The four fail-accountable code columns are deliberately unchanged** - only B and D were asked for, and those four name a person picked out of a fail rather than the case's own pair. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-184 | **A Tax check that PASSES and still flags a remedial action is deferred to the AQS submit like any other Tax remediation.** `SubmitReviewPlugin.DeferredTaxFail` reads Q-FQTAX-03 off the Tax review when `al_taxoutcome` asks for nothing. | Found 2026-09-21 while confirming the project owner's fourth request - "for tax -> Aqs, if a tax has remediation it should not appear on the Advisers queue and remediation page until the aqs check has been submitted" - which the 2026-09-20 deferral already satisfies. **One path through it did not.** The Tax submit defers `taxRequiresRemediation \|\| remedialFlagged`: the Q-TAX-02 verdict OR the Q-FQTAX-03 tick, two different questions. `DeferredTaxFail` rebuilt the deferral from `al_taxoutcome` alone, so a checker who passed the tax outcome and still asked for a remedial action had it **silently dropped** - the Tax submit raised nothing because AQS was still to come, the AQS submit found a Pass and carried nothing forward, and the case closed clean with the adviser never asked. The reason is worded "Tax check: remedial action required" rather than the outcome's label, because telling an adviser "Tax check: Pass" as the reason they have work to do says the opposite of what happened. One test reproduces it and passes after. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-185 | **An unsubmitted review page re-reads its own checklist through the Web API, and brings itself up to date in place.** A JSON island carries the option scales, each answer row carries its question id, and a script asks `/_api/al_questionversions` for the version in force as of the day the server rendered against. | Project owner, 2026-09-21: "the changes should show immediately after they are made", after a question retyped in the Code App was still drawing the old scale on the portal ten minutes later. **The cause is not ours and not fixable where it sits**: Power Pages renders from a server-side cache and learns about a Dataverse write through polled change tracking - AD-094 measured up to fifteen minutes, AD-117 concluded "a longer delay narrows the window and cannot close it", and change tracking was re-verified enabled on `al_question`, `al_questionversion` and `al_section` on 2026-09-21. There is no site setting for it; the only two cache settings on this site are the header and footer output caches. **The Web API is not the Liquid render cache**, which is the whole point of reading through it - the same move `OT Remediation` already makes for its stale case-status badge. **It runs only on an UNSUBMITTED review**: a submitted one is read as of its submission day (BR-013) and must keep rendering the versions it was answered against, so refreshing it would relabel answered history. **The row is repointed at the live version before anything else**, because `ResponseGuardPlugin` refuses a write against a superseded version - a form left pointing at the old one fails the checker's very next tick with a message the page has not explained. **A tick whose value the new scale has no option for is cleared and SAID SO**; coming back to an empty row with no explanation is how a reviewer concludes their work was lost. **A grid is re-headed only where every row agrees**, the same test the server makes. `Webapi/al_questionversion/enabled` grants nothing new: the `Question Version - read` table permission is read-only and the Web API cannot exceed a table permission. Progressive enhancement throughout - a failure, a refusal or a browser that never runs it leaves the rendered form exactly as it was, which is the state we already had. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-186 | **The para-planner is identified by their ADDRESS.** The extract's `ParaplannerEmail` maps to a new `al_outcomecase.al_paraplanneremail`; `MatchParaplanner` takes the email as well as the name; the letter, the import's reachability check and Trail Light column D all read it. | Project owner, 2026-09-21: "the paraplanner email field should now be used to map the paraplanner ... emails are more safe than names", with the supplied workbook (`Pre-Advice Check Required Task 14-09 (1).xlsx`) carrying `ParaplannerEmail` in column M beside `AssignedBy` in column L. **Nothing new was decided here** - AD-160's reasoning and the 2026-09-20 `AdviserEmail` direction already settled it, and `NotificationOutbox.MatchPerson` has read email first and name second since that day. The para-planner was simply **the one person on a case with no address at all**, so every route to them resolved a display name and gave up whenever two contacts answered to it: the letter went unsent, the import reported them unmatched, and column D exported blank. **The name is still mapped and still used** - it is what a person reads on the case, and the fallback for a row carrying no address, which is every case imported before today. **A matched address is resolved through the Contact; an unmatched one is still written to** (AD-168: a lost letter is worse than one sent to an address the directory does not happen to hold) - a judgement the para-planner could not make until there was an address to fall back on. **The name is NOT tried when the address misses**, which is deliberate and tested by name: falling through would reintroduce exactly the collision the address was mapped to remove. `app/src/features/imports/caseUpload.ts` carries the header too, because that is what transcribes the workbook to CSV and a header it omits never reaches the importer. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-187 | **The case-header dropdowns become reference ROWS with a management page, not choice metadata. `al_listoption` holds every list's options, keyed by `al_list`; `al_outcomecase.al_producttypeid` is the case's chosen one. Product / solution type is migrated; Sample source, Case type and Pre or post check are declared and deliberately not yet offered.** Adding, renaming, reordering and retiring an option is ordinary CRUD on `/admin/lists`, granted to the Outcome Testing Manager as well as the administrators. `ListOptionRules.Resolve` is the authority: it refuses an option that does not exist, belongs to another list, or is not in force on the day it is chosen. | Asked for by the project owner (2026-09-21: "for dropdown fields like Product / solution type, Sample source, Case type, and Pre or post check - How easy would it be to create a management page where users can add, edit, and remove options?"). A page over the choice columns themselves was rejected for three independent reasons: adding an option is `InsertOptionValueRequest`, needing **System Customizer**, which no end user should hold; it is an **authoring** act, and TEST and PROD run the managed solution, so an edit there is an unmanaged layer that never flows back to DEV; and both **pickers are hard-coded** - `CaseEditPanel` from the generated constant, `OT Review Detail` as five literal `<option>` tags - so a new option would render on a case that already held it (`choiceLabel` prefers the server's formatted name) but nobody could choose it until a rebuild. An earlier note in this session said the labels themselves were baked in; that was half wrong and is corrected here - display was never the problem, **selection** was. One table for all four lists rather than a table each: adding a **list** stays a developer change because it needs a lookup and code that reads it, while adding an **option** becomes data, which is the split the request actually asks for. **Removal is retirement**, by effective date, on the BR-013 / AD-015 window (`from <= day < to`) so "in force" means one thing across the solution; a retired option leaves every picker and every case holding it is untouched. **Deletion is refused by Dataverse, not by the page**: the lookup carries `CascadeType.Restrict`, not the `RemoveLink` the generic `addlookupcolumn` verb uses, which would have silently blanked the product type on every case holding a deleted option - the AD-161 failure mode. That is NFR-SEC-01 applied to integrity: the guarantee is in the platform, so it holds for a caller who never opens the page. Proved in DEV on 2026-09-21, both directions: deleting the option case 900000005 held was refused with `0x80040227` naming the relationship and the blocking case, and deleting an unused option succeeded. The **choice column is kept** and stays editable: a case imported before the lookup carries only the integer, `caseOptionLabel` resolves it through `al_legacyvalue` so a rename follows the old cases, and taking the column away before every case is backfilled would leave a product type nobody could correct. End-to-end in DEV: an option created seconds earlier with **no equivalent choice value** was accepted on case 900000005 and audited as `Product/solution type 'Accumulation Pension' -> 'Drawdown'`; an option from another list was refused; the same option once retired was refused by name. The fixture was restored. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-188 | **All four case-header lists are migrated.** `al_outcomecase` carries `al_producttypeid`, `al_samplesourceid`, `al_casetypeid` and `al_preorpostcheckid`, every one a Restrict-cascade lookup to `al_listoption`. `MIGRATED_LISTS` now holds all four, so the management page offers all four and the Code App and the portal draw all four from rows. | The pilot's shape held, so the remaining three were mechanical and there was no reason to leave them half-done: a list still on choice metadata is one an administrator has to raise a ticket for, which is the thing AD-187 was asked to remove. The **tooling was generalised rather than triplicated** - `createlistoptiontable` loops `ListOptionTable.Lists`, and `seedlistoptions` turns each choice column into rows by reading that column's OWN metadata, never a list typed out a second time, which is the failure the whole exercise exists to stop. Both verbs are idempotent: the re-run reported every existing part as already present and added only the three new lookups. Seeded in DEV 2026-09-21: **15 options across the four lists, 14 cases backfilled, no orphans**. Proved end to end on a second list: a new "Ad hoc escalation" sample source with **no equivalent choice value** was accepted on case 900000005 and audited as `Sample source 'Mandatory' -> 'Ad hoc escalation'`; sending that same option id into the Case type field was refused with "Case type is not an option on that list", which is the check that earns the single-table design. Fixture restored, scratch option deleted. The portal's other three selects were **nineteen more hand-written `<option>` tags**; a test now asserts the set of portal dropdowns equals `MIGRATED_LISTS`, so a list migrated in the app but left hardcoded in Liquid fails the build. Two tests **changed meaning rather than being deleted** - they pinned that three lists were not yet migrated - and the property they protected, that a list with nowhere to put a chosen option is not offered, is kept and asserted against a constructed list so migrating all four did not quietly retire the rule. `CaseEditPanel` makes **one** read for all four dropdowns rather than one per list. **Still unverified**: whether a signed-in checker's browser can read `al_listoption` on the portal. The permission matches every other permission in DEV and is bound to two web roles, and the review page already reads five other tables through the same Liquid `fetchxml`, so the expectation is that it renders - but it has not been seen, and is not claimed. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-189 | **A table created after the Power Pages site was last started is invisible to it until the site is restarted.** `al_listoption` was created in DEV on 2026-09-21 and the portal cannot read it: all four managed dropdowns render empty. The Liquid, the table permission and the option rows are all correct - the running site simply does not know the table. **The fix is a site restart / configuration sync, not a code change.** | Diagnosed by browser (Playwright) against DEV rather than inferred, after AD-188 recorded the portal half as unverified. Four things were ruled out one at a time. **Not the filter**: a temporary UNFILTERED fetch over `al_listoption` also returned nothing (the diagnostic was reverted and the template read back at its previous byte size). **Not the data**: the rows carry statecode 0, the right `al_list`, and null effective dates. **Not the case lookup**: `h_preorpostcheckid.name` rendered "Pre", so resolving the lookup THROUGH the case works; it is querying the table that fails. **Not the web-role bindings**: binding the permission to all nine roles changed nothing, and every other table permission in this environment has ZERO bindings while those tables read fine. The decisive evidence is the Web API: `/_api/al_questionversions` returns **200 with data** while `/_api/al_listoptions` returns **404** - not 403. A permission refusal is 403; 404 is the site not knowing the table exists. Both were enabled the same way, so the only difference is age. **Two consequences worth keeping.** First, the degraded state is now honest: the held value fell through the "not among the offered options" branch and rendered "Pre (retired)" when it was not retired, because that branch cannot tell "retired" from "the catalogue could not be read"; a second flag, set inside the loop, now suppresses the suffix unless a row was actually drawn, and the browser confirms it reads "Pre". Second, the **Code App path is unaffected** and was proved end to end - it reaches Dataverse directly and has no site cache between it and the table. The maker portal was NOT used to restart the site: it defaults to **Env_AQ_Test** and opens a site-creation flow, and driving it blind is one mis-click from acting on the wrong environment. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-190 | **Products is a managed list a case may hold SEVERAL of, attached through a many-to-many rather than a lookup.** `al_listoption` gains a fifth list value (120910844) seeded with four placeholders; `al_listoption_al_outcomecase_products` links them to a case. The Code App draws a checkbox group, the portal a tick list, and `ListOptionRules.ApplyProducts` replaces the whole set server-side. The free-text `al_products` column is kept and still read as a fallback. | Asked for by the project owner (2026-09-21: "turn the products page into dropdown with 4 placeholders", then "make it manageable as well"). **Multi-select was confirmed rather than assumed**, and the evidence is in the data: the column is labelled "Product(s)" and the seeded fixture is `"Pension; ISA"`, so a single lookup would have made the field record LESS than the free text it replaces, which is not an upgrade. **Manageable with no page changes at all** - a fifth list was a row of metadata, which is the return on holding one options table; `MIGRATED_LISTS` now counts a list as manageable if it has either a lookup or a relationship, and `SINGLE_CHOICE_LISTS` / `MULTI_CHOICE_LISTS` split the two for the places that care. **The payload is the whole set**, not a change to it: a page sending only additions could never remove one. Every id is validated before anything is written, so one bad id changes nothing rather than half-applying - a partly-applied set is a case whose products nobody chose. An option **already attached is left alone even once retired**: retiring stops an option being chosen from now on, it does not rewrite the cases carrying it, and without that a checker editing any other header field on an old case would be refused over a product they never touched. The set is **sorted** wherever it is built or read - the panel, the portal collector, and the stored value - because ticking A then B and B then A must give the same string or the change check fires on a set nobody changed. **Checkboxes, not `<select multiple>`**: a multi-select shows a few rows, needs ctrl-click for a second, and drops the rest of the selection on a mis-click. Proved against real Dataverse in DEV: two products attached and audited as `Products (none) -> 'Product 1 (placeholder); Product 3 (placeholder)'`; sending one id removed the other; a Product/solution type option sent as a product was refused. The **portal half is written and pushed but unverifiable** while AD-189 stands - the group renders with zero boxes because the table is unreadable. The collector was confirmed not to have broken header editing, by a positive check: `max` is set later in the same IIFE and is present. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-191 | **The checklist-freshness Web API call (AD-185) has been failing with 403 on the portal since it shipped.** The site setting `Webapi/al_questionversion/fields` allowlisted `al_questionid`, but the Web API exposes a lookup as `_al_questionid_value`, which is what the script both selects and filters on. `_al_questionid_value` was added to the allowlist on 2026-09-21. | Found by accident while browser-testing AD-190: the review page's only console error was this 403, and it predates today's products work. It had gone unnoticed because AD-185 is progressive enhancement and fails **quietly by design** - a stale form is what the page already had, so nothing on screen says the refresh did not happen. That is the right behaviour and it is also why the defect survived: a feature that degrades silently needs a test that watches the wire, not the page. A direct GET of the same table returned **200**, which is what ruled out the table being disabled and pointed at the field list. **The fix is NOT confirmed**: the 403 persisted after the setting changed, which is consistent with the stale configuration cache of AD-189 but equally consistent with the diagnosis being wrong. It was recorded as a candidate and has since been **confirmed**: `/_api/al_questionversions?$select=...,_al_questionid_value` returns 200 on the DEV portal (2026-09-21, alongside AD-195). | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-192 | **A new table is not readable until the app's security roles say so, and in DEV nobody notices.** `al_listoption` was created on 2026-09-21 and added to no role, so `grantsecurity` now grants it: read/append/appendTo to both app roles, and create/write/**delete** to the admin role. | Found in the browser while checking an unrelated header fix. Every DEV human is a System Administrator, so the table read fine for us; the project owner, who holds **Basic User + Outcome Testing App User**, got `0x80040220 prvReadal_ListOption` - a **403, logged and swallowed**. The consequence is the one that hid it: `useAllListOptions` treats an unreadable catalogue as an empty one, so the four case-header dropdowns and the Products tick list rendered with **nothing to choose**, and the header fell back to the free-text column the list replaced. Nothing said "refused"; the page simply looked like it had not been built yet ("the product page is still a free text"). **Third time for this root cause** - AD-142 and the 2026-09-14 note are the same blind spot, roles written from what the pages read by people who never hold them. **Append and AppendTo as well as read**, because products are a many-to-many and `Associate` needs both ends appendable. **Delete for the admin role specifically**, unlike every other admin grant: the Dropdown options page offers an outright delete, and `CascadeType.Restrict` is what actually refuses it when a case holds the option (AD-187) - without the privilege an administrator would be told they may not do a thing they may in fact do. Proved in DEV: 403 before, no error after, the Products tick list showing its four placeholders and Sample source offering Random/Mandatory. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-193 | **The read-only case header reads the managed lists, not the columns they replaced.** `Product(s)` takes the ticked products and falls back to `al_products`; `Pre or post check` takes its lookup and falls back to its choice column. Both the case page and the review page resolve the product names, through the same join. | The edit panel moved to the managed lists on 2026-09-21; the header did not follow it all the way. Products cannot be read off the case row at all - it is a many-to-many - so the header had nothing to show and rendered the free text, meaning a checker could tick two products, save them, reopen the case and be looking at the imported text. `Pre or post check` was simply missed when its three siblings moved, so a case pointed at a managed option showed the value it had been **migrated from**. Two tests fail before the change and pass after (`'ISA'` where `'Pension; ISA'` was due, `'Post'` where the chosen option was due). Both callers of `caseHeaderFields` were wired, not one: a shared header block that two pages fill differently is drift with extra steps. Precedence is the managed value first, then the replaced column, everywhere - cases imported before the lists existed keep reading correctly. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-194 | **A new admin page needs its `al_pagepermission` rules created with it, and a role assignment must be active to count.** `page.admin.lists` now carries the same three rules every comparable admin page has: Administrators, AL Portal - Portal Administrator and AL Portal - Outcome Testing Manager, all Manage. | Found straight after AD-192: with the privilege granted the dropdowns filled, but the **Dropdown options page still did not appear in the nav**. Two data reasons, neither a code defect. First, the reporter's `AL Portal - Outcome Testing Manager` and `Portal Administrator` assignments were **inactive** - only Adviser Remediation and T&C Supervisor were active, and neither grants any `page.admin.*`, so the whole Administration group was hidden. Reinstated through `al_SetRoleAssignmentActive` rather than a `statecode` write, so the rule check and the audit event both ran. Second, `al_pagepermission` held exactly **one** rule for `page.admin.lists` (`Administrators`) where `permissions.ts` declares three - the live table governs, so the two admin roles that own the page could not see it. Added through `al_SetPagePermission`. Deliberately **not** modelled on `page.admin.questions` or `page.admin.security`, which are Administrator-only by design: Dropdown options follows advisers and templates, because the checking team maintaining its own lists is the entire point of the migration. **No verb seeds these rows**, so there is nothing to harden - a new admin page needs its rules created alongside it, and that is what this entry exists to say. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-195 | **On the enhanced data model a table permission's web roles live inside the `powerpagecomponent.content` JSON, and the intersect table is a projection the runtime ignores.** AD-189 is closed: the restart has happened. | Signed in on the DEV portal to check and found `/_api/al_listoptions` returning **403 `EntityPermissionReadIsMissing`**, not the 404 AD-189 recorded - a 404 meant the site did not know the table existed, a 403 means it does, so the restart is behind us. Ruled out the Web API settings (`enabled` and `fields` both present and correct) and the permission record itself (present, active, Global, `read: true`). The difference from a permission that WORKS is that `Question Version - read` carries `adx_entitypermission_webrole` inside its `content` JSON and `List Option - read` does not. The upload wrote the nine roles the YAML declares into `mspp_entitypermission_webrole` instead - and those nine rows are the **only** rows in that intersect in the entire environment, while every table permission that works has zero, which is the proof that `content` is what the runtime reads. **The `mspp_*` tables are a legacy projection**, so a binding written there looks right in every query and changes nothing. Fix is a single PATCH to `content`, binding Administrators + Authenticated Users to match the Question Version precedent rather than the nine named roles: all nine are subsets of Authenticated Users, and binding by name means a tenth role added later gets empty dropdowns in silence - the exact failure AD-192 was. **Applied to DEV and verified in the browser**, with no site restart: `/_api/al_listoptions` 403 -> 200, and on an editable review page Case type, Product or solution type, Sample source and Pre or post check all offer their options while Products shows its four placeholders. The local YAML was changed to the same two roles, because left at nine the next `pac powerpages upload` would push the legacy shape back and undo it. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-196 | **The two T&C Supervisor controls on the remediation page are offered only to a T&C Supervisor.** The sign-off panel and the regrade panel both gate on `user.roles contains 'AL Portal - T&C Supervisor'`, defaulting to hidden, and say why rather than vanishing. | Both used to render for anyone, on the recorded reasoning that "hiding it by role in Liquid would look like access control and enforce nothing (NFR-SEC-01)". The first half is true and the conclusion does not follow (project owner, 2026-09-21: *"If i dont have the permission to do this, then I should not see these controls"*). The server is the boundary either way - the al_signoff create permission, `SignoffRequestPlugin.EnsureSupervisorRole` and `RegradeRequestPlugin` all still check - and hiding a control is not a security claim; offering one that cannot work spends somebody's time on a form they will be refused for filling in. **The idiom was already on the site and simply had not been applied here**: OT Review List gates "Run checks" on the AQS Reviewer role with exactly this pattern and exactly this justification. Both panels moved together deliberately, because they are the same person's two controls and leaving one visible would make the rule look arbitrary. Five of six new tests fail before the change. The panel **says why** instead of disappearing: a vanished panel reads as "nothing to do here", which is wrong - there is something to do and somebody else has to do it. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-197 | **A legitimate portal sign-off is being refused by the guard that exists to stop illegitimate ones, and the interaction was never tested.** Open. | Reported live (project owner, 2026-09-21): pressing "Sign off this remediation" returns `RemediationResponseGuardPlugin`'s refusal - *"'Recheck required?' and 'Do the remedial actions change the advice?' are the T&C Manager's answers..."* - on the one path that is supposed to be allowed. The reporter had passed `EnsureSupervisorRole`, which runs first and has its own distinct message, so this is not a permission problem. Ruled out against DEV rather than assumed: the deployed template is current (the adviser form no longer sends those columns; the button PATCHes `contact`), the deployed assembly is current (pushed 18:52 UTC+2, after the 16:08 commit that added the bypass), and both steps are Enabled and correctly registered (guard stage 20 sync on five filtering attributes; `SignoffRequestPlugin` stage 40 sync on `al_signoffrequest`). So `IsWithinMessageOn(context, "Update", "contact")` is not matching in the live pipeline. **Why it shipped: `FakeOrganizationService` does not run the guard.** `SignoffRequestPlugin.Apply` is tested with it absent and `TcOnlyRefusal` is tested as a pure function with the nesting flag passed by hand, so the only thing that joins them - the real ParentContext chain - has never been exercised. That is the same hole the 2026-09-21 note named when it said a successful sign-off writing the two columns was not proved live. **Next step is a non-destructive reproduction**: the same request with a rejection and empty notes writes the two answers first, then `SignoffGuardPlugin` refuses the empty notes and rolls the whole transaction back, so the refusal that comes back names the culprit and nothing is written. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-198 | **Signing a case off is the T&C Manager mapped to its adviser, not anyone holding the supervisor role.** `SignoffRequestPlugin.EnsureMappedToCase` compares the signatory against `al_advisermapping` after the role check. **This reverses the Phase 1 model** that `TcManagerRouting` documented in bold - every T&C Manager attests to every case, the mapping decides only who is TOLD. | Project owner, 2026-09-21: *"service accounts holds the T&C role but they are not linked to the adviser handling the case, so they should not be able to perform sign off"*. An attestation is a supervisory act by a named person over a named adviser's work (BR-008), and a signature from outside that relationship records something that never happened. **Reading stays open to every supervisor; attesting does not.** Order matters and is tested: the role first, then the mapping - somebody with neither is told they lack the role, because answering "you are not this adviser's manager" would send them after a mapping they could not use. **A mapped manager with no work email may still sign**: reachability is a routing concern, so the manager is compared whenever the mapping resolved one at all. **The refusal names the adviser only when there is no mapping**, where the gap is the reader's to fix (F58); when somebody else is the manager it says only that it is not their case, because anyone holding the role can reach that message and would otherwise enumerate who supervises whom. Six new tests; the two existing sign-off fixtures had to grow the whole chain, which is itself the evidence that the rule now binds. **Consequence to plan for: `al_advisermapping` is now operational data, not notification convenience.** DEV holds **2** mappings and TEST holds **0**, so a remediation cannot be completed against any other adviser until they are populated. **The portal panel is NOT yet gated on the mapping** - only on the role (AD-196) - because the site has no table permission for `al_advisermapping`, so a Liquid check would read empty and hide the panel from everybody. Granting one would expose the supervision structure to every portal role and is a decision in its own right. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-199 | **AD-197 closed: Power Pages wraps every write as `Upsert/none -> Update/<table>`, so no plug-in context test can tell the adviser's PATCH from the supervisor's sign-off.** The guard now asks whether a sign-off is in flight - some contact carries `al_signoffrequest` - and the two T&C-only columns have been removed from `Webapi/al_remediationaction/fields`, which is the actual boundary. | **Three attempts, two of them wrong, and the reason each failed is the finding.** (1) `IsWithinMessageOn(context, "Update", "contact")`: printing the parent chain in DEV gave `Update/al_remediationaction(d2) <- Upsert/none(d1)` - Power Pages issues an **Upsert**, whose `PrimaryEntityName` the platform reports as **"none"**, so the word `contact` never appears. (2) A shared-variable flag set by `SignoffRequestPlugin`: the chain showed `vars{IsAutoTransact,AcceptLang,ChangedEntityTypes}` and never the flag, because **a parent's SharedVariables are snapshotted before the plug-in that would set one runs**. (3) The chain also proves no context test *could* work: an adviser PATCHing the action directly produces the identical `Upsert -> Update` shape at the identical depth. **Isolated by running the same payload two ways**: through the SDK it reached the rejection-notes refusal (both columns written, then rolled back) and through the portal it hit the T&C refusal - same contact, same action, same body. **The real hole was configuration**: `al_recheckrequired` and `al_changesadvice` were still in the portal's Web API field list, so the adviser's page could have sent them regardless of any plug-in. Removing them is the first line; the in-flight check is the second. **Residual, stated not glossed**: a write could ride on somebody else's simultaneous sign-off - a narrow window, unreachable from the portal now that the fields are gone. **Why every test passed throughout**: they all passed the decision in as a bool, so the one thing that was wrong - how the bool is computed from a live pipeline - was the one thing never exercised. Proved fixed live through the portal. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-200 | **F57, F59 and the empty TEST role catalogue are closed, DEV taken as authoritative throughout.** Page permissions: 67 active in each, no key missing either way and no level mismatch. Questions: **56 -> 47 in DEV and 55 -> 47 in TEST**, matching `data/v8-seed`. `al_role`: 10 rows seeded into TEST, carrying DEV's own ids. | Project owner, 2026-09-21: *"F57 - sort out, ensure there's parity between the 2"*, *"F59: delete them as they were never use, delete from both environments"*, *"Populate the roles"*. **F59 was verified, not assumed**: zero `al_response` rows referenced any of the 19 versions in DEV or the 8 in TEST, which is what made deleting safe rather than tidy - a question an answered review points at would have had to be retired instead. Versions were deleted before their questions. **F57 moved TEST in three ways** and the third is the one to know about: three keys added, nine levels set to DEV's, and **nine grants TEST held that DEV does not were DEACTIVATED, not deleted** - `command.assign`, `command.regrade`, `command.signoff` and `remediation.complete` for Administrators, `page.cases` for Adviser Remediation, and `page.exports`/`page.remediation` for the two reviewer roles. Each is one call to restore, and the concern was raised twice before acting: **removing them takes access away from whoever was relying on it**, and DEV lacking the Administrators command grants may be DEV's gap rather than TEST's excess. DEV wins because DEV is the only authoring environment; if any of the nine turn out to be needed, the right fix is to grant them in DEV and re-run parity, not to re-add them in TEST. **`ROLE-TEST-ROLE` was left out of the seed** as scratch of the same family F59 removed. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-201 | **The sign-off panel is offered only to the T&C Manager mapped to the case's own adviser, and `al_advisermapping` is readable on the portal at CONTACT scope to answer it.** The project owner: "Supervisors should only see the sign off controls on cases advisers linked to the handled". AD-198 had already settled the server side, so the page was offering a form that could only be refused. The blocker was that no table permission existed for `al_advisermapping` at all, and a Global one would have put who-supervises-whom in front of every role bound to it. **Contact scope removes the privacy objection rather than accepting it**: `al_tcmanagerid` is a contact lookup, so the runtime narrows every read to the rows where the reader IS the manager - a supervisor sees the advisers mapped to them and learns nothing about anyone else's. Bound to **one** web role, read-only, and deliberately given **no `Webapi/al_advisermapping/*` settings** so the table stays off the browser-reachable API; the gate is server-rendered Liquid. Roles written inside `powerpagecomponent.content`, per AD-195. **The Liquid filter names both the adviser email and `user.id`** although contact scope already narrows the rows: the scope is the privacy control, the filter is the answer, and a gate that delegated the matching to the scope would be correct only while an assumption about the runtime held - the assumption AD-195 caught being wrong about this very model. **Two gates, not one**: `RegradeRequestPlugin` carries `EnsureSupervisorRole` and no mapping check, so `can_regrade` stays role-only while `can_signoff` needs both. Folding regrade into `can_signoff` would have hidden a control the server accepts, which is this defect pointing the other way. **Extending the mapping gate to the regrade command is left open** - the argument applies to it unchanged, but it restricts a command the owner did not name. Consequence to keep in view: four of DEV's six cases in remediation have no mapped manager, so the panel now disappears from them - the server has refused those since AD-198, and what changed is only that the refusal is visible before the form is filled in. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-202 | **Recording the regraded outcome is the T&C Manager mapped to the case's adviser, the same rule the sign-off carries, and the two now share one implementation.** AD-198 put the mapping gate on `SignoffRequestPlugin` and left `RegradeRequestPlugin` on `EnsureSupervisorRole` alone, so anyone holding the supervisor role could set the final outcome on **any** case - their own cases included, and including the very cases whose sign-off the mapping had just refused them. **Found on the portal, not reasoned about**: while verifying AD-201, the Regraded outcome form rendered on two cases whose mapped manager was somebody else while the sign-off form on the same pages was correctly withheld, and the reader was those cases' own adviser. The page was right to show it - it was mirroring the command - so the **command** was fixed. The rule moved into `SupervisorMapping.EnsureManagesCase`, called by both, because it is one rule and the copy that did not exist is exactly what leaked; the message takes the act as a parameter ("Signing a case off" / "Recording the final outcome") so a reader looking at one of two controls knows which is refused.
