The page collapsed back to one gate, `has_signoff_role` surviving **only** to choose the wording between "you do not hold the role" and "you hold it but not over this adviser"; a test pins that it never appears in a panel condition again. **Scope**: only the two portal paths. `RegradeCasePlugin.Regrade` and the `al_RegradeCase` Custom API behind the Code App are untouched, because a back-office user is authorised by their Dataverse role - a different boundary, and not the one leaking. **Cost accepted**: a case whose adviser has no mapping can now be regraded by nobody, the same consequence the sign-off already carried. Verified live both ways on DEV. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-203 | **Every portal component created through the Web API today was outside the solution, and a promotion would have carried the page without its permissions.** Six: both table permissions (List Option, Adviser Mapping) and four Web API site settings. 267 on the site, 261 in the solution. A Web API create takes no solution context and nothing warns. **The consequence was specific, not theoretical**: the OT Remediation template WAS in the solution, so TEST would have received the page that reads `al_advisermapping` and none of the permission that lets it - the sign-off panel withheld from everyone including the right person, every managed dropdown empty (AD-192 again), the freshness call 403 (AD-191 again) - and all three render as EMPTY PAGES rather than errors, which is the class of failure that took a day to find in DEV. Fixed with six `AddSolutionComponent` calls, `AddRequiredComponents: false` so a site setting does not drag the site graph in behind it; verified 267 of 267. **Rule**: anything created by raw POST needs an explicit `AddSolutionComponent`, and the membership diff is worth running before a promotion rather than after. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-204 | **The portal has end-to-end specs, because the vitest suite reads template SOURCE and three of today's defects were invisible to it.** AD-195 (roles written to a projection the runtime ignores), AD-201 (no table permission at all) and AD-202 (the regrade command checking the role alone) each left source that was correct and a page that looked EMPTY rather than broken. `app/e2e`, Playwright, asserting BOTH directions of the gate - the form appears for the mapped manager AND both controls are withheld for everyone else - plus no Liquid error, options actually present in the managed lists, and the AD-199 allowlist still refusing. No environment detail is committed: URL, case ids and review URL are environment variables, and an unconfigured checkout skips, naming the variable. **Two findings from running it**, neither visible by reading: a skip inside the test body fires after the `page` fixture is built, so an unconfigured machine still launches a browser and blames the product for a laptop - it belongs at group level; and the first session guard asserted the body did not contain the sign-in text, which let a signed-out run PASS the two specs asserting `toHaveCount(0)` - no session, no controls, no controls expected, green. **A suite that reports a gate as working because nobody could see the page is worse than no suite.** The guard is positive now (portal host plus visible navigation). **General rule: an assertion phrased as an absence must be anchored to something whose presence was proved first.** | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-205 | **TEST upgraded to 1.0.6.0 and the day's work promoted, after a full component audit found nothing beyond AD-203's six.** The audit covered every type, not just portal components: 267/267 Power Pages, 31/31 Custom APIs, 24/24 SDK steps, 2/2 roles, 1/1 assembly. **The apparent 28-vs-27 table gap was a false positive and is recorded so the next person does not chase it**: two of the 28 `al_*` tables are N:N intersects, which solution packager carries as a *relationship* (`ManyToMany`, `IntersectEntityName`) rather than an entity component - both confirmed present. Imported with **`--activate-plugins`**, then verified rather than trusted: 55 of 55 steps active. That verification needed three attempts - `$expand=plugintypeid($select=assemblyname)` returns nothing usable and an unfiltered `plugintypes` page misses most of them, and BOTH wrong queries reported 0 steps, which reads exactly like the 2026-09-02 disaster; fetch types by `_pluginassemblyid_value` then steps by those ids in chunks. **A solution carries the table, never the rows**: `al_listoption` arrived empty, which would have reproduced AD-192 in TEST precisely, so 19 options were seeded carrying DEV's own ids (the lookups and the products N:N point at them by id, so fresh guids would make every saved choice unresolvable across environments). TEST now matches DEV on list options, roles and questions. **Still open**: `al_advisermapping` holds one row, so with AD-198 and AD-202 in force TEST can complete a remediation for one adviser and no other - F58 is now blocking rather than cosmetic. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-206 | **The Outcome Testing App User role can create, write and delete `al_listoption`, which `grantsecurity` does not grant - reported, not changed.** The tool gives that role read/append/appendTo and reserves create/write/delete for the admin role, so those three were granted outside it; and because `GrantTable` only ever adds, re-running `grantsecurity` will not take them back. Found while confirming the TEST promotion was faithful - it is, TEST and DEV are identical, which is what made the underlying state worth looking at. **Left alone deliberately**: the Dropdown options page is offered to the Outcome Testing Manager and Portal Administrator so the checking team maintains its own lists, and if those people hold App User rather than App Admin the page needs exactly these privileges. Tightening it might be correcting a mistake or might break the page for the team it was widened for, and that is not a call to make from the outside. Note the page permission closes the UI path but not the API path; `CascadeType.Restrict` still refuses the delete of any option a case holds. | 2026-09-21 |
| AD-207 | **The staff code is held against the PERSON, and Trail Light columns B and D carry codes again.** `contact.al_staffcode` is new; `NotificationOutbox.MatchPerson` returns it with the match; `GenerateExportPlugin` snapshots it at generation time; `TRAIL_LIGHT_HEADERS[1]` and `[3]` are back to "Adviser Code" and "Paraplanner Code". **Six columns, not two** - the four fail-accountability code columns (L, N, R, T) were blanked whenever a specific person was named, on the stated grounds that "a contact carries no adviser or paraplanner code", and now carry that person's own. **This supersedes AD-183's choice of email for columns B and D - not its removal of column 21**, which stands; Trail Light is still twenty columns. AD-183 shipped to TEST at 1.0.6.0 on 2026-09-21 (AD-205). | Project owner, 2026-09-22: Trailight "said it would be difficult to accommodate that change to email rather than code". **The emails were never the point** - they went in because the codes were EMPTY. `al_advisercode` and `al_paraplannercode` were filled only by hand, OD-050 expected Tax/AQS to type them, and nothing ever did. Reverting to codes is only viable because they now come from somewhere. **`ParaplannerEmail` stays mapped on the import** (AD-186): it is the join key that resolves the para-planner to exactly one contact, and without it the code would be blank on precisely the rows where two people share a name - the failure this change exists to remove. It also still addresses their letter, which AD-165 records is their only sight of the check. **The per-case code fields were retired from both editable allowlists** - `UpdateCaseDetailsPlugin.Editables` and `CaseHeaderRequestPlugin.CheckerEditable` - and from the portal templates, `OT-Case-Detail` and `OT-Review-Detail`; the database columns `al_advisercode`/`al_paraplannercode` are untouched and keep their historical values. **Nothing on the Trail Light path reads them** - that is the point, and `GenerateExportPlugin` reads the registry on both the named and the unnamed accountability branch (corrected 2026-09-22; the unnamed branch, which is the common one, was still falling back to the case column). Three readers do remain and are deliberately left alone: `caseWorklistMapping.ts` carries both onto `CaseSummary`, from where `caseExport.ts` writes them into the app's OWN case-list CSV - an internal download, not the external file; `peopleDirectory.ts` still folds them into `PersonLoad.code`, which since the same correction nothing displays or exports; and `caseDetailMapping.ts` still declares and fills them on `CaseEditValues`, inert now that `CaseEditPanel` no longer renders either field and neither allowlist would accept them. The app's case CSV is the one place a historical hand-typed code is still visible to a user, which is correct - it is history. **The People page moved to `/admin/people`**, gated by `page.admin.users`: its Role column writes `al_userrolemapping`, which `PermissionHelpers` reads as an authorisation source, and on `page.cases` - where it used to live - every case-worker could have granted roles. **Amends OD-050**: adviser code and paraplanner code are struck from the fields Tax/AQS were expected to complete by hand; the other nine are untouched and that OD stays Open. **Corrected on deployment, 2026-09-22:** the Role filter and the Grant control were built against `al_Role` - the six labels in `data/roles-seed` - and neither worked, because `al_userrolemapping` carries a Power Pages **web role name** and the two vocabularies share no member. The filter read "0 of 12 people" for every role and every Grant was refused with "The role code does not match an active role", `AssignUserRolePlugin` resolving `RoleCode` against the web role registry only. `al_role` is retired by OD-037 - verified 2026-09-08 that no `ROLE-*` code appears in `al_userrolemapping` or `al_pagepermission`, and dropping the last read of it is what allows the table to be deleted - so wiring a new feature to it was the error. Both now read `useRoles`, the live `mspp_webrole` list, as Security configuration already did; the web role's name IS the code, so there is no second vocabulary to keep in step. **Senior Checker is dropped**, having no web role and so never having been grantable; Paraplanner is `AL Portal - Planner`. The unit tests passed throughout because they compared the map to itself and to the seed file, never to a vocabulary the server accepts. | 2026-09-22 |
| AD-208 | **Both teams edit the case header throughout; the freeze added on 2026-09-22 is reversed.** `CaseHeaderRequestPlugin` no longer calls `EnsureTaxCheckNotSubmitted` on the ordinary-header path, and OT Review Detail no longer fetches for a submitted Tax review to force `header_locked`. Client name, adviser, products and dates stay editable by whichever team holds the open review, before the handover and after it. | Project owner, 2026-09-23: *"I need to reverse the change we made, for tax -> AQS checks. both teams need to be able to edit the headers"*, reversing their own direction of 2026-09-22 ("once tax have edited the case headers, aqs should not be able to edit the headers but see the edits"). **The Tax team's own two fields did NOT reverse** and keep the rule that predates the batch: `TaxCheckRequired` and `TaxTeamDisposition` re-derive the review route (BR-004, AD-036), so `EnsureTaxReviewOpen` goes on refusing them once the Tax check is submitted, and goes on requiring that the check be the caller's (audit finding 2, 2026-09-20). **Nothing changed about WHO may edit**: `EnsureAssignedToCase` still runs for every payload and still needs an open review of the caller's, so a Tax checker whose own check is submitted is refused the header by that rule rather than by this one. The refusal message was narrowed with the rule - it read "its header can no longer be changed", which is now false and would send an AQS checker away from an edit they are allowed to make. The portal's `tax_submitted` fetch was **deleted rather than left wired to an unread variable**, along with the notice that explained the restriction; a query whose answer no longer changes anything is how a reversed rule comes back. `HeaderStaysEditableAfterTaxTests` supersedes `HeaderFreezesAfterTaxTests`, pinning the reversal at the two public guards the ordinary path still has. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-209 | **The AQS all-Yes lock was challenged on the day it shipped and CONFIRMED (project owner, 2026-09-23: "all-Yes lock still stand"), with this consequence accepted: the File Quality fail points are one undivided block of twenty reasons across four categories - AML, Breach, Record Keeping, Tax check - so locking it on a clean AML and CRA section also withdraws the Breach and Record Keeping reasons. A file that passes AML and fails on record keeping has no reason to tick. That is the shape of the defect the 2026-09-09 direction reversed (reasons were filtered by category until a Tax reviewer who found a record-keeping failure had nowhere to record it); TEST review `8cb7fb72` is a live instance - Q-FQ-01 Fail with AML and CRA 5/5 Yes. Raised with the evidence, reaffirmed, implemented as directed. Three of the four gating rules shipped on 2026-09-22 were keyed on the wrong question and are corrected.** The Tax fail points lock on `Q-TAX-02` (the Tax CHECK outcome, S-TAX) rather than `Q-FQTAX-01` (the tax FILE QUALITY outcome, S-FQTAX); the AQS fail points lock when **every** AML and CRA point reads Yes rather than staying locked until a finding arrives; and "Remedial action required?" is now a lock rather than a default, with the answer the file quality outcome does not imply disabled. | Project owner, 2026-09-23, on each in turn: the Tax rule *"is done on the File quality Outcome instead of the File quality - Tax check section"*; the AQS rule *"if all the responses are yes, then lock it. Otherwise open it"*; and the remedial answer *"the user can still select yes manually, it needs to be disabled"*, generalised as *"any value that defaults to something needs to disable the other option"*. **The Tax correction is the conflation S-TAX already warns about**, two questions asked of the same file by the same team on different sections. **The AQS reversal makes the two disciplines agree on their resting state**: locked-until-a-finding meant a checker who had not reached AML and CRA yet met the block shut, which is exactly what the Tax rule refuses to do on an unanswered outcome. "All Yes" is counted against the **questions in force** and not the answers recorded - `ChecklistQueries` reads the section's question codes separately, because three Yeses and two blanks leave no row to find and would otherwise read as clean. That forced the answer query to drop its Fail/No/Insufficient filter: a Yes and a Pass are now both values the rules need, and a filter that cannot answer the question is not an optimisation. **The remedial lock is enforced three ways**, because disabling a radio is an affordance and the portal writes through a contact permission shared with the sign-off and the claim (NFR-SEC-01): `ResponseGuardPlugin` refuses the write, `ResponseProgressPlugin` **moves** an answer recorded before the outcome changed under it - moved, not cleared, since the outcome has already decided what it should be and the question is mandatory - and the page disables the option, in Liquid for the state it opens in and in JavaScript for what changes after. Everything that existed to tell a defaulted answer from a hand-made one (`remedialChosenByHand`, the `defaulting` re-entry flag) is gone; it answered "may this be overwritten?", which now has one answer. **Consequence accepted, stated rather than glossed: a passed file can no longer carry a remedial action.** AD-184 repaired a Tax check that passed and still flagged one; that path turns on `Q-TAX-02` and survives, but a **file quality** Pass beside a remedial Yes is now unreachable. The direction was given twice and confirmed when raised. **Found while writing the tests**: the `ChecklistGatingTests` fixture wired the grade and file quality questions to section ids it never seeded, so those rows could not be joined and the two tests asserting they are IGNORED had been passing for the wrong reason. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-210 | **"Products do not appear after submitting a check" was an undeployed fix, not a defect in the page.** `ListOptionRules.AppliedByAssociation` and its caller in `CaseHeaderRequestPlugin` - which keep `al_productids` out of the `ColumnSet`, because Products is a many-to-many that names no column - existed **only in the working tree**. They are in no commit, so they had never reached any environment. TEST ran an assembly without them until the push of 2026-09-23 08:01Z. | Without the guard, every portal header edit touching Products faulted on the READ - "'al_OutcomeCase' entity doesn't contain attribute with Name = 'al_productids'" - and because the submit button flushes the header first (`window.otHeader.flush`), the fault took the submit down with it. So the products were never written, which is why they "do not appear": there was nothing to appear. **Proved on TEST after the push**: ticking one product on case 900000004 through the portal wrote the association, confirmed against Dataverse (`al_listoption_al_outcomecase_products` returned "Product 1 (placeholder)"), and the association was removed again to leave the case as found. **A second, independent effect worth not confusing with it**: the portal's render cache is eventually consistent (AD-094, up to fifteen minutes), so immediately after that write the review page still served the checkbox UNTICKED while Dataverse held the association. A reader checking the page rather than the table would conclude the save had failed. The header cache-lag notice exists for exactly this and is why the verification here was done against the Web API, not the page. **The general finding is the one to keep**: this batch has 62 uncommitted files and TEST now runs code that exists in no commit, so "is the fix deployed?" could not be answered from git history at all - it had to be answered by reading the working tree against the environment. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-211 | **The AML and CRA count is scoped to the review's own checklist version.** `ChecklistQueries.AmlCraQuestionCodes` filters the section link on `al_section.al_checklistversionid`, matching the review's, and a review carrying no version yields an empty set rather than the whole environment's. | Found auditing AD-209's own implementation before it went further than TEST. The first cut counted every in-force S-AMLCRA question in the environment, which is not how anything else in the solution reads a section - `ResponseGuardPlugin` compares the review's `al_checklistversionid` against the section's before it will accept an answer at all. Keying the set on question CODE masked it while one version existed, because two versions of one logical question collapse to a single entry; a later version that ADDS a point (Q-AML-06) would not collapse, so a review on the older version would be owed an answer to a question it was never issued, `expected.IsSubsetOf(clean)` could never hold, and the AQS fail-points lock would silently stop working. **It fails OPEN**, which is the safe direction and precisely why nothing would have reported it: the block stays available, no checker is blocked, and the rule is simply dead. Two tests, one per direction - a point added on ANOTHER version is ignored, a point added on the review's OWN version is still owed - because scoping that read nothing would pass the first test and be just as wrong. The fixture had to start seeding `al_reviewinstance` at all: nothing read the review row until this, which is itself worth noting when adding facts to `ReadGatingFacts`. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-212 | **A lock is per OPTION, so the code that redraws a row has to re-apply it per option.** `rebuild()` in OT Review Detail read `querySelector('input[disabled]')` into one boolean and applied it to every option it then drew. | That was right while a lock was all or nothing - a read-only form disables the row entire - and it stopped being right on 2026-09-23, when the gating rules began disabling a SINGLE option and leaving the rest live. A checker whose checklist was reissued mid-answer had the row redrawn with EVERY option dead and could no longer answer the question at all. It now records which values were locked and re-applies them one by one, with `allLocked` keeping a wholly read-only row read-only including options the new scale ADDS. Not caught by the gating tests because the two run in different scripts: nothing re-runs the gating rules when a row is redrawn under them. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-213 | **The page does not write at load. `syncRemedialAction` takes `userDriven`, and only a checker's own change may save.** | Opening a review whose file quality outcome was a Pass and whose "Remedial action required?" was simply UNANSWERED ticked No and dispatched `change`, which the autosave turned into a PATCH. **Reading a page answered a mandatory question and recorded it against whoever opened it** - including someone who only came to look. The load path now locks the forbidden option, says so where it had to untick a stored answer, and returns before it can write. The repair was never the page's job: `ResponseProgressPlugin.ReconcileRemedialAction` moves a contradicting answer when the outcome is saved and `ResponseGuardPlugin` refuses a new one, which is NFR-SEC-01 read the right way round - the server is the boundary, the page is the affordance, and an affordance does not write on its own. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-214 | **`ReadGatingFacts` asks the AML and CRA questions only where that section has been answered.** | The version read and the question count ran on every review, inside the transaction holding a checker's save open - so every Tax review, which has no AML and CRA section and never will, paid two round trips per save to be told a section it does not have is not complete. The short-circuit returns the SAME answer: with nothing answered on the section, a non-empty expected set cannot be a subset of an empty clean set, and an empty expected set fails the count. Pinned on the round-trip COUNT rather than on the result, because the result was already right; what was wrong was the cost of reaching it. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-215 | **DEV and TEST are different portal hosts, and `e2e/.auth/portal.json` holds one session at a time.** DEV is `outcometesting.powerappsportals.com`; TEST is `outcometestingtest.powerappsportals.com`. | The TEST site record carries DEV's primary domain, copied by an import (PRT-114), so the site row cannot be used to tell them apart - the two are distinguishable by the Entra client id each redirects with. Running both suites in one sitting means capturing each session in turn, not once. **`expectSignedIn` did not catch the mismatch**: its host check passes, because a Power Pages site with a local sign-in page answers an unauthenticated request with `/SignIn` on its OWN host rather than redirecting away, and the sign-in page still renders the Main Navigation landmark. Every spec then failed on whatever it asserted first - "the case list page has an .ot-page__inner" - which reads as a broken page rather than a signed-out one. The guard now tests the path as well as the host. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-216 | **Products is the real catalogue of 55, and a tick list that long folds away.** The four placeholders are DEACTIVATED, not deleted. | Project owner, 2026-09-23. Deleting the placeholders would have taken three TEST cases' history with them; the review page already renders a held-but-no-longer-offered option ticked and marked "(retired)", so retiring them is the path the design already had. **Still checkboxes**: the argument against `<select multiple>` did not stop being true when the list got longer, it got MORE true, because a ctrl-click that drops the selection costs more the more there is to re-pick. What changes is that the list is shut behind a line NAMING what is ticked, with a search box and a capped panel - measured on DEV at 45px shut and 329px open, against 55 rows unfolded. The portal renders the panel OPEN with the controls hidden and lets script shut it, so a page whose script never runs is the long list it always was rather than a field that has vanished. **A ticked option is never filtered away** by the search, on either surface: searching is for finding the next product, not for deciding what is selected. The fetch says `count="200"`, because a catalogue cut at a default page size looks exactly like a catalogue that is complete. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-217 | **A Trail Light download is called `DFALIN1_outcometesting_yyyy_mm_dd`.** | Project owner, 2026-09-23. The name is part of the interface the same way AD-039's twenty columns are: `DFALIN1` is the receiving end's name for this feed, and a file arriving under another name is a file nobody picks up. Used EXACTLY as given - no batch code, no "filtered", and underscores in the date where `stampedFilename` writes hyphens, because a convention is not improved by being made consistent with something it is not part of. Two downloads on one day therefore share a name, which is deliberate: the convention describes the day's file, not the click that made it. The day is the **UK** day via `ukToday` - 23:30Z on 1 June is already 2 June in London under BST, so a machine reading UTC would name a daily feed for yesterday. `ExportMenu.filenameFor` carries it, leaving every other export on stem-plus-date. **Open question raised, not resolved**: the "Download filtered rows" control on the same page is a deliberate SUBSET and now carries the feed's name too, so a partial file is indistinguishable from the day's feed by its name alone. | 2026-09-23 |
| AD-218 | **Each person sees their own work: role-scoped access replaces OD-022's Global read.** Six access columns on `al_outcomecase` (`al_taxcheckercontactid`, `al_aqscheckercontactid`, `al_aqsqueueaccountid`, `al_aqsqueuedon`, `al_advisercontactid`, `al_tcsupervisorcontactid`) are kept correct by `CaseAccessReconciler`, run as SYSTEM from synchronous post-operation steps on case, review and remedial-action writes. Portal case reads go through those columns (Contact scope; Account scope for the AQS queue); reviews, answers, outcomes, remedial actions and sign-offs are children of the case. In the Code App, cases are shared with the "Outcome Testing - Tax Team" and "Outcome Testing - AQS Team" owner teams with Share and Reparent cascade, and two new web roles, `AL Portal - Tax Team Manager` and `AL Portal - AQS Team Manager`, allocate their own discipline only. New portal permission ids use the band `c0`-`df` (amends AD-059). | Project owner, 2026-09-23, supplying `docs/reference/2026-09-23-access-requirements.md` (AR-01 to AR-04) and answering eight questions recorded in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-role-scoped-access-design.md`. **Supersedes OD-022 and AD-056** (every signed-in user reading every case) and AD-083's organisation-wide Home counts; **amends AD-135** ("Depth is Global throughout") for the manager role; **closes OD-029(c)**. A case is released to its adviser only once it has a remedial action, is at Awaiting Remediation or later, and owes no unsubmitted review; Pass cases are never released. Planners keep their emails and lose all system access. Roles and permissions are remapped by the project owner, not by a command (AD-144 forbids an application command conferring platform privileges). **Residual, accepted until that remap:** anyone holding `Outcome Testing App User` still reads every case in the Code App, and checkers must hold it to be allocated work (AD-144). | 2026-09-23 |
